- **T4, the Deployed version check, the Upstream download refusal and the summary's `malicious_change`.**
  - `Workflows.deployed_checks(db, application, observed, commits)` is the I/O before the fence. It takes an observation's data (or None) and Own-repository Vulnerability fix commits, and gives `{commit_id: {state, version, reason}}`. It reads `unknown` with its reason, without cloning, when no version check has run, when the recipe's repository (`domain.source_repository`) is not the Own one, or when the observation is not `detected`. Otherwise it opens the Own repository's mirror and takes `exposure.locked_tag(run, version, None)`; with no tag it reads `unknown`. With a tag it reads `serving_fix` when `exposure.contains` finds the fix (or a patch-id backport, `backports`) in it, else `before_fix`. A `DomainError` from the mirror reads `unknown` with the message.
  - `domain.apply_deployed_check(db, finding, check, observation_id)` writes `finding.data.deployed_version_check` (`{state, version, reason, observation_id, checked_at}`). On `serving_fix` it calls `transition_finding(..., "remediated", {by: deployed_version, observation_id, version})`. It does nothing unless `finding_cell` is (`own`, Vulnerability fix): this caller holds the restriction, and `transition_finding` is unchanged. `domain.deployed_findings` lists the Application's open or confirmed Findings in that cell.
  - Call sites: `version_check` checks them all before its fence and applies the checks after it, with the new Observation's id. `commit_analysis` computes, for a Vulnerability fix and before its fence, the check of each Application whose in-use Own repository it is, at that Application's latest observation (`domain.last_observation`); `run_playbook` applies it to the Finding it opens or keeps open (`prepared["deployed"]`).
  - `finding_fields.deployed_version` is the check's state (`unknown` by default), so the summary carries it too. `finding_json` gains `deployed_version_check` and `exportable`, and the Playbook's `deployed_version_check` step reads the state.
  - A15: `domain.exportable(db, resource_type, resource_id)` is false for an Upstream Finding (`finding_role == "upstream"`, a cve Finding on an Upstream source repository included), for its Labs' artifacts, for an Analysis of a commit whose repository any Application links as Upstream, and for a CVE analysis whose Application's source repository is Upstream. `GET /findings/{id}/download`, `GET /analyses/{id}/download` and `GET /artifacts/{id}/content` (for any file not ending in `.md`) then answer 403 `upstream_proof_not_exported`. `analysis_json` (with artifacts) carries `exportable`; the agent's `analysis.json` does not.
  - 7(d): `finding_summaries` carries `malicious_change`.
  - UI: the Finding page hides "Download bundle" / "Download draft bundle" when `exportable` is false. `ArtifactBrowser` and `ArtifactPreview` take `exportable`: without it they show no "Download file" link and no preview of a non-`.md` file, only a notice. `AnalysisResult` hides the analysis bundle and PoC downloads and the ZIP hint; "Download report" stays. The Findings list shows a "Static Proof" badge instead of the Lab badge on a Malicious change row. The Deployed version row and the Playbook step read "Still serving code from before the fix", "Serving the fix" or "Unknown", and the row adds the check's reason.
  - Tests:
    - `test_playbooks.py::test_the_deployed_version_check_reports_before_the_fix_serving_the_fix_or_unknown`, parametrized over six cases. A local Own repository acme/shop has v1.0.0, two fixes and v1.1.0, and `version_check` runs with FixtureBroker probe results. Detected 1.0.0 gives `before_fix`; detected v1.1.0 gives `serving_fix` and Remediated with cause `{by: deployed_version, observation_id, version}`. Ambiguous, unknown, a version with no tag (2.0.0), and a recipe on the Upstream repository (its link made the earliest) each give `unknown` with the reason and leave the Finding Open. Each case first shows "No version check has run" from the Analysis.
    - `test_playbooks.py::test_serving_the_fix_remediates_only_an_own_vulnerability_fix_finding`: with detected 1.1.0, the Own Vulnerability fix Finding is remediated. An Own Vulnerability introduced Finding and an exposed Upstream Vulnerability fix Finding (numen/lib) of the same Application keep their state and cause, with no `deployed_version_check`. A second fix analysed after that observation opens and is remediated at once by it.
    - `test_artifacts.py::test_downloading_an_upstream_proof_is_refused`: an Upstream commit Finding's zip, its `poc.py` and Dockerfile, the Analysis's `poc.py`, an approved Lab's output, the Upstream Analysis zip, and a cve Finding's zip and CVE analysis zip on an Upstream source repository all give 403 `upstream_proof_not_exported`. Both `report.md` files stay readable. An Own Finding's zip, its Analysis zip and its `poc.py` still download.
    - `test_findings.py::test_the_summary_carries_malicious_change`.
    - Changed tests: `queue_cve_analysis` and `prepared` take source overrides. The cve download tests in `test_artifacts.py` and two Analysis download tests in `test_commit_review.py` now use an Own repository (the re-analysis there also leaves `patch_drafting` queued). `test_analysis.py` (the Upstream `indicators.json`) and `test_workflows.py::test_historical_pinned_commit_job_remains_valid_after_current_head_advances` (an Upstream Analysis's `manifest.json`) read the file from storage and assert that its download is refused. `test_findings.py`'s route list needed no change: its Finding has no source commit, so it has no role.
    - UI: `Findings.integration.test.tsx` covers the three Deployed version labels with reasons, a Malicious change row with "Static Proof" and no Lab badge, and an Upstream Finding with no "Download" text and no `/download` or `/content` link, whose `poc.py` is never fetched. `ArtifactPreview.test.tsx`: `exportable={false}` shows the report, no "Download file" link, and the notice for `poc.py`, which is never fetched. `CommitReview.integration.test.tsx`: an Upstream Analysis offers no bundle, PoC or file download, but keeps "Download report".
    - The backend tests were written first and failed (`KeyError: 'deployed_version_check'`). The UI tests were written after the code; with the four UI changes reverted, all four new Findings and ArtifactPreview tests fail.
  - Deviations:
    - `deployed_checks` takes commits rather than Findings, because the Analysis computes the check before its fence, when the Finding does not exist yet.
    - The check also reads `unknown` when no version check has run. A manual observation (origin `manual`, status `detected`) counts as the latest observation for an Analysis (`last_observation`), as the brief says.
    - Prettier reformatted lines that earlier tasks left in `types.ts`, `Findings.tsx` and both integration tests.
  - Runs after T4 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to github.com, a registry or any other outside host:
    - Focused run (`test_playbooks`, `test_artifacts`, `test_findings`, `test_commit_review`, `test_analysis`, `test_version_fingerprints`, `test_accounts`): 189 passed.
    - `make check` passed.
    - `make test-integration` against PostgreSQL: 496 passed. A first run had one failure, `test_workflows.py::test_historical_pinned_commit_job_remains_valid_after_current_head_advances`, which read an Upstream Analysis's `manifest.json` that is now refused. That test was then changed as described above, and the second run passed in full.
    - `make test-unit`: 244 backend tests passed; runner 138 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests).
    - `make test-ui`: 144 passed in 19 files.
- **T5, Upstream Proof Labs, one bundle per role, and the Lab refusals.**
  - A07's vulnerable app for an Upstream Proof: `lab_run` composes it under `vulnerable-app/` beneath the bundle, whose files win. When the Finding's Upstream link stands for npm packages at a Locked version (`domain.locked_packages`), it is the Own repository's snapshot at its `cursor`, or with no Own repository a minimal private `package.json` with those packages at the Locked version; `manifest.json` and `.dockerignore` are left out (`domain.LAB_DROPPED`). Each package's tarball comes from `Sources.npm_tarball(dist)`: only a URL under `NUMEN_NPM_REGISTRY_URL`, at most `MAX_FILE` (`Sources.get` takes a `limit`), and only bytes matching the sha512 of `dist.integrity`, else 422 `package_untrusted` before any broker call. It is stored at `vulnerable-app/.asset-monitor/{name}-{version}.tgz` and pinned with `remediation.dependency_files(..., "pin", dist, spec="file:.asset-monitor/...")`; `spec` replaces the version in the manifest and lock root, and the lock entry keeps `version`. A non-npm link is refused with `lab_stack_unsupported`. Without a package or a Locked version the bundle is sent alone and `LabRun.data.bundle_only` says why. `LabRun.data` records `packages`, `locked_version` and `own_ref`. Completeness runs on the composed files.
  - 7(a): `artifacts.completeness(files, supplied=())` adds "Labs run only Node applications: the Proof bundle needs vulnerable-app/package.json" when neither holds it, and a `COPY` may name a supplied path; `RULES` is 3. `domain.lab_supplied` is the Own snapshot's paths at the Verdict's commit for an Own-repository Proof, `{vulnerable-app/package.json}` for an Upstream Proof with npm packages at a Locked version, else `()`. `set_finding_artifacts`, `finding_artifact_state` (whose cache also keys on a fingerprint of the supplied paths) and `POST /findings/{id}/lab-runs` pass it, so a non-Node bundle is Draft and refused before any Approval.
  - 7(b): `finding_artifacts` passes `own_repository`, `vulnerable_app`, `packages` and `locked_version`. For `own_head` it reads the Own repository at its `cursor` (pin basis `own_head`, with its `pin_message`); a `cve` Finding drafts for `minimal`, because its Lab supplies no Own tree.
  - 7(c): `domain.vulnerable_app_rule(db, application, repository)` is `own_commit`, `own_head` (an Upstream link with packages and a Locked version in an Application with an Own repository) or `minimal`. `commit_analysis` drafts for `own_commit` when the repository has an in-use Own link, else for `minimal` with the first in-use Upstream link's packages and Locked version as facts, and records the rule as the result's `vulnerable_app`. `run_playbook` publishes the drafted bundle only to targets with that rule; any other gets a Draft of `report.md` and `disclosure-report.md` (if drafted) and drafts its own bundle with **Generate**.
  - 7(e): `domain.require_lab` (the API route and `lab_run`) refuses, with 409 `lab_not_allowed`, a Lab while `domain.malicious_exposures` finds an exposed or unknown Malicious change Exposure on a link not removed: all of the Application's for a Lab built on the Own repository, only the Finding's repository's for a minimal app. `Workflows.require_clean_tree` refuses a Lab whose Own revision (the Verdict's commit or the Own `cursor`) reaches one of the Own repository's Malicious change commits in its read-only mirror, unless a merged revert Remediation PR of that commit has its `merge_commit_sha` in that revision (marked `ponytail:`: a hand revert keeps Labs refused). It clones only when the Own repository has a Malicious change commit.
  - `prompt_templates/sections/bundle.md` states the three rules, so a bundle carries only the Dockerfile and its own files. `runner/README.md` (Labs) describes what the backend sends; the runner is unchanged.
  - Tests (written first; each failed before the code):
    - `test_findings.py::test_an_upstream_proof_builds_the_own_repository_head_with_the_locked_version_installed`: Shop (Own acme/shop at HEAD) locks react-server-dom-webpack@19.0.0 and is exposed to the fixture React2Shell fix commit; its Finding gets a report-only Draft, the uploaded bundle completes it, and the lab call holds Shop's head files without `.dockerignore`, the bundle's Dockerfile, the registry's exact bytes at `.asset-monitor/react-server-dom-webpack-19.0.0.tgz`, and `package.json` and `package-lock.json` pointing at the `file:` spec with the lock entry at 19.0.0. The LabRun records the packages, 19.0.0 and HEAD.
    - `test_findings.py::test_an_upstream_proof_without_an_own_repository_builds_a_minimal_app_pinning_the_locked_version`: the facts read `minimal` with the package and version, the drafted bundle is complete, and the Lab gets the minimal `package.json` and the tarball, with no lockfile.
    - `test_findings.py::test_a_locked_package_whose_integrity_or_origin_does_not_match_is_refused`, over other bytes, another host and a sha1-only integrity: the job fails with `package_untrusted` and no lab call is made; a tarball on another host is never requested.
    - `test_findings.py::test_the_automatic_bundle_draft_is_told_its_vulnerable_app_rule` (7(b)) and `test_a_repository_own_for_one_application_and_upstream_for_another_gets_a_bundle_per_role` (7(c)).
    - `test_artifacts.py::test_a_non_node_bundle_is_refused_before_approval` (7(a)) and `test_a_lab_whose_tree_contains_a_known_malicious_change_is_refused` (7(e), AC-A07 "no job ever executes a Malicious change payload", on a local git repository acme/shop).
    - `test_remediation.py::test_dependency_files_with_a_spec_keeps_the_lock_entrys_version`; `test_prompts.py` checks the three rules.
    - Changed tests: `bundle()` carries `vulnerable-app/package.json`; Own-repository Lab tests draft without it, so the Own repository's wins. `test_an_upstream_proof_keeps_the_bundle_only_lab` is now `..._without_a_locked_package_keeps_the_bundle_only_lab` and checks `bundle_only`. Four `test_playbooks.py` tests on Shop (Own repository, Upstream numen/lib or numen/react with packages) expect the report-only Draft (`proof` reads `draft`) and upload Shop's own bundle before a Lab; the Disclosure report test stubs `Sources.npm_tarball`.
  - Deviations:
    - The Lab vendors every npm package the Upstream link stands for, all at its one Locked version, so LabRun and the facts carry `packages` (a list), not `package`: a React monorepo link stands for several packages.
    - `own_head` needs a package and a Locked version on the link; without them the rule is `minimal` and the Lab bundle-only, because the Own head cannot have an unknown version installed.
    - An Upstream Proof's Locked version is vendored as a `file:` tarball because the Lab's fetch gateway reaches only the public registry on port 443. This departs from A07's "phase one fetches dependencies" for those packages; phase one still installs them with install scripts disabled. Whether `npm ci` accepts the rewritten lock entry is left to T6 on the real Engine.
    - The journey's Upstream Finding downloads its bundle, which T4 refuses (A15); T6 extends the journey, and `make test-journey` was not run here.
  - Runs after T5 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to github.com, a registry or any other outside host:
    - Focused run (`test_findings`, `test_artifacts`, `test_remediation`, `test_detection_rules`, `test_prompts`, `test_playbooks`): 176 passed.
    - `make test-integration` against PostgreSQL: 506 passed.
    - `make check` passed.
    - No runner or frontend code changed, so `make test-runtime` and `make test-ui` were not re-run.

- **T6, the React2Shell journey on the real Engine, fixtures and documentation.**
  - Fixture server (`tests/integration/fixture_server.py`):
    - An npm registry under `/npm`. `GET /npm/react-server-dom-webpack/19.0.0` (and `/19.0.1`) gives `{name, version, repository: {url: git+https://github.com/numen/react-fixture.git}, dist: {tarball, integrity}}`, and `/npm/react-server-dom-webpack/-/react-server-dom-webpack-<v>.tgz` serves a `package/` tarball (`package.json`, `index.js`). `tar_gz` builds it in memory with gzip mtime 0, so its bytes, and the sha512 `integrity` of them, never change; `archive` uses it too.
    - The stand-in is not React's code and holds no exploit. 19.0.0's `decodeAction(body)` returns the fixed string `asset-monitor-simulated-flaw` for a JSON body with a `$$id` field and executes nothing; 19.0.1 throws on that field.
    - An Own Node app family `numen/node-app-<hex>`: one head commit `d`×40 (the commit list with an ETag, and the commit detail), `contents` for `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (lockfileVersion 3, the stand-in 19.0.0 resolved from the fixture registry with its integrity; every other manifest 404), and a tarball whose `server.js` routes `POST /action` through `decodeAction` (HTTP 400 `refused` when it throws).
    - `alias()` maps `numen/fixture-<hex>` and `numen/react-fixture` onto the fake Upstream repository, for GET and POST; its Release branch lookups (`/branches/{name}`) answer 404.
  - `compose.test.yaml`: the test env sets `NUMEN_GITHUB_GIT_URL: http://fixtures:8090/git`. The fixture answers 404 there, so a mirror clone would fail fast as unknown. The journey made no clone: the fixture logged no `/git` request.
  - `tests/integration/journey.py`:
    - The GitHub-fixture Upstream Finding: `exportable` is false and `GET /findings/{id}/download` answers 403 `upstream_proof_not_exported`; its artifact list still holds the bundle and `manifest.json`. Its bundle-only Lab still verifies.
    - New React2Shell section. An Application with the Own repository `https://github.com/numen/node-app-<suffix>` runs Check now; its `derive_upstreams` adds `https://github.com/numen/react-fixture` with packages `[npm:react-server-dom-webpack]` and Locked version 19.0.0 from the manifest. A second Check now and a Backfill of 1 follow, and the journey takes any stored commit of that repository, since repositories outlive runs. The Operator opens a commit Finding on it and uploads `poc.py`, `report.md`, `disclosure-report.md` and `vulnerable-app/` `Dockerfile` (`FROM asset-monitor-lab:0.1.0`, `WORKDIR /app`, `COPY . /app/`, `CMD ["node", "server.js"]`), `compose.yaml`, `README.md` and `application.lock.json`; the Lab supplies `package.json`, so the bundle is complete. `disclosure_report` equals the upload with the form link `https://github.com/numen/react-fixture/security/advisories/new`, `exportable` is false, the bundle zip and `poc.py`'s content give 403 `upstream_proof_not_exported`, and `report.md` reads back unchanged. A wrong password is refused with `approval_refused`. The approved Lab, on the real Engine, builds the Own head with the 19.0.0 stand-in vendored as `file:.asset-monitor/react-server-dom-webpack-19.0.0.tgz` and installed by phase one's `npm ci --ignore-scripts`: `verified`, the Finding Confirmed with cause `proof`, and the Lab run's `packages` `[react-server-dom-webpack]`, `locked_version` 19.0.0 and `own_ref` `d`×40. A PATCH sets the link's Locked version to 19.0.1, and a second approved Lab (vendoring 19.0.1) is `not_reproduced`, with the Finding's state and cause unchanged (AC-A07). The Application is archived with the others at the end.
  - Before the journey, the same composition was checked offline on the real Engine: the backend's `remediation.dependency_files(..., spec="file:...")` over the fixture's `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, then `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund` in `asset-monitor-lab:0.1.0` with `--network none`. It installed the stand-in, and the app answered the marker for 19.0.0 and HTTP 400 for 19.0.1. The journey's Labs then proved it through the broker.
  - Docs: README.md (the Own and Upstream cells of the Playbook matrix, Upstream Proofs never downloadable, the journey's React2Shell case); `docs/implementation-contract.md` (two statements that said Phase 7 was still to come now describe it, and a bullet on the Phase 7 fixtures and the vendored Locked version); this entry. `runner/README.md` was not touched.
  - The journey changes and the fixtures were written together, and they passed on the first run, so I did not see the new section fail first. The Upstream download step was already failing before T6 (T4 refuses it; T5 noted it).
  - Deviations:
    - The PATCH to 19.0.1 queues no `exposure_check`. `domain.queue_exposure` (T2) queues none while the repository has no commit with a Verdict that opens a Finding, and the journey runs no Analysis agent. The journey waits for any such job of the Application, and there is none.
    - The node-app family has no compare route: its one commit means a check never compares (no cursor first, then a 304).
    - The journey's Upstream Finding is Operator-opened, so it carries no Exposure row. Exposure at 19.0.0 is proven by `test_exposure.py::test_an_application_locking_19_0_0_is_exposed_to_the_react2shell_fix`.
- **Phase 7 summary.**
  - Tests by acceptance criterion (backend tests use FixtureBroker, local git repositories built by `conftest.git_repository` and stubbed registry calls; none reaches github.com or a registry):
    - AC-A05 (Exposure): `test_exposure.py`: `test_an_application_locking_19_0_0_is_exposed_to_the_react2shell_fix`, `test_a_locked_version_whose_tag_contains_the_fix_is_not_exposed`, `test_a_release_branch_backport_with_the_same_patch_id_is_not_exposed` (the backport case), `test_an_unresolvable_tag_is_unknown_and_counts_as_exposed`, `test_the_locked_version_maps_to_the_first_existing_tag`, `test_a_wordpress_commit_is_contained_in_tags_copied_at_or_after_it` (SVN `tags/`), `test_an_introduced_or_malicious_commit_exposes_only_releases_that_contain_it`, `test_bumping_a_locked_version_into_a_release_with_a_known_malicious_change_opens_a_finding` (the Malicious change bump case), `test_recomputing_exposure_runs_the_playbook_only_when_it_becomes_exposed`, `test_a_locked_version_changed_during_analysis_is_checked_again`, `test_a_lockfile_rederivation_recomputes_exposure`, and `test_the_mirror_takes_the_read_token_only_from_its_environment`.
    - AC-A12, the not-exposed case: `test_exposure.py::test_a_not_exposed_verdict_is_recorded_on_the_upstream_repository_only`.
    - AC-A06, the Own and Upstream columns: `test_playbooks.py`: `test_each_verdict_runs_only_the_steps_in_its_cell` (six cells), `test_a_step_that_needs_approval_waits_visibly_without_blocking_the_others`, `test_without_an_own_repository_upstream_remediation_steps_are_skipped`, `test_an_upstream_malicious_change_pins_the_latest_release_without_it`, `test_the_react2shell_fix_bumps_to_the_first_release_that_contains_it`, and `test_the_deployed_version_check_reports_before_the_fix_serving_the_fix_or_unknown` (including unknown, never a guess); `test_remediation_prs.py::test_without_an_own_repository_remediation_prs_are_skipped`; UI `Findings.integration.test.tsx` and `CommitReview.integration.test.tsx`.
    - A11, the Own Vulnerability fix remediated by the Deployed version check: `test_playbooks.py::test_serving_the_fix_remediates_only_an_own_vulnerability_fix_finding`.
    - A07, the Upstream vulnerable app and the Lab refusals: `test_findings.py`: `test_an_upstream_proof_builds_the_own_repository_head_with_the_locked_version_installed`, `test_an_upstream_proof_without_an_own_repository_builds_a_minimal_app_pinning_the_locked_version`, `test_a_locked_package_whose_integrity_or_origin_does_not_match_is_refused`, `test_an_upstream_proof_without_a_locked_package_keeps_the_bundle_only_lab`, `test_the_automatic_bundle_draft_is_told_its_vulnerable_app_rule` (7(b)), `test_a_repository_own_for_one_application_and_upstream_for_another_gets_a_bundle_per_role` (7(c)), `test_the_summary_carries_malicious_change` (7(d)); `test_artifacts.py`: `test_a_non_node_bundle_is_refused_before_approval` (7(a)), `test_a_lab_whose_tree_contains_a_known_malicious_change_is_refused` (7(e)); `test_remediation.py::test_dependency_files_with_a_spec_keeps_the_lock_entrys_version`; and the journey on the real Engine.
    - A15, Upstream Proofs never exported and the Disclosure report never sent: `test_artifacts.py::test_downloading_an_upstream_proof_is_refused`, `test_playbooks.py::test_an_upstream_vulnerability_introduced_shows_its_disclosure_report_and_never_sends_it`, UI `Findings.integration.test.tsx` and `ArtifactPreview.test.tsx` (no download control), and the journey.
    - Plan Done-when "the React2Shell fixture is exposed at `react-server-dom-webpack@19.0.0` and reproduces in a Lab after Approval": exposure by `test_exposure.py::test_an_application_locking_19_0_0_is_exposed_to_the_react2shell_fix`, reproduction by the journey ("PASS React2Shell stand-in 19.0.0 reproduces in a Lab after Approval"), and the fixed 19.0.1 stand-in shows Not reproduced.
  - Deviations and accepted limits (the planner's risks):
    - An Upstream Proof's Locked version is downloaded by the worker from `NUMEN_NPM_REGISTRY_URL`, checked against its sha512 integrity, and vendored as a `file:` dependency, because the Lab's fetch gateway reaches only public hosts on port 443. This departs from A07's "phase one fetches dependencies" for that package; phase one still installs it with install scripts disabled, and still fetches the app's other dependencies from the registry. The journey proves `npm ci` accepts the rewritten lock entry on the real Engine.
    - The journey runs no Analysis agent, so Verdict-driven Exposure and Playbooks are proven only in backend tests with FixtureBroker and local git repositories.
    - Every Exposure, cleanup-report, Deployed version and Lab tree computation makes a fresh blobless bare clone, with no disk bound (`ponytail:`); large Upstream repositories such as React will be slow. Backports older than the newest 1,000 non-merge commits of the watched branches are missed, and that release reads exposed.
    - A tag that cannot be named or matched gives unknown, which counts as exposed: expect more Findings, and Remediation PR steps that read `no_safe_release` or `waiting_for_release`.
    - "The first tag that contains it" and "the latest tag that does not" are ordered by git creatordate, never by semantic version, so a re-tagged or back-dated release can be picked wrongly.
    - A release tag added without a new commit is seen only at the next source check that is not a 304 (AC-A02 forbids any job on a 304). Turning a link on recomputes nothing.
    - A Locked version changed while an Analysis runs is caught by `commit_analysis` queueing an `exposure_check` after its fence; concurrent checks for different Applications rely on the (application_id, commit_id) unique key.
    - The A15 refusal also covers cve Findings on an Upstream source repository and Analyses of Upstream commits. That changes Numen's AC-12 beyond commit Findings, and existing download tests were edited. Markdown reports stay readable in the page.
    - An `own_head` bundle needs the Operator to click Generate, because the Analysis never sees the Own repository, so demo step A16-4 needs one extra click.
    - "Pin or bump" for an Upstream Vulnerability introduced is a pin to the latest release without the commit, and a monorepo link pins or bumps all of its packages to the tag's version.
    - A Lab stays refused while the Own tree reaches a Malicious change commit, until a merged Cure revert is in that tree; a hand revert that went through no Remediation PR keeps Labs refused.
    - When a Locked version change makes an Application no longer exposed, its open Findings stay as they are; remediation by a version bump on the default branch is Phase 8 (AC-A09).
    - The git read token reaches the clone only through `GIT_CONFIG_*`, scoped to the GitHub base and never in argv, and `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL` limits clones to https (the fixture scheme in test mode).
  - Still open after Phase 7: the Findings list shows a "Lab unverified" badge on Own Vulnerability fix rows, which have no Lab, because the summary carries no Playbook; the Playbook's `proof` step does not show that a Lab is refused for a Malicious change Exposure until Start Lab is pressed; the Jobs page names `exposure_check` generically, with no summary in `frontend/src/jobSummary.ts`.
  - Runs after T6 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to github.com, a registry or any other outside host:
    - `make test-journey` (which runs `make build` first) passed: every step printed PASS, including "PASS an Upstream Finding shows its Disclosure report for copying, and its Proof is never exported", "PASS React2Shell stand-in 19.0.0 reproduces in a Lab after Approval" and "PASS the fixed 19.0.1 stand-in shows Not reproduced and leaves the Finding as it was", then JOURNEY PASSED. The fixture's request log showed only `/repos/numen/fixture`, `/repos/numen/node-app`, `/npm/react-server-dom-webpack/...`, the NVD route, `/health` and `/admin/state`.
    - `make check` passed.
    - `make test`: unit 246 passed and runner 138 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests); integration 506 passed against PostgreSQL; runtime 203 passed, 1 skipped on the real Engine; UI 144 passed in 19 files.

### Phase 7 review fixes (2026-11-14)

These supersede three deviations above: "Turning a link on recomputes nothing", "An `own_head` bundle needs the Operator to click Generate", and "Markdown reports stay readable in the page" (now only the two reports).

- **A link turned on, or an Application restored, computed no Exposure.** A dependency that a later derivation adds starts off (A01), so its Exposure to Verdicts already stored on the shared repository was never computed, and a Locked version holding a known Malicious change opened nothing. `PATCH /applications/{id}/repositories/{repository_id}` now queues `domain.queue_exposure` when it turns a link on, and `PATCH /applications/{id}` when it restores an archived Application, for each of its Upstream links. `test_exposure.py::test_a_dependency_turned_on_later_gets_its_exposure_and_playbook`.
- **Generate dropped the Disclosure report.** `finding_artifacts` published only the agent's outputs, and `domain.disclosure_report` reads the current revision, so a regenerated Upstream Vulnerability introduced bundle lost it. The run now keeps the current revision's `disclosure-report.md` when its agent writes none. `test_playbooks.py::test_an_upstream_vulnerability_introduced_shows_its_disclosure_report_and_never_sends_it` (the bundle drafted for Shop's head keeps it) and `test_findings.py::test_a_repository_own_for_one_application_and_upstream_for_another_gets_a_bundle_per_role`.
- **A role whose Lab builds another app got no bundle.** `run_playbook` now calls `domain.draft_bundle` for an Application whose `vulnerable_app_rule` is not the one the Analysis drafted for (an `own_head` Application, or the Upstream role of a repository that is Own for another). It queues an automatic `finding_artifacts` run (input `automatic: true`, `shared: false`, `source_ref: null`), which joins `CAPPED_KINDS` and so counts against the Analysis caps like patch drafting. With no API-key profile, or over a cap (where `over_cap` records the cap Alert), none is queued and the Proof stays a Draft for **Generate** (`ponytail:`: no waiting_for_cap resume for bundle drafts). Demo step A16-4 now needs no Generate click. Tests: `own_head_bundle` in `test_playbooks.py` and `test_findings.py::test_an_upstream_proof_builds_the_own_repository_head_with_the_locked_version_installed` run the queued draft instead of uploading; `test_the_automatic_bundle_draft_is_told_its_vulnerable_app_rule` (formerly the Generate test) also checks the cap; the per-role test runs the `minimal` draft for Partner. Test helpers that leave drafts queued (`analyze`, `QUEUED`, `QUEUED_ONLY`, `drain`) now leave `finding_artifacts` queued too.
- **A Malicious change with no Exposure row did not refuse a Lab.** `domain.malicious_exposures` now selects the Malicious change commits of the current generation on the Application's Upstream repositories (links not removed) whose Exposure is `exposed`, `unknown` or missing; missing is unknown (A05, decision 12). `test_artifacts.py::test_a_lab_whose_tree_contains_a_known_malicious_change_is_refused` deletes the row and is refused.
- **A link added during an Analysis got a Finding without Exposure.** After its fence `commit_analysis` now queues an `exposure_check` for each in-use Upstream link it read no Exposure for and leaves that Application out of its Playbooks, so no Finding or Alert opens before Exposure decides (AC-A12). `test_exposure.py::test_an_upstream_link_added_during_analysis_gets_its_own_exposure_check`.
- **Every `.md` file of an Upstream Proof was downloadable.** `GET /artifacts/{id}/content` now exempts only `report.md` and `disclosure-report.md` (`api.REPORTS`), so the bundle's `vulnerable-app/README.md` is refused. The UI previews only those two paths for a non-exportable resource, and its size notice no longer says "Download it". `test_downloading_an_upstream_proof_is_refused` adds `vulnerable-app/README.md`; `ArtifactPreview.test.tsx` adds it to the Upstream case.
- **The backport scan read mostly default-branch history.** `exposure.equivalents` now reads only the Release branches' commits that the default branch does not reach (`<release>... ^HEAD ^sha`), and every watched branch only for a commit the default branch does not reach. `Workflows.backports` catches a failed or timed-out scan and keeps the commit itself, so containment no longer turns every Exposure unknown. The `ponytail:` note names the 1,000-commit window and the 300 s per-command ceiling. `test_a_release_branch_backport_with_the_same_patch_id_is_not_exposed` adds the failed-scan case.
- **Rejected: the journey running an Exposure-opened Upstream Finding end to end.** The compose stack has no agent harness, by design since Phase 4 (no paid model requests; FixtureBroker exists only in backend tests), so it cannot produce the Verdict that opens such a Finding, and serving the fixture repository over git HTTP would compute nothing without one. The journey still shows an Upstream Finding with its Disclosure report, `exportable: false`, refused downloads and the React2Shell stand-in Lab on the real Engine; Exposure-opened Findings and their Disclosure reports are proven in `test_exposure.py` and `test_playbooks.py` against local git repositories.
- Runs after these fixes on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to github.com, a registry or any other outside host: `make build` passed; `make check` passed; `make test-unit`: 246 passed, runner 138 passed and 66 skipped (the Engine tests); `make test-integration`: 508 passed against PostgreSQL; `make test-runtime`: 203 passed, 1 skipped on the real Engine; `make test-ui`: 144 passed in 19 files; `make test-journey` passed with every step PASS, and its stack was stopped afterwards.

## Phase 8: Advisory check (2026-11-14)

Phase 8 (A09 and AC-A09; the Advisory check paragraph of A06; the advisory Finding source of A11, with remediation by a bump seen on the default branch). This section records each task as it lands.

What each task changed:
- **T1, pin collection, storage, triggers and the Advisory check job (no feeds yet).**
  - Migration `d3f4a5b6c7e8` adds every Phase 8 table and column, and only adds, so no existing data moves; its downgrade drops them. It adds `applications.advisory_pins` (JSON, null until the first check; model column `deferred`, so the Application list, `application_json` and the scheduler never load a large lockfile's pins). It adds table `advisory_matches` (model `AdvisoryMatch`: `application_id`, `vulnerability` (the canonical id), `aliases`, nullable `finding_id`, `state` `exposed`, `unknown` or `not_exposed`, `data`, timestamps), unique on (`application_id`, `vulnerability`). It adds table `advisory_cache` (model `AdvisoryCache`: `key` primary key, `etag`, `modified`, `body`, `fetched_at`). Nothing writes the two tables until T2.
  - New module `backend/numen/advisories.py`, pure (no database, no network):
    - `pin_file(path)`: a basename in `prefilter.MANIFESTS`, `.nvmrc`, `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.*` or `*.Dockerfile`.
    - `dockerfile_pins` reads each `FROM` (line continuations joined), skipping `--platform=` and other options and any earlier stage's `AS` name. An image `[registry/][library/]name[:tag][@digest]` whose name is in `RUNTIMES` (`node`, `php`) gives `{name, version, field: dockerfile, source, image, tag, reason}`. The image is the reference up to its name, such as `docker.io/library/node`. A tag `v?X.Y.Z` with an optional `-suffix` gives the version, so `node:20.11.1-alpine` is node 20.11.1 with image `node` and tag `20.11.1-alpine`. Otherwise the version is null with reason `a variable` (any `$` in the image), `no tag`, or `a moving tag` (`lts`, `20`, `latest`, `20-alpine`). `bitnami/node` and `ghcr.io/acme/node` are not read as Node.
    - `nvmrc_pin` (the first line that is not blank or a comment; otherwise `a moving tag`, as for `lts/*`), `engines_pin` (`package.json` `engines.node`) and `platform_pin` (`composer.json` `config.platform.php`) each take `=?v?X.Y.Z` as exact, and otherwise give reason `a range` (or `a variable`).
    - `collect(files, prefix, overrides)` gives `{runtimes, packages}`. It reads the manifests, lockfiles and `.nvmrc` at `prefix` (the repository's `source_path`) and Dockerfiles at any depth.
    - Packages are `{ecosystem, name, version, source, direct, reason}`, sorted by ecosystem and name. They cover every version in `dependencies.locked_versions` and every direct dependency. `source` is the lockfile path the version came from, found by reading each lockfile alone in `locked_versions`' order. A direct dependency with no lock entry has version null, `source` its manifest and reason `no lockfile entry`. An override, from an in-use derived Upstream link whose `locked_version_source` is `operator` or `deployed`, replaces that package's version with source `Locked version (operator)` or `Locked version (deployed)`. It applies only to a package the snapshot still lists.
    - An unreadable manifest or lockfile raises `invalid_manifest` (422). `dependencies.UNREADABLE` is now the exception tuple that both this and `derive_upstreams` catch.
  - `domain.advisory_repository` (the Own repository, else `source_repository`), `queue_advisory` (job kind `advisory_check`, input `repository_input(repository)`, with the given idempotency key), `schedule_advisory_checks` and `advisories_json`. `schedule_advisory_checks` queues, for each Application that is monitored, not archived and whose advisory repository has a cursor, one check per UTC day with key `advisory:{id}:{YYYY-MM-DD}`, skipping a key that exists. `advisories_json` returns `{repository_id, revision, collected_at, feeds, runtimes, packages}`, where every pin gains `exposure`: `unknown` when its version is null, else null until T2 matches it, and never `not_exposed` for an unreadable version. `APPLICATION_SUBJECTS` maps `advisory_check` to `("advisory", "advisories")`.
  - `scheduler.main` calls `schedule_advisory_checks` after `schedule`, in its own transaction. `domain.schedule` is unchanged, so its tests and their job counts are unchanged.
  - `Workflows.advisory_check` refuses with 409 `input_changed` unless the job's repository is still the advisory repository (`execute` already refuses a changed source generation). It reads the overrides from the Application's links, and `self.snapshot(...)` at the job's `source_ref` (a stored snapshot is reused, so a daily check of an unchanged head makes no request). It then runs `advisories.collect`, fences, locks the Application row with `db.refresh(..., with_for_update=True)`, and writes `advisory_pins`. It returns `{source_ref, runtimes, packages, unknown}`. `worker.LONG_KINDS` gains `advisory_check`.
  - `Workflows.queue_derivation` also queues `advisory:{application}:{head}` for each Own link (not removed, Application not archived) when an imported default-branch commit's files (path or previous path) include a pin file. The Baseline imports no commit and queues none; the daily check is the first one.
  - API: `GET /api/applications/{id}/advisories` returns `advisories_json`. `POST /api/applications/{id}/advisory-checks` (optional `Idempotency-Key`) returns 202 `{job_id, status_url}`, or 422 `repository_required` with no repository and 422 `source_not_checked` when the advisory repository has no cursor.
  - Tests:
    - `tests/test_advisories.py`:
      - `test_runtime_pins_are_read_from_dockerfile_nvmrc_engines_and_composer_platform` (7 cases);
      - `test_an_unreadable_runtime_version_is_unknown_not_not_exposed` (node:lts, node:20, `node:${NODE_VERSION}`, a bare `FROM node`, `.nvmrc` `lts/*` and engines `>=20`, each run end to end through the endpoint and the worker; GET shows version null, the reason and exposure `unknown`);
      - `test_package_versions_come_from_lockfiles_and_locked_versions` (a source path, a package-lock v3 with a transitive package, composer.lock, an operator override, a direct dependency with no lock entry, `invalid_manifest`);
      - `test_a_pin_file_change_on_the_own_default_branch_queues_an_advisory_check` (test_dependencies' fixture GitHub, with `snapshot_github` reading its trees: the Baseline and a README push queue none; a Dockerfile push and then a lockfile push each queue and run `advisory:{app}:{head}`; the pins read node 20.11.1 and react 19.0.0 from `package-lock.json`);
      - `test_the_advisory_check_runs_once_per_utc_day` (two calls queue one job, the next day another; an Application with monitoring off, and one whose repository has no head, get none);
      - `test_run_advisory_check_endpoint` (both 422s; with no Own repository the source repository's snapshot is read and an operator Locked version on a derived link replaces the lockfile's; a check queued before an Own repository was linked fails with `input_changed`).
    - `tests/test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_advisory_check_tables`: upgrade from `c2b3d4e5f6a7`; `alembic check` is clean; existing Applications read `advisory_pins` null; a duplicate (`application_id`, `vulnerability`) raises `IntegrityError`; the downgrade drops both tables and the column and keeps the Application and its Finding; then the same again.
  - Deviations:
    - The change trigger fires only for pin-file changes on the Own repository's default branch after its Baseline. An Application with no Own repository gets the daily and the manual check only, because queueing on Upstream source repositories would make existing backend tests drain snapshot-fetching jobs.
    - Daily scheduling is the separate `domain.schedule_advisory_checks`, not part of `domain.schedule`.
    - The trigger matches a pin file at any depth, but `collect` reads only the manifests at the source path, so a nested `package.json` change queues a check that may find nothing new.
    - `Idempotency-Key` on the POST is an addition, as on the other manual job routes.
  - Limits, marked `ponytail:` where they are code:
    - Two checks of one Application that run at once keep the pins of the one that finishes last, which may be an older head's; the next change or daily check reads the head again.
    - The pins list is not paginated.
    - A check of a GitHub repository whose head snapshot is not stored needs the read token, like any snapshot.
    - The existing `test_dependencies.py::test_lockfile_change_adds_new_dependencies_off_and_marks_removed_ones` now also runs an Advisory check after its lockfile pushes. That check fails on the fixture's missing tarball route (`tarfile.ReadError`, `internal_job_error`), and the test still passes, because it asserts only the Check now jobs.
  - Not yet: feeds and matching (T2), Findings (T3), the bump Remediation PR and remediation (T4), the UI, the journey, README and the implementation contract (T5). AC-A09's fifth bullet (ten checks of an unchanged page start no new Triage) is A10's, so Phase 9 tests it.
  - Outside traffic: a temporary hook on `httpx.HTTPTransport.handle_request`, added to `conftest.py` for one full PostgreSQL run of the backend suite and then removed, logged no request at all. The suite's only Advisory checks were the new tests' (all through stored snapshots or the MockTransport fixture), test_dependencies' failed check above, and the expected `input_changed`. No request reached github.com, a registry or any other outside host.
  - Runs after T1 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB):
    - the focused run (`test_advisories`, `test_dependencies`, `test_intake`, `test_repositories`, `test_workflows`): 134 passed;
    - `make check` passed;

- **T2, OSV, GHSA and NVD matching, version-range Exposure and alias-merged AdvisoryMatch keys.** No Finding is opened yet.
  - `Sources` (worker code; no agent fetches a feed):
    - `osv` is `https://api.osv.dev`, or `NUMEN_OSV_URL` in test mode. GHSA uses the GitHub base (`NUMEN_GITHUB_API_URL`) and the read token. NVD uses the NVD base (`NUMEN_NVD_API_URL`) and the NVD account key.
    - `get(..., json=)` POSTs the body, with the same retries (429, 502-504 with `Retry-After`) and size limit. A 304 is returned like any other answer below 400.
    - `feed_json` refuses an answer that is not JSON of the expected type with 502 `invalid_source_response`.
    - `osv_query(queries)`: `POST {osv}/v1/querybatch`, 1000 queries a request, each followed through `next_page_token` for at most 10 pages, else `source_history_limit`. It returns `[[{id, modified}]]`. `osv_vuln(id)`: `GET {osv}/v1/vulns/{id}`.
    - `ghsa_advisories(params, etag)`: `GET {github}/advisories` with `per_page=100` and `If-None-Match`. It follows `Link: rel=next` for at most 10 pages, and only to the GitHub base, so the token goes nowhere else. It returns `(etag, items)`, or None on 304. GitHub's REST docs confirm that `affects` takes a comma-separated list of up to 1000 `name@version`, and that `first_patched_version` is a string.
    - `nvd_cpe(cpe_name)`: `GET {nvd}?cpeName=&startIndex=` with the `apiKey` header, every page (at most 10). Outside test mode it sleeps 0.7 s with a key, or 6.1 s without one, before each request.
  - `advisories.py`, pure:
    - `RUNTIMES`: node is OSV `Bitnami/node` and `cpe:2.3:a:nodejs:node.js`; php is `Bitnami/php` and `cpe:2.3:a:php:php`. `ECOSYSTEMS`: npm (OSV `npm`, GHSA `npm`, semver) and composer (OSV `Packagist`, GHSA `composer`, a numeric tuple). Runtimes compare as plain semver.
    - `compare` uses the existing `cves.compare_versions` semver for npm and the runtimes. For composer it compares numeric tuples, padded with zeros, with an optional `v`, so `v1.2.10` is after `1.2.9`. Anything else gives None.
    - `queries(collected)` sends only pins whose version can be read in their scheme: OSV `{package: {ecosystem, name}, version}`, GHSA `{ecosystem, affects}` (100 `name@version` a request) and NVD `{cpe}:{version}:*:*:*:*:*:*:*`. Composer versions are sent without a leading `v`. A pin with a null or unreadable version is never sent, because matching every advisory for its product would open one Finding per advisory. It stays `unknown` in the pins list.
    - Ranges have one form for every feed: a list of comparator lists, such as `[[">=","20.0.0"],["<","20.19.5"]]`. The range functions return `{pin identity: ranges}`, where an identity is `("runtime", None, name)` or `("package", ecosystem, name)`. They are `osv_ranges` (SEMVER and ECOSYSTEM events, with introduced `0` as no lower bound, `fixed` exclusive and `last_affected` inclusive), `ghsa_ranges` (`vulnerable_version_range` split on commas, plus `< first_patched_version` when the range has no upper bound) and `nvd_ranges` (each vulnerable `cpeMatch` with a runtime's CPE, by its `versionStart*`/`versionEnd*` fields and the criteria's exact version, where `-` reads as unknown). An operator it cannot read becomes `?`, which gives None.
    - `affected(version, ranges, scheme)`: True when every comparator of some list holds. A comparator that fails rules its list out, even beside one that cannot be read. Otherwise any None gives None, else False. `pin_state`: any feed True is `exposed`, else any None is `unknown` (counted as exposed), else `not_exposed`.
    - `aliases(feed, raw)`: OSV `id` and `aliases`; GHSA `ghsa_id`, `cve_id` and `identifiers[].value`; NVD `id`. Only ids matching `[A-Z][A-Z0-9]*-[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,80}` are kept. `canonical` is the first by (not CVE, not GHSA, id).
    - `match(collected, records, known)` unions records whose alias sets overlap. Each stored row's alias set is seeded as a bridge, so two groups that each share an alias with one row become one. For each group it gives `{vulnerability, aliases, state, pins: [{kind, ecosystem, name, version, sources, ranges: {feed: ranges}, state}], summary, severity, references, feeds, records}`. The summary is GHSA's, then OSV's, then NVD's English description, at most 500 characters. The severity is GHSA's, else NVD's highest base score. The references pass `cves.safe_reference`, at most 20. A group that concerns no readable pin is dropped.
    - `runtime_cves(records)`: the CVE ids of OSV and NVD records of a runtime, for which the check asks GHSA `cve_id=`. GHSA has no runtime ecosystem, so this only adds aliases, summary and severity.
    - `refresh(old, group, collected)`: a stored match's data and state after a check. For each pin, each feed's latest ranges win, and a feed that did not return the vulnerability keeps its stored ranges. Every pin is then read again from the new collection. So a row that no feed returns (as after a bump to a fixed version) is read against its stored ranges, and a feed that fails or falls silent never makes a pin read as not exposed.
  - `Workflows.advisory_check`, before the fence, calls `advisory_feeds`. It fetches OSV, then NVD, then GHSA (whose `cve_id` lookups need the first two), each through `AdvisoryCache`:
    - `osv:{id}` is fetched again only when the querybatch `modified` differs;
    - `ghsa:{sha256(params)}` is revalidated by its ETag;
    - `nvd:{cpeName}` is reused when fetched less than 12 hours ago.
    Each feed runs in its own `try/except DomainError`. An error is recorded as `advisory_pins.feeds[feed] = {error: {code, message}}`, and success as `{records: n}`. After the fence it writes `feeds`, `domain.store_advisory_cache(db, writes)` (key order, so two concurrent checks cannot deadlock; insert or update with a savepoint, as `record_exposure`), and `domain.record_advisory_matches`. The job result adds `matches`, the count of rows that are `exposed` or `unknown`.
  - `domain.record_advisory_matches(db, application, records)` loads the Application's rows, oldest first, and calls `advisories.match`. A group that overlaps rows folds into the oldest: the key is kept, the aliases merge and the other overlapping rows are deleted (`ponytail:`). Otherwise it creates the row through `begin_nested`, re-reading on `IntegrityError`. Every row, including one that no feed returned this time, then gets `refresh`'s data and state. `advisory_json(row)` is `{id, vulnerability, aliases, state, finding_id, pins, summary, severity, references, feeds, records, updated_at}`. `advisories_json` adds `matches`. A pin's `exposure` is `unknown` when its version cannot be read; otherwise it is the worst state of the matches that list its version, else `not_exposed` once any feed answered, else null.
  - `cves.safe_reference` returns False instead of raising on a URL that `urlparse` refuses, such as an unclosed IPv6 bracket from a feed.
  - Test safety: the autouse `local_sources` fixture sets `NUMEN_OSV_URL=http://osv.invalid` and `NUMEN_NVD_API_URL=http://nvd.invalid`. No existing test asserts the default NVD URL. `NUMEN_GITHUB_API_URL` is left alone, because tests assert its default, and GHSA is never asked without a read token. `compose.test.yaml` sets `NUMEN_OSV_URL=http://fixtures:8090/osv` for the app and the worker. The fixture server answers 404 there until T5 adds OSV routes, so a journey Advisory check records an OSV feed error and never reaches api.osv.dev.
  - Tests, `tests/test_advisories.py`. The `Feeds` MockTransport stub serves `osv.fixture` (querybatch and vulns), `nvd.fixture` (by `cpeName`'s CPE identity) and `github.fixture/advisories` (`cve_id` or `ecosystem`+`affects`, an ETag and a 304). It hands every other request to a fallback (test_dependencies' `Fixtures`) and logs each request with its status. It holds the CVE-2026-111497 fixture: OSV `BIT-node-2026-111497` (Bitnami/node, 20.0.0 to 20.19.5 and 22.0.0 to 22.12.0, aliases the CVE and `GHSA-9c4w-m7v2-x5qh`), the GHSA with `cve_id` and identifiers, and NVD `nodejs:node.js` from 20.0.0 up to 20.19.5. Like a loose feed, it answers a record for its package whatever the version, so Exposure is the check's own comparison. Every Feeds test asserts that the request hosts are a subset of {osv.fixture, nvd.fixture, github.fixture}.
    - `test_ranges_use_each_ecosystems_version_semantics` (unit): GHSA `>= 19.0.0, < 19.0.1` and first_patched_version; OSV events, introduced 0, last_affected, a GIT range ignored, listed versions without a range, a PyPI entry ignored; NVD versionEnd and an exact criteria version; composer `v1.2.10` > `1.2.9`; an unparseable version or bound giving None, a failing comparator beating it, and `~>` giving None.
    - `test_osv_ghsa_and_nvd_aliases_key_one_match`: `node:20.11.1-alpine` gives exactly one row, keyed `CVE-2026-111497`, with aliases {BIT, CVE, GHSA}, state `exposed`, feeds `[ghsa, nvd, osv]`, GHSA's summary and severity, and the pin `node 20.11.1` from `Dockerfile` with OSV and NVD ranges. The test also checks the GHSA `cve_id` request, the NVD `cpeName` and the OSV query.
    - `test_the_next_days_check_keeps_one_match_and_a_later_alias_merges_into_it`: a second run with the cache aged by one day keeps one row. Then OSV lists only a new `OSV-2026-111497` whose only alias is the GHSA id, and NVD lists nothing. That record folds into the same row and key, adds its alias and keeps NVD's stored ranges. After a bump to `node:20.19.5` that no feed lists, the same row reads `not_exposed` for 20.19.5.
    - `test_a_version_outside_every_range_is_not_exposed_and_an_unreadable_range_is_unknown`: `node:22.12.0` is `not_exposed`. A GHSA react advisory with range `~> 19.0` is `unknown` (job `matches` 1). `.nvmrc lts/*` and a direct dependency with no lock entry read `unknown` in the pins list, take no part in a match, and are absent from the OSV, NVD and GHSA requests.
    - `test_feed_responses_are_cached`: the second run makes only the OSV querybatch (no vulns GET), sends GHSA `If-None-Match` and is answered 304, and makes no NVD request. A changed `modified` refetches the OSV record.
    - `test_a_feed_that_fails_is_recorded_and_the_others_still_match`: with no read token, `feeds.ghsa.error.code` is `github_token_required`, and the OSV and NVD records still give the one exposed match, with the GHSA id taken from OSV's aliases.
    - T1's tests now run with Feeds. The unreadable-version cases also assert that no feed request was made. The pin-file change test's node pin reads `exposed`, and the endpoint test's job result has `matches: 1`.
  - Deviations:
    - AC-A09's "ranges from all three feeds" holds only for packages. For a runtime pin GHSA contributes aliases, summary and severity but no ranges, because GHSA has no runtime ecosystem (as the plan says). The keying test asserts OSV and NVD ranges plus feeds `[ghsa, nvd, osv]`.
    - `record_advisory_matches` takes the feed records and calls `advisories.match` itself, seeding the stored alias sets, instead of taking groups from the workflow.
    - `refresh`, which re-reads every stored match against each new collection, is in T2 so that the state and the pins' exposure are right after a bump. T4 only has to act on the move to `not_exposed`.
    - OSV `affected[].versions` count only for an entry with no SEMVER or ECOSYSTEM range (`ponytail:`), not as the union the OSV schema defines, because OSV also lists a range's versions there.
    - `compose.test.yaml` was not in T2's file list; one line points the journey's OSV at the fixture server.
  - Limits, marked `ponytail:` where they are code: two keys joined by a later alias fold into the oldest row and the other row is deleted, leaving its Finding as it is; a pin no longer collected, or whose version can no longer be read, keeps its last entries and never reads `not_exposed`; only GHSA's first-page ETag is kept; composer stability suffixes read as unknown; npm prerelease range rules are not modelled; cache rows are never pruned, so a GHSA key from an old package set stays.
  - Not checked against the live APIs (no test may reach them; GitHub's docs were read, NVD's page did not render): whether NVD's `cpeName` needs an entry in the CPE dictionary (if so, `virtualMatchString` is the alternative); OSV's runtime coverage through the Bitnami ecosystem; whether GHSA's `type` defaults to `reviewed` when `affects` or `cve_id` is set; and whether OSV accepts composer versions without the `v`.
  - Outside traffic: a temporary hook on `httpx.HTTPTransport.handle_request`, added to `conftest.py` for one full SQLite run of the backend suite (507 passed, 25 PostgreSQL-only skipped) and then removed, logged no request at all. So the safety-net `.invalid` hosts were never even dialled. Every feed request in the new tests went to the `Feeds` stub's fixture hosts.
  - Runs after T2 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB):
    - the focused run (`test_advisories`, `test_workflows`, `test_dependencies`, with ruff): 104 passed;
    - `make check` passed;
    - `make test-unit`: 246 passed, and runner 138 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests);
    - `make test-integration`: 532 passed against PostgreSQL.
    - Runtime, UI and the journey were not re-run. T2 changes no runner or frontend code. The journey gains only `NUMEN_OSV_URL`, and T5 runs it with the fixture's feed routes.
- **T3, advisory Findings: opened through `open_finding`, or attached to an existing commit or cve Finding.**
  - `domain.attach_advisory(db, application, match)` runs for each row after `record_advisory_matches`:
    - It does nothing for a `not_exposed` match or one whose `finding_id` is set. So the next day's check opens no duplicate, and a Dismissed or Remediated Finding is never reopened.
    - Otherwise it looks for the Application's oldest Open or Confirmed commit or cve Finding whose `finding_aliases` overlap the match's aliases. `finding_aliases` is a cve Finding's `cve_id`, or the non-SHA `addresses` of a commit Finding's source Analysis, plus the aliases of the matches already attached to it. If one is found, the match is attached: `finding_id` is set, an `advisory_attached` Action records `{vulnerability, aliases}`, and `transition_finding(..., "confirmed", {by: advisory, advisory_id})` moves Open to Confirmed (A11). No Alert is sent.
    - Otherwise it calls `open_finding(db, application, "advisory", vulnerability, "{name} {version}: {summary}", vulnerability, {by: advisory, advisory_id, aliases})`, which sends the one fixed-template Alert (verdict `Advisory`, source the canonical id). It sets `target_applicability` to `affected` for an exposed match or `unknown` for an unknown one, and sets `finding_id`.
  - The job result adds `opened`, `attached` and `finding_ids`, the Findings this check opened or attached to.
  - `link_change` also links a fix to a Finding whose attached match has one of its addressed ids among its aliases. So a fix that addresses the GHSA id links to the advisory Finding keyed by the CVE.
  - Playbook (A06): `PLAYBOOKS[("advisory", None)] = (report, remediation_pr)`, and `finding_cell` returns that cell for source `advisory`. The `report` step reads `ready`. `remediation_pr` reads `no_own_repository`, or the draft's status, else `not_drafted` until T4 drafts the bump. `require_lab` refuses with 409 `lab_not_allowed`, "An Advisory check match gets a report and a bump Remediation PR, never a Lab Proof".
  - `advisories.report(advisory)` renders plain text from the stored feed data: the canonical id and its other aliases, severity, feeds, the summary (already capped at 500 characters when stored, so no 2,000 cap is applied), each pin with its state, sources and each feed's ranges, and the references. `finding_json` gains `advisories`: each attached match's `advisory_json` with its `report`. The report is never part of an Alert.
  - `api.remove_finding` clears `AdvisoryMatch.finding_id` for the deleted Finding, so the foreign key holds. Its next check opens a new Finding while the match is exposed (`ponytail:`); Dismiss is the way to close one.
  - Tests in `tests/test_advisories.py`, with every feed request going to the `Feeds` stub's fixture hosts:
    - `test_a_dockerfile_pinning_an_exposed_node_version_opens_exactly_one_finding_across_three_feeds` (AC-A09 bullet 1): one Finding with source `advisory`, `source_ref` `CVE-2026-111497`, `affected`, and title `node 20.11.1: …`. There is one Notification `{verdict: Advisory, source: CVE-2026-111497}`. A next-day run with the cache aged by one day opens nothing more and sends no second Alert, and after Dismiss a third run leaves it Dismissed.
    - `test_the_react2shell_package_advisory_and_the_exposed_fix_commit_share_one_finding[commit_first|advisory_first]` (AC-A09 bullet 2). It is built on `test_findings.react_fix`, which is `react_finding` split before its Analysis. Shop's Own head locks `react-server-dom-webpack@19.0.0`, the GHSA fixture `GHSA-fv66-9v8q-g76r` (`CVE-2025-55182`, `>= 19.0.0, < 19.0.1`) matches it, and the fix commit's Analysis addresses the GHSA id. Commit first: the match attaches, and the commit Finding becomes Confirmed with cause `advisory`, an `advisory_attached` event and no second Alert. Advisory first: the advisory Finding opens, and the fix links to it and opens nothing. Either way there is one Finding and one Alert.
    - `test_an_advisory_finding_runs_only_its_cell` (A06): a `not_exposed` match (node 22.12.0) opens nothing and sends no Alert. After a change to 20.11.1, the playbook is `[report ready, remediation_pr no_own_repository]`, the report text is asserted line by line, and POST `/lab-runs` answers 409 `lab_not_allowed`.
    - `test_deleting_an_advisory_finding_frees_its_match` (A11): a GHSA range that cannot be read gives an `unknown` match, which opens a Finding with `target_applicability` `unknown`. Deleting the Finding leaves the match with `finding_id` null.
    - `test_run_advisory_check_endpoint`'s job result now includes `opened`, `attached` and `finding_ids`.
  - Limits, marked `ponytail:`: the addresses of fixes linked to a commit Finding are not read as its aliases. An Analysis that commits while a check is running can open its commit Finding beside the advisory one, because `run_playbook` does not lock the Application row.
  - Runs after T3 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB):
    - the focused run (`test_advisories`, `test_findings`, `test_alerts`, `test_playbooks`, `test_exposure`): 115 passed;
    - `make check` passed;
    - `make test-unit`: 246 passed, and runner 138 passed, 66 skipped;
    - `make test-integration`: 537 passed against PostgreSQL.
    - Runtime, UI and the journey were not re-run, because T3 changes no runner or frontend code.
- **T4, the advisory bump Remediation PR, and remediation by a bump seen on the default branch.**
  - `advisories.lowest_fixed(version, ranges, scheme)`: the lowest `<` bound above the pinned version that every feed's ranges read as not affected. GHSA's `first_patched_version` is stored as a `<` bound when its range has no upper bound, so it is a candidate too. Only a version matching `\d+\.\d+\.\d+([-+][0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?` is a candidate, because a bump writes it into the repository.
  - `advisories.target(data, collected)` returns `(dependency, reason)` from a match's exposed or unknown pins and the pins they were collected from:
    - for a runtime, `{ecosystem: runtime, name, version, edits: [{source, field, from, to}]}`. There is one edit per collected pin of that runtime at an exposed version. A Dockerfile edit goes from `{image}:{tag}` to `{image}:{tag with the version replaced}`, and the new tag must match `[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,127}`. A `.nvmrc`, `engines` or `platform` edit goes from the version to the fixed version;
    - for direct packages, `{ecosystem, names, version, versions: {name: version}}`;
    - otherwise `(None, no_fixed_version)`, `(None, not_direct)` when only a lockfile names the package, or `(None, None)` when nothing is exposed.
  - `remediation.runtime_files(files, edits)` changes:
    - the exact image token on `FROM` lines (any case, any options before it), dropping a digest after it because the digest names the old image;
    - the first non-comment line of `.nvmrc`, keeping its `v`;
    - `engines.node` (indent 2) and `config.platform.php` (indent 4), written as JSON.
    A source that no longer holds `from` raises 409 `pin_changed`. `remediation.branch_name(ref, action)` keeps `ref[:7]` for a hex ref and turns any other ref into a lowercase `[a-z0-9-]` slug, so the branch is `asset-monitor/cve-2026-111497-bump`.
  - `domain.attach_advisory`: a match that opens a Finding, on an Application with an Own repository and a target, calls `draft_remediation(…, subject=vulnerability, "bump", own.cursor, automatic=True, dependency=target)`. That queues the `remediation_draft` job, which is application code with no agent and no cap. An attached match drafts nothing.
  - `domain.advisory_dependency(db, finding)` returns the target of the match that opened the advisory Finding (`data.subject_id == vulnerability`). `remediation_actions` is `[bump]` for such a Finding when the Application has an Own repository and a target, else `[]`. In the advisory cell, the `remediation_pr` step reads `no_own_repository`, else the draft's status, else `no_fixed_version` or `not_direct`, else `not_drafted`.
  - `domain.remediate_advisories(db, application, pins, revision)`:
    - The Advisory check calls it after its fence and before `record_advisory_matches`, only when the repository it read is the Own repository (A11: "a version bump seen on the Own repository's default branch").
    - For each Open or Confirmed advisory Finding joined to the match that opened it, every stored pin's runtime or package must have collected pins, and each must be readable and `not_exposed` against the stored ranges. Then `transition_finding(…, "remediated", {by: version_bump, advisory_id, revision, versions: {name: [version]}})` runs and the match reads `not_exposed`.
    - A runtime or package no longer collected, or an unreadable pin of it, leaves the Finding as it is. `version_bump` was already a Remediated cause, so no cause was added.
  - `Workflows.remediation_draft`:
    - For a `runtime` dependency it takes the head files the edits name, applies `runtime_files` and `tree_diff`, and summarises the result as `Bumps node to 20.19.5 (Dockerfile, .nvmrc, package.json)`.
    - On the package path each name gets its own version from `versions`, falling back to `version`, with one npm registry lookup per name. The summary groups names by version, so Phase 7's `Bumps react-dom, react-server-dom-webpack to 19.0.1` is unchanged.
    - Both paths go through `draft_ready`, so `apply_diff` validates every diff.
  - `Workflows.remediation_pr`: a subject that is not a Commit gets the title `Asset Monitor bump for {subject}` and `branch_name(subject, action)`. Commit subjects keep `Asset Monitor {action} of {short sha}`.
  - `POST /findings/{id}/remediation-prs` for an advisory Finding drafts `bump` with the match's subject and target, on the Own repository's cursor. Without an Own repository or a target it answers 422 `remediation_unavailable`. The message is "No Own repository: Remediation PR steps are skipped", or one naming the fixed-version and direct-dependency rule.
  - Tests in `tests/test_advisories.py`. `Feeds` also serves npm metadata from `stub.npm` at `npm.fixture`, and every other request goes to its fallback. `feeds()` sets `NUMEN_NPM_REGISTRY_URL=http://npm.fixture`, and `HOSTS` adds `npm.fixture`:
    - `test_lowest_fixed_versions_and_runtime_edits` (unit): 20.11.1 gives 20.19.5 and 22.1.0 gives 22.12.0. A bound that another feed's range still holds is skipped, and a bound that is not a plain version is never a candidate. `runtime_files` replaces the image token after `--platform` and drops its digest, leaves a comment line alone and bumps `config.platform.php`, and `.nvmrc` holding another version raises `pin_changed`. The branch names are checked too.
    - `test_an_exposed_node_pin_drafts_a_dockerfile_bump_to_the_lowest_fixed_tag` (A06): the Own head holds a two-stage `FROM node:20.11.1-alpine`, `.nvmrc v20.11.1` and `engines.node 20.11.1`. The ready draft has subject `CVE-2026-111497` and the edits to 20.19.5 (not 22.12.0). Its diff equals `tree_diff` of the files with 20.11.1 replaced by 20.19.5 and is re-applied by `apply_diff`. The playbook is `[report ready, remediation_pr ready]` with `remediation_actions` `[bump]`. The jobs are only `advisory_check`, `notification` and `remediation_draft`, the broker gets no call, and npm gets no request.
    - `test_an_exposed_package_drafts_a_bump_to_its_lowest_fixed_version` (A06, advisory-only React2Shell): `react-server-dom-webpack` 19.0.0 bumps to 19.0.1 in `package.json` and the `package-lock.json` entry, with the npm fixture's `resolved` and `integrity`. That is exactly `dependency_files`' output, and the only npm request is `/react-server-dom-webpack/19.0.1`. A transitive-only `scheduler` advisory reads `not_direct`, and the `~> 19.0` react advisory reads `no_fixed_version`. Neither drafts, and a manual bump of either answers 422.
    - `test_approving_the_advisory_bump_opens_one_draft_pull_request` (A08, with `FakeGitHub` as the Feeds fallback): approving twice gives one job, and approving again after it ran returns the same job. There is one `POST /pulls`, on the draft branch `asset-monitor/cve-2026-111497-bump`, titled `Asset Monitor bump for CVE-2026-111497`, whose tree holds `FROM node:20.19.5-alpine`. A merged PR seen by the next poll remediates the Finding through the existing `remediation_pr` path.
    - `test_without_an_own_repository_the_advisory_bump_is_skipped` (A06): the step reads `no_own_repository`, `remediation_actions` is `[]`, no `remediation_draft` job exists, and a manual bump gets 422 with "No Own repository: Remediation PR steps are skipped".
    - `test_a_bump_to_a_fixed_version_seen_on_the_default_branch_remediates_the_finding` (AC-A09 bullet 3, A11): Shop is `react_fix`'s, with the fix commit analysed to address the GHSA id, and `test_dependencies.serve` serves its Own repository. A pushed commit pins `node:20.11.1-alpine`, `react-server-dom-webpack` 19.0.0 and `left-pad` 1.3.0. Its check opens the node Finding, attaches React2Shell to the commit Finding (Confirmed) and opens a left-pad Finding, which is then Dismissed. A second pushed commit moves them to `node:20.19.5-alpine`, 19.0.1 and 1.3.1. The pin-file trigger queues `advisory:{app}:{sha}`, and the node Finding becomes Remediated with cause `{by: version_bump, advisory_id: CVE-2026-111497, revision: <sha>, versions: {node: [20.19.5]}}`. The attached commit Finding and the Dismissed advisory Finding keep their status and cause.
    - The existing tests are unchanged apart from the `Feeds` npm route. The React2Shell `advisory_first` case now also drafts a bump. Its npm lookup is answered 404 in-process, so that draft fails, which the test does not look at.
  - Deviations:
    - The automatic and manual bump uses `own.cursor` as its revision, as the brief says. `remediation_draft` drafts on the Own repository's cursor as before, and the PR title and branch use the subject, not that revision.
    - Remediation by a bump runs only when the check read the Own repository. An Application with no Own repository keeps its advisory Finding until the Operator marks it, because A11 names only the Own repository's default branch.
    - A runtime target moves every exposed pin of that runtime to the lowest fixed version above the highest exposed one (`ponytail:`), and a match with both runtime and package pins bumps only the runtime (`ponytail:`).
    - `target` takes the collected pins as a second argument (the brief's `target(match_data)`), because the Dockerfile image and tag, the pin's field and the `direct` flag are on the collected pins, not on the match.
    - `pin_changed` is 409, as an input that changed since the check.
  - Not done here (T5): the UI for the draft and the new step statuses (`no_fixed_version`, `not_direct`), and the README and implementation contract. Those cover the `remediation_actions` and `remediation_pr` step values for advisory Findings, the branch slug and title, and the `version_bump` cause's fields.

- **Phase 8 summary.**
  - Tests by acceptance criterion. Backend tests serve every feed from the in-process `Feeds` stub (`osv.fixture`, `nvd.fixture`, `github.fixture`, `npm.fixture`) and assert that no other host was asked. The journey uses the compose fixture server. No test reaches osv.dev, api.github.com or nvd.nist.gov.
    - AC-A09 bullet 1 (a Dockerfile pinning a Node version exposed to CVE-2026-111497 opens exactly one Finding across all three feeds, and the next day's check opens no duplicate):
      - `test_advisories.py::test_a_dockerfile_pinning_an_exposed_node_version_opens_exactly_one_finding_across_three_feeds`;
      - `test_osv_ghsa_and_nvd_aliases_key_one_match`;
      - `test_the_next_days_check_keeps_one_match_and_a_later_alias_merges_into_it`;
      - the journey's "PASS the Advisory check opens exactly one advisory Finding for a Dockerfile node pin across OSV, GHSA and NVD, and a second check opens none".
    - AC-A09 bullet 2 (the React2Shell package advisory and the exposed React2Shell fix commit share one Finding): `test_the_react2shell_package_advisory_and_the_exposed_fix_commit_share_one_finding[commit_first]` and `[advisory_first]`.
    - AC-A09 bullet 3 (a bump to a fixed version, once seen on the default branch, moves the Finding to Remediated): `test_a_bump_to_a_fixed_version_seen_on_the_default_branch_remediates_the_finding`.
    - AC-A09 bullet 4 (a runtime version that cannot be read is reported as unknown, not as not exposed):
      - `test_an_unreadable_runtime_version_is_unknown_not_not_exposed`, with six cases: `node:lts`, `node:20`, a variable, no tag, `.nvmrc` `lts/*` and an engines range;
      - `test_a_version_outside_every_range_is_not_exposed_and_an_unreadable_range_is_unknown`;
      - in the UI, `ApplicationAdvisories.integration.test.tsx`.
    - The fifth bullet the plan lists ("ten checks of an unchanged page after an Unrelated Verdict start no new Triage run") is AC-A10's, served content, and belongs to Phase 9.
    - A06, the Advisory check paragraph:
      - the report: `test_an_advisory_finding_runs_only_its_cell`;
      - an Alert only when the match opens a Finding: bullet 1's test and the React2Shell `commit_first` case, where attaching sends none;
      - a bump Remediation PR after Approval: `test_an_exposed_node_pin_drafts_a_dockerfile_bump_to_the_lowest_fixed_tag`, `test_an_exposed_package_drafts_a_bump_to_its_lowest_fixed_version`, `test_approving_the_advisory_bump_opens_one_draft_pull_request`;
      - no Lab Proof: `test_an_advisory_finding_runs_only_its_cell` and the journey;
      - without an Own repository the step is skipped: `test_without_an_own_repository_the_advisory_bump_is_skipped`.
