    - A09 collection and triggers: `test_runtime_pins_are_read_from_dockerfile_nvmrc_engines_and_composer_platform`, `test_package_versions_come_from_lockfiles_and_locked_versions`, `test_a_pin_file_change_on_the_own_default_branch_queues_an_advisory_check`, `test_the_advisory_check_runs_once_per_utc_day`, `test_run_advisory_check_endpoint`, and `test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_advisory_check_tables`.
    - A09 matching: `test_ranges_use_each_ecosystems_version_semantics`, `test_feed_responses_are_cached`, `test_a_feed_that_fails_is_recorded_and_the_others_still_match`, `test_lowest_fixed_versions_and_runtime_edits`.
    - The UI: `ApplicationAdvisories.integration.test.tsx`, the advisory test in `Findings.integration.test.tsx`, and `Jobs.integration.test.tsx` "summarizes an Advisory check".
  - Deviations (the planner's, and each task's above):
    - The change trigger fires only for pin-file changes on the Own repository's default branch, after its Baseline. An Application with no Own repository gets the daily and the manual check only. Daily scheduling is its own `domain.schedule_advisory_checks`.
    - An advisory Remediation PR's branch is `asset-monitor/<vulnerability-slug>-bump` (for example `asset-monitor/cve-2026-111497-bump`), not A08's `<short-sha>-bump`, and its title is `Asset Monitor bump for <id>`.
    - A runtime bump's base-image tag is the current tag with the fixed version in place of the old one (`node:20.11.1-alpine` becomes `node:20.19.5-alpine`). Its existence is not checked against a registry; the Operator reviews the diff at Approval.
    - GHSA has no runtime ecosystem. For a runtime pin it is asked by `cve_id` and adds aliases, summary and severity, but no ranges, so "across all three feeds" holds for the vulnerability's identity, while a runtime's ranges come from OSV and NVD.
    - A pin whose version cannot be read is never queried. It reads unknown, counting as exposed, and opens no Finding of its own. A readable pin's match is unknown, and opens a Finding, only when a range cannot be evaluated.
    - Attaching to an Open commit or cve Finding also Confirms it. An attached match drafts no bump, and `version_bump` remediates only the advisory Findings their match opened. A commit Finding's aliases come only from its source Analysis's `addresses`.
    - Remediation by a bump runs only when the check read the Own repository. Without one, an advisory Finding stays until the Operator marks it.
    - `advisories.target` takes the collected pins as a second argument, and `pin_changed` is 409.
  - Known ceilings, each marked `ponytail:` in the code:
    - two keys joined by a later alias fold into the oldest row, and the other row's Finding is left as it is;
    - a removed pin never remediates;
    - deleting an advisory Finding frees its match, so the next check reopens it while it is exposed (Dismiss is the supported path);
    - a transitive package is matched but not bumped (`not_direct`);
    - composer versions are numeric tuples (stability suffixes read as unknown), and npm prerelease rules are not modelled;
    - a match with runtime and package pins bumps only the runtime;
    - GitHub keeps only the first page's ETag;
    - concurrent checks keep the pins of the one that finishes last;
    - an Analysis that commits during a check can open its commit Finding beside the advisory one;
    - the pins list is not paginated.
  - Not checked against the live APIs (no test may reach them): whether NVD's `cpeName` needs an entry in the CPE dictionary (`virtualMatchString` is the alternative), how far OSV covers runtimes through the `Bitnami` ecosystem, whether GHSA's `type` defaults to reviewed with `cve_id` or `affects`, and whether OSV wants composer versions without the leading `v`.
  - Runs after T5 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to osv.dev, api.github.com, nvd.nist.gov, a registry or any other outside host:
    - the focused UI run (`ApplicationAdvisories`, `Findings` and `Jobs` integration tests): 53 passed;
    - `make check` passed, before and after the last two frontend edits (the Findings list badge and a description string);
    - `make test`: unit 246 passed and runner 138 passed, 66 skipped (the Engine tests); integration 543 passed against PostgreSQL; runtime 203 passed, 1 skipped on the real Engine; UI 147 passed in 20 files;
    - `make test-journey` (which runs `make build` first) passed twice. Every step printed PASS, including "PASS the Advisory check opens exactly one advisory Finding for a Dockerfile node pin across OSV, GHSA and NVD, and a second check opens none". The second run started with the `advisory_cache` rows the first run had stored in the journey database, and in both runs every feed request in the fixture's log was one of its four feed routes;
    - T5 changes no backend code, so the outside-traffic hook of T1 to T4 was not run again.

### Phase 8 review fixes (2026-11-14)

Each fix has its regression test in `backend/tests/test_advisories.py`.

- **An advisory Finding could never get the n-day Proof that A16 step 4 asks for.** The daily check runs once the Own repository has a head, so in the demo the React2Shell advisory opens its Finding before the Backfill reaches the fix commit. The fix then only linked to that Finding: `run_playbook` returned None for a linked Vulnerability fix, and `require_lab` refused every Lab on an advisory Finding. Now an exposed (or unknown) Upstream Vulnerability fix that links to an Open or Confirmed advisory Finding runs its Upstream Playbook on that Finding. The Finding records `fix_commit_id`, which `domain.source_commit` reads, so `finding_cell` gives `("upstream", "Vulnerability fix")` (`proof`, `remediation_pr`). The Finding gets the fix's drafted bundle (or `draft_bundle`'s), its Lab after Approval, `exportable: false` and the job's `finding_ids`. It keeps the advisory bump: `remediation_step` drafts no Upstream bump for a Finding that is not a commit Finding, and `playbook_steps` reads an advisory Finding's `remediation_pr` from its own draft. `test_the_react2shell_package_advisory_and_the_exposed_fix_commit_share_one_finding` now checks, in both orders, the fix's `finding_ids`, the steps `proof` and `remediation_pr`, `exportable: false`, and a Lab request that is refused only as `incomplete_bundle` (422), never `lab_not_allowed`.
- **No change trigger without an Own repository.** `Workflows.queue_derivation` now queues `advisory:{app}:{head}` for each unarchived Application whose `advisory_repository` is the polled repository, when an imported commit touches a pin file. That is the Own repository, else the source repository, as PRD A09 and the brief ask. The deviation that the contract recorded is removed. `test_a_pin_file_change_on_the_own_default_branch_queues_an_advisory_check` adds a Blog Application with no Own repository: a `.nvmrc` push on its source repository queues and runs its check. No other backend test needed a change.
- **GHSA's `first_patched_version` was dropped beside a `<=` bound.** With `<= 1.2.3` and `1.2.4`, `lowest_fixed` had no `<` candidate, so the bump step read `no_fixed_version`. `ghsa_ranges` now appends `["<", patched]` unless the range has a `<` bound, or a `<=` bound that the patched version is not above (that would narrow the range). `test_ranges_use_each_ecosystems_version_semantics` covers both cases and `lowest_fixed`.
- **Remediation judged the bump against stale ranges.** `advisory_check` now calls `remediate_advisories` after `record_advisory_matches`, so the decision uses each row's refreshed pins and the feeds' latest ranges. The requirement that every collected pin of each runtime or package be readable and outside stays (`domain.bumped`). The line that set the row's state is dead and is removed: the refresh already reads `not_exposed`. New test: `test_a_bump_short_of_a_widened_range_leaves_the_finding_as_it_is`.
- **A later alias could orphan a Finding or open a second one.** When rows fold, the kept row is now the oldest that has a Finding, else the oldest, so its key and `subject_id` still match. The other rows are deleted only when they have no Finding or the same one. A row with a different Finding stays beside it (`ponytail:`, now a known ceiling in place of the orphaned Finding). New test: `test_a_later_alias_that_joins_two_keys_keeps_the_finding_of_one`. A not-exposed key, then an exposed key with a Finding, are joined by an OSV alias: one row is left, with the Finding, and there is no second Finding or Alert.
- **A bump left attached commit and cve Findings as they were.** `remediate_advisories` now takes every Open or Confirmed Finding that rows point to: the advisory Finding a row opened, or the commit or cve Finding a row attached to. It moves the Finding to Remediated (`version_bump`, `advisory_id` the first row's vulnerability) when all of its rows are bumped (A11). In `test_a_bump_to_a_fixed_version_seen_on_the_default_branch_remediates_the_finding`, Shop's React link now reads the lockfile's version. The bump to 19.0.1 remediates the shared React2Shell commit Finding with `versions: {react-server-dom-webpack: [19.0.1]}`. The left-pad advisory Finding that the Operator Remediated keeps its cause.
- **An exposed match left its own Finding Open.** `attach_advisory` now Confirms a Finding that an exposed match opens, with the same cause (A11: "an Advisory check match"). An unknown match leaves its Finding Open. Dismiss is from Open only, so tests that dismissed an exposed advisory Finding now close it as Remediated by the Operator. `test_a_dockerfile_pinning_an_exposed_node_version_opens_exactly_one_finding_across_three_feeds` checks `confirmed`, and that an Operator-Remediated Finding stays Remediated on the next check.
- Docs: the implementation contract (trigger, GHSA bound, fold, Confirm, the n-day Proof on an advisory Finding, remediation of attached Findings, deviations and ceilings) and README step 6 are updated. The Phase 8 summary above still describes the behaviour before these fixes; this entry supersedes the parts it contradicts.
- Runs on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB). The Docker VM's disk was full: 170 MB free of 118 GB, nearly all of it other projects' images and a build cache that the label-filtered prune cannot reach. No request went to osv.dev, api.github.com, nvd.nist.gov or any other outside host.
  - `make build` passed.
  - `make check` passed.
  - `make test-unit` passed: 246 passed; runner 138 passed, 66 skipped.
  - `make test-integration` (PostgreSQL) gave 511 passed and 34 errors. Every error is a `tests/test_workflows.py` setup that failed with pytest's `OSError: could not create numbered dir … after 10 tries`, because the disk is full. `tests/test_workflows.py` alone against the same PostgreSQL passed 49 of 49. The whole backend suite in five SQLite groups passed 520 with 25 skipped (the PostgreSQL-only tests).
  - `make test-runtime` passed: 203 passed, 1 skipped.
  - `make test-ui` passed: 147 passed in 20 files.
  - `make test-journey` passed, with every step PASS. Its stack was then stopped with `down`.

### Phase 8 verification fix (2026-11-14)

- **`make test-integration` filled the Docker VM's disk.** pytest keeps every test's `tmp_path` until the session ends, and the full PostgreSQL run needs more than the 178 MB the VM had left, so `tests/test_workflows.py` failed late in the run with `No space left on device`. The `backend-tests` service now sets `PYTEST_ADDOPTS: -o tmp_path_retention_policy=failed` in `compose.yaml`, so a passing test's `tmp_path` is removed at its teardown and a failing test's is kept for debugging. Set there, it applies to every backend entry point without rebuilding the image. During the run, the test container's `/tmp` stayed at 2 MB or less, and the VM never had less than 169 MB free.
- Runs on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to an outside host:
  - `make build` passed.
  - `make check` passed.
  - `make test-unit` passed: 246 passed; runner 138 passed, 66 skipped.
  - `make test-integration` (PostgreSQL) passed: 545 passed in one run.
  - `make test-runtime` passed: 203 passed, 1 skipped.
  - `make test-ui` passed: 147 passed in 20 files.
  - `make test-journey` passed, with every step PASS, including the Advisory check step. Its stack was then stopped with `down`.

## Phase 9: Served-content checks (2026-11-14)

Phase 9 (A10 and AC-A10, including AC-A09's "ten checks of an unchanged page after an Unrelated Verdict start no new Triage run", which Phase 8 left here; the Served content column of the A06 Playbook matrix and the Upstream Malicious change sweep; the A07 Served content Detection rule validation; the served_content Finding source of A11 with remediation when a later check matches the Baseline again). This section records each task as it lands.

What each task changed:
- **T1, the fetch boundary and Content fingerprint primitives.** No database change.
  - `runner/numen_runner/gateway.py`:
    - `USER_AGENTS` is the fixed pair: `browser` (a desktop Chrome 131 user agent) and `googlebot` (`Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; Googlebot/2.1; +http://www.google.com/bot.html)`). `TargetFetcher.fetch(url, method, user_agent=None)` sends the named one on every redirect hop. Any other name raises "target user agent denied". None keeps `Numen-version-fetch/1`, so version recipes are unchanged. `do_POST` passes the request's `user_agent`.
    - The policy key `served_content: true` skips the origin list, so any http(s) origin is allowed. `origin()` still refuses other schemes, credentials in the URL and fragments, and the method stays GET or HEAD.
    - `resolve()` unwraps an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address and refuses any address that is not `is_global`, or is multicast (`is_global` alone lets 224.0.0.0/4 and ff00::/8 through). This covers private, loopback, link-local, multicast, reserved, unspecified and shared addresses, for every policy and the provider CONNECT as well. `allowed_private_cidrs` is the only exception, and `blocked_cidrs` still wins. The per-hop re-resolve and `PinnedHTTP` (connect to the checked address, verify TLS by hostname) are unchanged.
  - `runner/numen_runner/fetch.py`: `fetch(url, method, user_agent=None)` adds `user_agent` to the body when given.
  - `runner/numen_runner/engine.py`: `served_fixture_cidrs(client)` returns the IPAM subnets of the Docker network that `NUMEN_SERVED_CONTENT_FIXTURE_NETWORK` names, else `[]` (unset, empty, or a network that does not exist). For a `target_policy` with `served_content`, `run()` replaces `allowed_private_cidrs` with that list, whatever the request carried, and `start_gateway(..., extra_network)` connects the run's gateway to that network before it starts. `blocked_cidrs` (the `_application`/`_control` subnets) is computed as before. `compose.yaml` passes the setting to `runner-broker`, empty by default, with a comment that it is for the demo VM only.
  - `backend/numen/served_probe.py`, staged as `recipe.py` like `fingerprint_probe.py` (standard library only):
    - `Page(HTMLParser)` collects script srcs in order, inline scripts, `a[href]`, visible text (outside script, style, noscript and template), and token values: a meta `csrf-token`/`csrf_token`/`_csrf` content, a hidden input's value whose name matches `csrf|_token|authenticity_token`, and every `nonce` attribute. A repeated attribute is read as a browser reads it (the first wins).
    - `collect(fetch, target)` takes `{url, user_agents, max_scripts, max_bytes}`. For each user agent it GETs the page and, on 200, resolves its script srcs against the final URL (http(s) only, each once, a src that is no valid URL skipped) and fetches the first `max_scripts` with the same user agent. The rest are listed with status `skipped` and error `limit`. A response whose new bytes would take the stored ones past `max_bytes` is dropped and listed as `skipped` with error `budget`, and that user agent fetches no further script, so the stored bytes never pass the budget. A failed request is listed with its exception's type name. Nothing else is ever requested: no link, image or preload, and no URL a script names. It returns `({"fetches": {ua: {url, final_url, status, sha256, content_type, error, scripts: [...]}}}, {sha256: bytes})`.
    - `main()` reads `NUMEN_TARGET_INPUT`, fetches through `numen_runner.fetch.fetch`, writes each body to `/workspace/outputs/<sha256>` and prints the result.
  - `backend/numen/served.py`, pure and standard library only (hekata is ported, not imported):
    - `family()` with `bare`, `HEX_RE`, `BUILD_RE`, `HASH_RE` and `_hashlike`, from hekata's `monitor/gather.py`.
    - `normalise_js(text)`: jsdiff.py `_read`'s normalised token stream (its `TOKEN_RE`, `REGEX_RE`, `TEMPLATE_RE`, `KEYWORDS`, `REGEX_AFTER` and `REGEX_BUDGET`), without lines, widths or secrets. Spaces and comments are dropped, an identifier of 1-3 characters (no keyword, not after `.` or `?.`) reads as `_`, and a number after `_(` reads as `0`.
    - `summary(html_bytes, final_url, scripts)`, where scripts is `[(url, bytes or None, status label)]`. Every token value of 8 characters or more is first replaced by `*` in the inline scripts, hrefs and text. It returns sorted, deduplicated lists: `script_hosts` (hosts other than the page's), `scripts` (`family#sha256(normalised)`, or `family!label` for a script with no bytes), `inline_scripts` (the sha256 of each normalised inline script, and of each src that is no http(s) URL, such as a `data:` URL, which is never fetched), `links` (bare resolved hrefs, the raw text when it is no valid URL), `link_hosts`, and `words` (lowercased words of 3 or more letters or digits from the visible text, with digit runs collapsed to `0`, capped at `WORD_CAP` 5000 and marked `ponytail:`).
    - `summaries(result, files)` gives each user agent's summary of a `collect` result, or None for a user agent that was not served the page with 200. `fingerprint(summary)` is the sha256 of the canonical JSON. `diff(before, after)` is `{key: {added, removed}}` for the keys that differ; with no before, everything is added.
  - Tests:
    - `backend/tests/test_served_fingerprints.py` (in-process pages through `served_probe.collect` with a fake gateway fetch; judol links point at `slot-gacor.test`, and the ClickFix prompt's clipboard command is `echo asset-monitor-fixture`):
      - `test_gambling_links_shown_only_to_googlebot_change_only_its_fingerprint` (AC-A10 bullet 1): the browser summary and fingerprint equal the clean ones despite rotated tokens. Only Googlebot's changes, and its diff adds `link_hosts` `slot-gacor.test`, the two links and the Indonesian words, and nothing else.
      - `test_an_added_external_script_host_changes_the_fingerprint` (bullet 2): a fake "Verify you are human" prompt and a loader from `cdn.clickfix-fixture.test` change both user agents' fingerprints and add that `script_hosts` entry.
      - `test_rotating_nonces_csrf_tokens_and_bundler_hashes_do_not_change_the_fingerprint` (bullet 3): meta csrf-token, hidden `_token` and `authenticity_token`, nonce attributes, the token in an inline script and a `?_token=` link, `app.3f9a1c2b.js` vs `app.9e8d7c6b.js`, and renamed minified identifiers and module ids. All four summaries are equal. An `eval(atob(p))` added to the bundle, or a fetch added to the inline script, still changes it.
      - `test_family_and_normalised_scripts_follow_hekata`.
      - `test_the_probe_never_runs_a_script` (bullet 4, probe level): a script and an inline script that would request `/served/executed` if run, plus a link, an image and a preload to it. Only the page and the script src are requested, as each user agent.
      - `test_the_probe_fetches_the_page_and_at_most_20_linked_scripts_as_each_user_agent`: 24 unique srcs (same-origin, resolved against the redirect's final URL, and external), a duplicate, a `data:` src and an empty one. Each user agent requests the page and the first 20; the other 4 are `skipped`/`limit`. One refused request records `RuntimeError` and a missing one 404. Files are keyed by their sha256. With a small `max_bytes`, the scripts past the budget are `skipped`/`budget` and the stored bytes stay within it.
      - `test_short_or_empty_token_values_are_not_masked` (2 cases) and `test_a_page_with_invalid_urls_is_still_summarised`.
    - `runner/tests/test_gateway.py`:
      - `test_served_content_policy_allows_any_public_origin_and_refuses_non_public_addresses`: getaddrinfo patched for 10.0.0.1, 127.0.0.1, 169.254.169.254, 224.0.0.1, 240.0.0.1, 0.0.0.0, ::1, fe80::1, ff02::1 and ::ffff:127.0.0.1, each refused before any connection. ftp, userinfo, a fragment and POST are refused too.
      - `test_served_content_private_range_is_only_the_allowed_one_and_blocked_still_wins`, `test_a_redirect_to_a_private_address_is_refused` (a 302 to `http://10.0.0.1/`), `test_the_connection_is_pinned_to_the_resolved_address` (the socket goes to the checked address and TLS gets the hostname), `test_the_user_agent_is_one_of_the_fixed_pair` (sent on the redirect hop too, an unknown name refused, the default unchanged), `test_version_recipe_policy_stays_origin_scoped` (an off-origin redirect is still denied) and `test_served_fixture_cidrs_are_empty_unless_the_broker_names_a_network` (a fake client).
    - `runner/tests/test_docker_runtime.py::test_served_content_gateway_reaches_a_private_fixture_only_on_the_named_network`: a fixture on its own internal network echoes the User-Agent. A real `probe` run with `served_content` and the fixture's subnet in its own `allowed_private_cidrs` gets 403 with the setting empty: the gateway config's list is `[]` and the gateway is not on the network. With the setting naming the network, the fetch is 200 and echoes the Googlebot user agent, and the gateway's own address on the job network (private, outside that subnet, and it would answer) is still 403.
  - Docs: runner/README.md (Boundary) and the implementation contract (`target_policy` and the gateway's fetch body) describe the policy, the pair, the refused address classes and the demo setting.
  - Deviations:
    - The demo range is a Docker network name, not a CIDR, as the plan's risks note: the run's gateway sits on its own bridge and has to join the fixture's network to reach it.
    - Token values shorter than 8 characters are not masked (`served.TOKEN_MIN`), so a short or empty value cannot blank out words; a short rotating token would change the fingerprint instead.
    - `served.summaries` (one `collect` result to each user agent's summary) and `served_probe.join`/`script_urls` are additions that the probe, the summary and the tests share. A src that is no http(s) URL counts as an inline script, so an injected `data:` script still changes the fingerprint.
  - Limits: Cloudflare-style bot-challenge inline scripts that carry per-request values, and bot-protection script URLs (hekata's `BOT_SCRIPTS`), are not normalised, so such a page may change its fingerprint on every check until the Operator accepts one; add normalisation if the demo or a real Asset shows it. The word cap is marked `ponytail:`.
  - Runs after T1 on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB). The VM had about 69 MB free after the gateway and probe rebuilds and `make prune`; the rest is other projects' images and a build cache the label-filtered prune cannot reach. No test reached a real Asset or an outside host.
    - the focused runs: runner `tests/test_gateway.py` 12 passed; backend `tests/test_served_fingerprints.py` 9 passed; `./scripts/compose.sh build fetch-gateway`, `--profile images build probe-image` and `make prune`; runtime `-k served_content` 1 passed;
    - `make check` passed;
    - `make test-unit`: 246 passed, and runner 145 passed, 67 skipped (the Engine tests);
    - `make test-integration` (PostgreSQL): 554 passed. A guard sampled the VM's free disk every 5 s; it never went below 60 MB;
    - `make test-runtime`: 205 passed, 1 skipped, and 6 failed. All 6 are `tests/test_session_history_docker.py::test_full_native_history_survives_container_stop_and_private_archive_transport`, whose workload hit `No space left on device` while writing its 4 MiB session history to a volume, because the VM's disk ran out at the suite's peak. Run alone right after, that file passed 6 of 6, with the VM's free disk at 47 MB at its lowest. The new runtime test passed in the full run;
    - UI and the journey are unchanged by T1 and were not re-run.
- **T2, Watched pages, Served-content checks, stored evidence and accepting the first Baseline.** Migration `e5f6a7b8c9d0` (after `d3f4a5b6c7e8`) only adds: the tables `watched_pages`, `served_fetches` (indexed on page, user agent and time) and `served_changes` (unique on page, user agent and fingerprint, with every Phase 9 column, so T3 and T4 add no migration), and a homepage row for each existing Application. The implementation contract has the records, the job and the routes.
  - `backend/numen/domain.py`: `USER_AGENTS`, `MAX_WATCHED_PAGES` (20), `page_origin`, `sync_homepage`, `watched_pages`, `queue_served_check`, `schedule_served_checks`, `record_served_change`, `watched_page_json` and `accept_baseline`. `APPLICATION_SUBJECTS` gains `served_check`, labelled with the Asset URL.
  - `backend/numen/workflows.py`: `SERVED_POLICY`, `SERVED_TARGET`, `served_fetches` (validates the probe result and builds each user agent's fetch), `Workflows.served_check` and `Workflows.served_files`. `execute()` exempts `served_check`, `served_triage`, `served_analysis` and `served_content_sweep` from the source-generation check.
  - `worker.LONG_KINDS` gains `served_check`. `scheduler.main` calls `schedule_served_checks` in its own transaction after `schedule_advisory_checks`; `domain.schedule` is unchanged.
  - `backend/numen/api.py`: the four watched-page routes and `POST /served-content-checks`. `create_application` and `patch_application` call `sync_homepage`. `schemas.py` gains `WatchedPageInput` and `BaselineInput`.
  - Tests, `backend/tests/test_served_content.py`. `served_broker(site)` is a FixtureBroker handler that answers each `probe` call with `served_probe.collect` over an in-memory site and records each (url, user agent) requested; T3 and T4 reuse it. The pages are T1's inert shop, judol and ClickFix stand-ins:
    - `test_the_homepage_is_always_watched_and_cannot_be_removed`: a new Application lists its Asset URL; DELETE gives 409 `homepage_required`; a PATCH of `asset_url` moves the homepage to a page with no Baseline, and the next check fetches only the new URL (a 404 there is a `failed` fetch with `{status: 404}`).
    - `test_watched_pages_must_share_the_asset_origin`: another host, scheme or port gives 422 with `fields.url`; a duplicate (the homepage included) gives 409 `page_watched`; a removed page is re-added with its id; the 21st page gives 422 `too_many_watched_pages`.
    - `test_served_content_checks_run_on_the_interval_and_on_check_now`: creating an Application and `domain.schedule` queue no `served_check`; `schedule_served_checks` queues one per interval window (none for a paused or archived Application, none twice in a window); Check now returns 202, reusing the active check.
    - `test_each_page_is_fetched_as_browser_and_googlebot_through_the_gateway`: one `probe` per page, with no profile, `recipe.py` equal to `served_probe.py`, `SERVED_POLICY` (served content, GET, no origins) and a target naming browser and googlebot. Each page and each of its scripts is requested once as each user agent.
    - `test_fetched_bytes_are_stored_once_by_content_hash`: `served_content` Artifacts keyed by sha256. `served_files` returns `page.html` and `scripts/NN.js`. A second unchanged check adds no Artifact.
    - `test_the_first_fingerprint_becomes_the_baseline_only_when_accepted`: no ServedChange before acceptance, even after a changed check. A wrong `X-CSRF-Token` gives 403 `csrf_required`. A failed fetch, or one of another page, gives 422 `invalid_baseline`. The body has no password. One Action `baseline_accepted` is recorded and re-accepting is a no-op. A changed fingerprint gives 409 `baseline_not_allowed`. The next check records the change against the accepted Baseline.
    - AC-A10 bullets 1 to 3 through the real job: `test_googlebot_only_gambling_links_change_the_googlebot_fingerprint_only` (Googlebot `changed` with `slot-gacor.test` in its diff, browser `matches`; the ServedChange holds the fetch, previous fetch, Baseline fetch and diff, and is recorded once over two checks), `test_an_added_external_script_host_is_a_changed_fingerprint` and `test_rotating_tokens_and_bundler_hashes_keep_matching_the_baseline` (three rebuilt, re-tokened checks match, and no change is recorded).
    - `test_a_check_runs_no_javascript` (AC-A10 bullet 4 at job level): the only broker calls are `probe`, and only the page and its script src are requested, never the `/served/executed` callback that its scripts would request if run.
    - `test_a_probe_result_unlike_the_fixed_probes_fails_the_check`: a result missing a user agent, or outputs not named by their sha256, fail the job with `invalid_probe_result` and store nothing.
    - `backend/tests/test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_served_content_tables`: run twice, an upgrade from `d3f4a5b6c7e8` gives the existing Application its homepage row (`homepage` true, `baseline` `{}`), and `alembic check` is clean. The (page, user agent, fingerprint) and (Application, URL) uniques refuse a second row. A downgrade drops the three tables and keeps the Application.
  - Deviations:
    - `sync_homepage` moves the homepage to a new row instead of rewriting the old row's URL, so fetches of the old URL never count as the new URL's latest or previous fetch. The old homepage is removed and keeps its history. A page already watched at the new URL becomes the homepage and keeps its Baseline, which avoids the (Application, URL) conflict.
    - A Watched page restored after removal keeps its Baseline.
    - Other Watched pages stay put when the Asset URL moves to another origin.
    - The probe stores the bodies of non-200 responses too; a failed fetch keeps them as evidence.
  - Limits: a check holds every page's bytes in memory until its fence (at most 20 x 7 MiB, marked `ponytail:`).
  - Runs after T2. By then the VM's disk had about 50 GB free, freed outside this project. No test reached a real Asset or an outside host.
    - focused, from the source tree (no image rebuild): `tests/test_served_content.py` failed at collection against the T1 code, then passed 11 of 11; the whole backend suite gave 540 passed and 26 skipped (the PostgreSQL tests);
    - `./scripts/compose.sh --profile test run --build --no-deps --rm backend-tests pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_served_content.py`: 11 passed;
    - `make test-integration CASE=job-recovery`: 27 passed;
    - `make check` passed;
    - `make test-unit`: 246 passed, and runner 145 passed, 67 skipped;
    - `make test-integration` (PostgreSQL): 566 passed.
    - Runtime, UI and the journey are unchanged by T2 (no runner or frontend change) and were not re-run.
- **T3, Triage and Analysis of served-content changes (the Security hardening, Unrelated, Insufficient context and not-allowed cells of the A06 Served content column).** No database change: T2's `served_changes` columns hold it. The implementation contract has the jobs, the routes and the Baseline rule.
  - `backend/numen/domain.py`: `queue_served_triage` (called by `record_served_change` in the savepoint that creates the change, so only that check queues its one Triage), `start_served_analysis` and `start_automatic` (the cap, blocked and queued states, now shared with `start_analysis`), `CAPPED_KINDS` gains `served_analysis`, and `start_waiting` starts waiting commits and served-content changes together, oldest first. `accept_baseline` and `watched_page_json`'s `accept_allowed` also allow a fingerprint whose change has a Security hardening or Unrelated Verdict (`BASELINE_VERDICTS`). New `served_change_json` and `served_change_label`. `job_subject` names a `served_change_id` job by page URL and user agent. `exportable` allows a served Analysis.
  - `backend/numen/workflows.py`: `SCHEMAS["served_triage"]` (commit_triage's) and `SCHEMAS["served_analysis"]` (the four served-content Verdicts, no `vulnerable_ref`, `addresses` or `before_after`; the Malicious change indicators schema is now the shared `INDICATORS`). `RUNNER_OPERATIONS` maps the two kinds to the runner's `commit_triage` and `commit_analysis` tool profiles. `validate_result` refuses a code-vulnerability Verdict on `served_analysis` with `verdict_not_allowed`. `triage_cut` and `Workflows.triage_run` are commit_triage's diff cap and reply handling, now shared by `served_triage`. `triage_result` and `set_commit_decision` take a Commit or a ServedChange. `Workflows.served_analysis` reads only stored bytes (`served_files`, with `served_names` naming them) and has no policy.
  - `backend/numen/worker.py`: `LONG_KINDS` gains both kinds. The Triage failure branch retries `served_triage` like commit_triage, and the Analysis failure branch records `failed` or `timed_out` on the change (`subject()` finds a job's Commit or ServedChange).
  - `backend/numen/api.py`: the dashboard Review queue lists Insufficient context served-content changes with a `link` to the Served content tab; `POST /served-changes/{id}/review` and `POST /served-changes/{id}/analyses`; Retry of a `served_analysis` resets its change's `analysis_state`.
  - Prompts: `prompt_templates/operations/served_triage.md` and `served_analysis.md` (with the `rules` section, and `detection-rule` for Analysis). They say the bytes were fetched and never run, list only the four served-content Verdicts and treat the content as untrusted. `prompts.OPERATIONS` and `DESCRIPTIONS` list them; profiles stay keyed by `commit_triage` and `commit_analysis`.
  - Frontend: `types.ts` gives Review queue items an optional `link`, and `Dashboard.tsx` follows it, else the commit page. `App.integration.test.tsx` checks a served entry's link.
  - Tests, `backend/tests/test_served_playbooks.py` (on T2's `served_broker`, which now also answers agent calls):
    - `test_ten_checks_of_an_unchanged_page_after_an_unrelated_verdict_start_no_new_triage` (AC-A10 bullet 5): 12 `served_check`, one `served_triage` and one `served_analysis` job, and one ServedChange over ten re-checks of the judol page. Accept Baseline then returns 200, its Action's `previous` is the old Baseline, and the next check reads `matches`.
    - `test_each_distinct_fingerprint_is_triaged_at_most_once`: flapping B, A, B, A, B triages B once; the same fingerprint served to the browser is that user agent's own change, triaged separately; one Transcript per Triage.
    - `test_analysis_reads_only_the_stored_bytes_of_both_user_agents`: broker calls are probe, agent, agent, with only the one check's six requests. Triage runs as `commit_triage` with no files, no policy, 120 s and the diff in its facts. Analysis runs as `commit_analysis` with policy None and 1800 s. Its files equal `served_files` of both changed fetches and both Baseline fetches plus `served-content.json`. The Analysis is stored and exportable, and the job subject names the page and Googlebot.
    - `test_each_served_content_verdict_runs_only_its_cell[Security hardening|Unrelated|Insufficient context]` (A06 Served content column, AC-A06, AC-A12): no Finding, no Notification, job kinds only `served_check`, `served_triage` and `served_analysis`. Accept Baseline gives 200 for Security hardening and Unrelated. Insufficient context gives 409 and lists the change in the dashboard Review queue; a review decision removes it and records an Action.
    - `test_a_served_content_analysis_that_returns_a_code_vulnerability_verdict_fails[Vulnerability fix|Vulnerability introduced]` (AC-A04, A06 "Not allowed"): the job fails with `verdict_not_allowed`, `analysis_state` is failed, there is no Verdict, Analysis or Finding, and Retry queues it again.
    - `test_triage_off_records_the_change_and_manual_analysis_still_works`: Triage off records the change as `disabled` with no Triage job, and Accept Baseline gives 409 until a manual Analysis returns Unrelated. Jev Triage blocks both changes with `jev_triage_unsupported`.
    - `test_a_failed_served_triage_is_retried_never_read_as_unrelated` (AC-A03): a reply failing the schema fails the job and leaves the change `failed` and `pending` with no Analysis. It queues a `served_triage` retry a minute later, which then completes.
    - `test_automatic_served_analysis_waits_at_a_cap_and_manual_analysis_still_works` (AC-A04): at the Application cap the change waits, and manual Analysis completes. A second waiting change starts after the reset through `domain.schedule` with `{served_change_id, automatic: true, shared: false}`.
    - `tests/test_prompts.py`: `detection-rule` is used by `commit_analysis` and `served_analysis`.
  - Deviations:
    - A Vulnerability fix or Vulnerability introduced served Verdict is refused in `validate_result`, before the schema check. The schema offered to the agent lists only the four served-content Verdicts, so the schema check alone would have failed it as `invalid_agent_output`.
    - A served-content entry in the Review queue fills `committed_at` with the time the change was first served. The field keeps the existing item shape and orders the queue.
    - Analysis gets the change's own `baseline_fetch_id` for its user agent and the page's current Baseline for the other user agent.
    - Jev mode blocks the change with the code `jev_triage_unsupported` (the plan named no code).
  - Limits:
    - Accept Baseline needs an Analysis Verdict, as A10 says. A Triage `unrelated` decision, or the Operator dismissing an Insufficient context change in the Review queue, does not allow a new Baseline. The page then stays `changed` until a manual Analysis returns Security hardening or Unrelated.
    - A Malicious change only stores its Verdict and the `candidate` review decision; T4 adds its Finding.
  - Runs after T3. No test reached a real Asset or an outside host.

    - `./scripts/compose.sh --profile test run --build --no-deps --rm backend-tests pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_served_playbooks.py tests/test_served_content.py tests/test_prompts.py tests/test_triage.py tests/test_analysis.py`: 71 passed;

- **T4, Malicious change in served content: static Proof, Detection rule validation, remediation by Baseline match, and the Upstream sweep.** No database change: the Finding's `data` holds it. The implementation contract has the details.
  - `backend/numen/served.py`: `check_indicators(changed, baseline, indicators)`, in `detection.check_indicators`' shape. A file is kept when it is a `source/changed/` path whose bytes no `source/baseline/` file holds, hashed here. A host, URL or command is kept when it occurs in the changed fetches' bytes and in none of the Baseline fetches'. The rest goes to `rejected` with its reason.
  - `backend/numen/detection.py`: `COMMIT_CHECKS` and `SERVED_CHECKS`. `checks(..., served=True)` words the messages by fetch ("matches the changed fetch's stored bytes", "also matches the previous fetch's", "also matches the Baseline fetch's"), and the check names are unchanged.
  - `backend/numen/domain.py`: `PLAYBOOKS[("served_content", "Malicious change")]` is static_proof, detection_rule and cleanup_report. The Upstream Malicious change cell gains `served_content_sweep`, whose step reads `served_content_sweep.status`, else `pending`. `finding_cell` reads the role `served_content` for a served_content Finding. The "Served content is Phase 9's" comments are gone.
  - `backend/numen/workflows.py`:
    - `served_analysis`, Malicious change: `open_finding(..., "served_content", change.id, title, page URL, {by: verdict, verdict, confidence, analysis_id, served_change_id})`. For an Open or Confirmed Finding it sets `source_analysis_id`, `evidence`, `static_proof {analysis_id, indicators}` and `cleanup_report {analysis_id, page_url, user_agent, fetch_ids, first_seen, secrets}`. It then publishes `report.md`, `indicators.json` and `detection-rule.yar` with `require_complete=False`, which queues `detection_rule`. There is no Remediation PR, and `require_lab` already refuses a Lab.
    - `detection_rule`, for a served_content Finding: the trees are `served_files` of the change's fetch, previous fetch and Baseline fetch. The baseline check is skipped with "The previous fetch is the Baseline fetch" when they are the same. A validated current revision whose cell is `("upstream", "Malicious change")` queues `served_content_sweep` (key `served-sweep:{finding id}:{revision}`).
    - `served_content_sweep`: one `detection_rule` run over `changed/<sha256>` of every stored 200 resource of the Application, unique and newest first, within `MAX_SOURCE_FILES` and `MAX_SUBMISSION` (`truncated`). There is no probe and no fetch, and with nothing stored nothing runs. It records `served_content_sweep` and an Action `served_content_swept` on the Finding.
    - `served_check`: a fetch that matches its Baseline moves every Open or Confirmed served_content Finding of that page and user agent to Remediated, with cause `{by: baseline_match, fetch_id, page_id, user_agent}`.
  - `backend/numen/worker.py`: `LONG_KINDS` gains `served_content_sweep`.
  - Frontend (the Finding page; without it a served Finding failed `findingSchema`): `cleanupReportSchema` defaults `commits` to `[]` and `releases` to null, and takes `page_url`, `user_agent`, `fetch_ids` and `first_seen`. For a served report the Cleanup report panel shows "Served at", "First seen" and "Fetches that served it" in place of commits and releases. `servedDetectionChecks` labels a served static Proof's checks by fetch, and the Playbook panel labels the sweep step and its three states.
  - Tests, `backend/tests/test_served_playbooks.py` (yara is `test_detection_rules.yara`, the simulated runner):
    - `test_a_malicious_change_in_served_content_gets_a_static_proof_detection_rule_and_cleanup_report_and_no_remediation_pr` (A06 Served content, A11, AC-A12):
      - The Finding's source is `served_content`, with the Watched page as its `source_ref` and the opening cause. Its steps are the three, validated, validated and ready.
      - The checked indicators keep the Googlebot page (hashed) and the judol host and URL. They reject a script the Baseline served too, a Baseline path, a bare name, a host also in the Baseline, a host and an empty value not in the changed bytes, and a command that is not there.
      - The cleanup report lists the fetches and the non-blank secrets.
      - No RemediationPR rows, `remediation_actions == []`, and a Lab is refused with 409 `lab_not_allowed`. Job kinds are only check, Triage, Analysis, `detection_rule` and notification.
      - One Alert: {application, source: page URL, verdict and confidence, link}, with no agent text. Accept Baseline is refused with 409.
      - A repeat Analysis gives a new revision on the same Finding, validated, and no second Alert.
    - `test_the_served_content_detection_rule_must_match_the_changed_fetch_and_not_the_baseline_or_previous[passed|changed|parent|baseline|skipped]` (A07 Served content, AC-A07):
      - An HTML comment, which leaves the fingerprint unchanged, puts the rule's string in the Baseline or the previous fetch.
      - The detection_rule files equal `served_files` of the three fetches.
      - A pass confirms with cause `detection_rule`. Each failure names its check with the served message, leaves the artifacts Draft and keeps the Finding Open with only its opened event.
      - With previous == Baseline, the baseline check is skipped and there is no `baseline/` tree.
      - Broker calls are probe, agent, agent, detection_rule.
    - `test_a_later_check_matching_the_baseline_again_remediates_the_finding` (A10, A11, AC-A11): three Findings, the judol one on Googlebot, ClickFix on the browser, and judol plus ClickFix on Googlebot.
      - The Operator dismisses one and marks one remediated (cause operator).
      - A browser-only Baseline match leaves the Googlebot Finding Open and the Dismissed one Dismissed.
      - The Googlebot match remediates it with `{by: baseline_match, fetch_id, page_id, user_agent: googlebot}` and a transition Action. The Operator-remediated one keeps its cause.
    - `test_an_upstream_malicious_change_sweeps_the_stored_served_content` (A06 Upstream Malicious change): an Upstream Application on the fixture shop, whose CDN script carries the postinstall loader's strings.
      - The sweep is the third broker call, a `detection_rule` with only `rule.yar` and the three stored files by sha256, and no probe.
      - The Finding records two matches, browser and Googlebot, with page, fetch, URL, sha256 and time. Status is matched, count 2, not truncated, and the step reads `matched`.
      - The Action is `served_content_swept`, and the Finding stays Confirmed by its Detection rule.
    - `test_a_sweep_with_no_stored_served_content_records_none`: status `no_served_content`, no matches, no extra broker call.
    - `tests/test_playbooks.py` `CELLS`: the Upstream Malicious change cell ends with `served_content_sweep`. `test_detection_rules` and `test_exposure` pass unchanged: their validated Upstream rules now also run an empty sweep, which makes no broker call.
    - `frontend/src/pages/Findings.integration.test.tsx`: a served Finding's cleanup report and check labels, and the sweep step's label and state.
  - Deviations:
    - `check_indicators` also rejects a changed file whose bytes a Baseline fetch served ("The Baseline fetch served these bytes too"), as the commit check rejects a file the commit did not change. The plan asked only for a changed input path.
    - The plan's "The page had no Baseline" skip is not implemented. `served_changes.baseline_fetch_id` is NOT NULL, and a change is only recorded against a Baseline, so the case cannot happen.
    - The served Finding also records `source_analysis_id` and `evidence`, as `run_playbook` does for a commit, and the bundle's `source_ref` is the fingerprint.
    - A sweep match is one entry per matched file and (page, user agent), from that pair's newest fetch, and `count` counts those entries.
    - The frontend Finding page change was not in the plan's file list. It is needed for a served Finding to render.
  - Limits:
    - The sweep runs once, when a revision validates, and content fetched later is not swept. The swept bytes are held in memory, at most `MAX_SUBMISSION` (marked `ponytail:`). The newest files win when the bounds truncate, and the runner lists at most 1000 matched files, which also sets `truncated`.
    - The Finding page shows the sweep only as its Playbook step's state. Listing its matches is left to T5's UI.
    - `cleanup_report.fetch_ids` grows with every check that serves the fingerprint, and fetches are never pruned.

Its Served content panel has the Asset's **Check now** (`queueJob` to `/served-content-checks`) and the add-page form. Its Watched pages table has one row per page and user agent:
      - the page URL as mono text, never a link, with a Homepage badge or **Remove**;
      - the latest fingerprint (12 characters) and time, or the failed fetch's `HTTP {status}` or error name;
      - the state badge (No Baseline yet, Matches the Baseline, Changed, Fetch failed) with the Baseline's fingerprint, and **Accept Baseline** only when `accept_allowed`;

      - the change's Triage, Analysis, Verdict and Review queue state, **Dismiss** while it waits in the Review queue, **Run Analysis** unless one is queued, and **Open Finding**.

    - `Findings.tsx`: the `served_content_sweep` step reads "Detection rule sweep of served content"; a new panel of that name lists the sweep's matches (page, user agent, file URL, sha256, fetched) as text with its job; the served cleanup report labels the user agent through `userAgentLabels`. `TextList` is exported and reused by the tab, as is `errorText` from `ApplicationRepositories.tsx`.

The `served` subject links "Open Served content".

It links `/served/beacon.js`, whose code would `fetch('/served/executed')` if anything ran it. With the variant `judol` and `Googlebot` in the user agent, it adds Indonesian gambling text and links to `https://slot-gacor.test/daftar` and `https://togel-maxwin.test/`.

Phase 9 deviations, from the planner and the tasks above:
- The demo fixture's private range is named as a Docker network (`NUMEN_SERVED_CONTENT_FIXTURE_NETWORK`), not a CIDR: the run's gateway has to join that network to reach it.
- Watched pages share the Asset URL's origin and number at most 20; moving the Asset URL moves the homepage and clears its Baseline.
- Served Triage and Analysis are their own job kinds (`served_triage`, `served_analysis`) on the runner's commit_triage and commit_analysis tool profiles and the Application's commit profiles, counted against the same caps. Jev mode blocks served Triage.
- Accept Baseline needs an Analysis Verdict of Security hardening or Unrelated, as A10 says: a Triage `unrelated` decision or a Review queue dismissal does not allow a new Baseline. The tab's **Run Analysis** is the way to get one by hand.
- The tab's **Dismiss** and **Run Analysis** were not in T5's plan. Without them, a served-content change that the dashboard's Review queue links to the tab could not be judged there, and a change recorded with Triage off could never get the Verdict that Accept Baseline needs.

### Phase 9 review fixes (2026-11-14)

Regression tests are in `backend/tests/test_served_fingerprints.py` and `backend/tests/test_served_content.py`.

- **A page with more than 20 scripts failed every check.** `collect` lists every script src, and those past `max_scripts` are `skipped`/`limit`, but `served_fetches` counted all of them against the limit. That raised `invalid_probe_result` and stored nothing for any page of the Application. It now counts only the fetched entries, so the listed hosts still count in the summary. `test_a_page_with_more_than_20_scripts_is_checked` runs a full check on a page with 24 scripts.
- **The browser's scripts could starve Googlebot's page.** Both user agents shared one 7 MiB byte budget, and the browser fetched first. Each user agent now has half of `max_bytes`, and bytes another fetch already stored cost nothing. `test_one_user_agents_scripts_never_starve_the_others_page` covers this, and the 20-script test's small budget is doubled to match.
- **`<base href>` was ignored.** An injected base moves every relative script and link to another host, and the fingerprint did not change. `Page.base_url` resolves srcs and URLs against the first `<base href>`, as a browser does. The probe then fetches the scripts from the base's host (`test_a_base_href_moves_the_scripts_the_probe_fetches`).
- **Frames, form actions, meta refreshes, stylesheets, objects and embeds were not in the summary.** `links` now holds what a page follows (`a`/`area` href, form action, a meta refresh's URL, each read bare) and what it loads (`iframe`/`frame` src, `link` href, `object` data, `embed` src). A loaded URL is read as a Script family, so a bundler-renamed stylesheet is not a change. Two parametrised tests cover them.
- **More per-request values are masked.** A hidden input named like `nonce` (WordPress `_wpnonce`) is a token now, and so is a string an inline script gives a key like nonce, token or csrf (`"nonce":"..."` in localized script data). Digit runs of 10 or more in inline scripts read as `0`. The rotating-token page of `test_served_fingerprints.py` gains a WordPress nonce and a render timestamp.
- **A skipped page's error status was a string.** `served_fetches` records `error.status` only when it is an HTTP status, else null, with the reason in `error.error`. The tab's schema stays `number | null`.
- **A new Asset origin left the old origin's pages watched.** `sync_homepage` now sets `removed_at` on every page left on an old origin, and their history is kept (`test_a_new_asset_origin_stops_watching_the_old_origins_pages`).
- Not changed: an automatic served-Triage cap. PRD A04 caps Analysis only, and commit Triage has no cap either. Cloudflare's `__CF$cv$params` ray id is still unmasked, so such a page can get a new fingerprint, and a Triage, on every check.
- Runs on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), about 48 GB free on the VM, with no request to an outside host:
  - `make build` passed.
  - `make check` passed.
  - `make test-unit` passed: 246 passed; runner 145 passed, 67 skipped.
  - `make test-integration` (PostgreSQL) passed: 596 passed.
  - `make test-runtime` passed: 211 passed, 1 skipped.
  - `make test-ui` passed: 158 passed in 21 files.
  - `make test-journey` passed, with every step PASS. Its stack was then stopped with `down`.

## Phase 10: Plugins, Transcripts and display hardening (2026-11-15)

Phase 10 (A13's read-only plugin list and the Pi upgrade, AC-A13's second and third bullets, A14 and AC-A14, A15's loopback and Content Security Policy bullets with AC-A15's first, and defaulted decisions 13 and 14). This section records each task as it lands.

- **T2, Pi 1.3.0 and `pi-commandcode-provider` 0.7.8 (decision 13, AC-A13 bullet 3).**
  - `runner/node/pi/package.json` pins `@earendil-works/pi-ai` 1.3.0, `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` 1.3.0, `pi-commandcode-provider` 0.7.8 and `undici` 8.12.4, and keeps the `undici` 8.12.4 override. `package-lock.json` was regenerated in the pinned `node:24.9.0-bookworm-slim@sha256:3e69…` image with `npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, so nothing was installed on the host. It drops from 234 to 120 packages, because Pi 1.3.0 no longer shrinkwraps its own dependency tree and npm hoists it. The Pi entries (`pi-ai`, `pi-coding-agent`, its nested brace-expansion and the provider) carry the same integrity as hekata's lock, which was read only.
  - Registry integrity: `npm view pi-commandcode-provider@0.7.8 dist.integrity`, run in the same container, returned `sha512-weLcSVM4dmlzZSS8Aei6lRGaaoKsRMprTYqFeQRr3lJSQ420HHgLS20CWeD4veRU8sV1DFbkLFC6LEXYm8hApg==`. That equals the new lock entry. `runner/plugins.lock.json` now records it with version 0.7.8, and `agents.pi.version` is 1.3.0.
  - The brace-expansion patch is removed. Pi 1.0.0 shrinkwrapped brace-expansion 5.0.9 and bundled its vulnerable `expand` into `dist/bundle/chunks/chunk-33XOIQ5N.js`, which `patch-pi-dependency.py` repaired. The 1.3.0 image built without the patch shows:
    - the installed `node_modules/@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent/node_modules/brace-expansion` is 5.0.12;
    - Pi 1.3.0 publishes no `npm-shrinkwrap.json`, so no shrinkwrap can override the lock. Its `package.json` declares brace-expansion exactly `5.0.12`, and both `/opt/numen/package-lock.json` and `node_modules/.package-lock.json` pin 5.0.12;
    - none of the 1,092 files under its `dist/` contains the 5.0.9 `expand`. The only bundled `expand`, in `dist/bundle/chunks/chunk-66G7ITPS.js`, is the fixed one, with `maxDepth` and `maxRewrites`.
    - So `runner/node/patch-pi-dependency.py`, `runner/dependency-patches.lock.json` and `runner/vendor/brace-expansion-5.0.12.tgz` are deleted (the `runner/vendor` directory goes with them), and the pi stage of `Dockerfile.runner` runs `npm ci --omit=dev --ignore-scripts && npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high && ./node_modules/.bin/pi --version && npm cache clean --force`.
  - `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` passes in the build. It reports two moderates and no high: minimatch 10.2.6 nested under `pi-coding-agent` (GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249), whose only fix npm offers is `pi-coding-agent` 1.5.2. That is below the gate, so no override was added.
  - Pi 1.3.0 keeps every API the runner uses, so `pi-capabilities.mjs`, `pi-commandcode-login.mjs`, `pi-tools.ts` and `adapters.py` are unchanged:
    - the exports `createAgentSession`, `DefaultResourceLoader`, `ModelRuntime`, `SessionManager` and `SettingsManager`;
    - `@earendil-works/pi-ai/compat`'s `clampThinkingLevel` and `getSupportedThinkingLevels`;
    - `./core/defaults.js`'s `DEFAULT_THINKING_LEVEL` (`medium`);
    - every `PI_BASE` flag, plus `--print --mode json`, `--no-tools`, `--no-builtin-tools`, `--tools`, `--extension` and `--thinking`. In 1.3.0, `--no-extensions` also disables Pi's built-in extensions, while an explicit `-e` path still loads.
  - Tests:
    - `runner/tests/test_docker_runtime.py`:
      - `test_actual_native_cli_version[pi-1.3.0]`.
      - `test_pi_carries_the_fixed_brace_expansion` replaces `test_pi_fixed_dependency_and_bundled_entrypoint_are_verified`. It checks the version 5.0.12 and its two pins, that there is no shrinkwrap, that no `dist/` file has the old `expand` (now `OLD_EXPAND` in the test) and that the fixed one is bundled, plus the existing depth-bound behaviour checks.
      - `test_pi_image_carries_the_pinned_plugin`: inside `asset-monitor-pi:0.1.0`, with no network, the installed provider is 0.7.8, its lock integrity equals `plugins.lock.json`'s, and `agents.pi.version` equals the installed Pi, 1.3.0.
      - `test_a_pi_job_records_its_pinned_image_agent_and_plugin_versions`: a real `engine.run` of a Command Code `commit_analysis`, with a probe in place of the workload so no provider is contacted, returns and reports the image's digest, `agent_version` 1.3.0 and `plugin_versions` `{"pi-commandcode-provider": "0.7.8"}`.

The test passed at once, because `queue.py` already recorded provenance; it adds the missing coverage.

- **T3, Analysis Transcripts in artifact storage, linked to their Verdict (A14, AC-A14 bullet 1).**
  - `Workflows.redacted` also replaces each secret's standard base64 form, so `artifacts.clean_files` never refuses a redacted Transcript. Triage Transcripts get this too.
  - Each completed commit, served-content and CVE Analysis reads its run's final session archive from the broker (`session_transcript`, right after the run and before the fence). The archive holds each native JSONL record in order, rendered as its role and content (`session_history.render_record`), then the latest Herdr terminal view. With the Verdict, `store_analysis_transcript` publishes the redacted text as `transcript.txt` in a `resource_type: transcript` Artifact, then adds a `transcripts` row (`kind: analysis`, `subject_id` the commit, change or CVE, `data: {status, analysis_id, run_id, revision}`). A run with no readable session gets a `transcript_unavailable` job event, and a Transcript that cannot be stored gets `transcript_not_stored`. Neither fails the Verdict.
  - Finding JSON gains `transcripts` (`domain.finding_subjects`: source and fix commits, the served-content change, the CVE, and linked commits; oldest first). `GET /transcripts/{id}` returns a Transcript with its `text`: a Triage one's data as JSON, or an Analysis one's file, read with its integrity checked. The Finding page lists the Transcripts in a Transcripts panel, and Show renders each one's text in a `<pre>` as escaped text.
  - The Operator password is stored only as a hash and never reaches an agent, so it cannot be redacted from agent text. The secret test checks that it is absent, but does not inject it into a reply.
  - Tests, each seen failing before the change:
    - `backend/tests/test_transcripts.py`:
      - `test_no_stored_transcript_contains_a_configured_secret`: a Triage reply and an Analysis session that hold the Analysis API key, the GitHub read and write tokens, the NVD key, the Discord webhook URL and the vault key (the session also holds the write token in base64). No Transcript row and no Transcript file contains any of them or the Operator password, and each holds `[redacted]` once per value.
      - `test_a_findings_transcripts_are_listed_with_it_and_the_analysis_one_names_its_verdict`: the Finding lists `[triage, analysis]`, the Analysis one's `analysis_id` is the Finding's `cause.analysis_id`, and `GET /transcripts/{id}` returns the session text and the Triage prompt and reply.
      - `test_an_analysis_without_a_session_still_records_its_verdict`: with no session, the Verdict is recorded, a `transcript_unavailable` event is added and there is no Analysis Transcript.
    - The served Malicious change test in `test_served_playbooks.py` and the CVE analysis test `test_artifacts.py::test_cve_analysis_produces_downloadable_finding_bundle_and_reuses_finding` also assert their Analysis Transcript.
    - `frontend/src/pages/Findings.integration.test.tsx` "shows a Finding's Transcripts as text": Show displays `<script>alert('transcript')</script>` as text, and the page has no `script` element.
    - `FixtureBroker` gains `screen` (a short fixture session by default; `None` means no session) and `session()`.
  - Runs on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to an outside host:

- **T4, Transcript retention and the backup round trip (A14, AC-A14 bullet 2).**
  - Migration `a7b8c9d0e1f2` adds `transcripts.size`, backfilled as `octet_length(CAST(data AS TEXT))` for existing rows (all Triage ones), and the index `ix_transcripts_subject_id`. The downgrade drops both. `triage_transcript` sets `size` to the bytes of its `data` as JSON, and `store_analysis_transcript` to the bytes of `transcript.txt`.
  - `domain.prune_transcripts` deletes Triage Transcripts older than 30 days, then the oldest Triage ones while the Triage total is over 256 MiB (`TRIAGE_TRANSCRIPT_BYTES`). It keeps every Analysis Transcript whose `subject_id` is in `domain.finding_subjects` of all Findings and deletes the other Analysis Transcripts older than 30 days with their `transcript` Artifact rows and returns those revisions. `scheduler.prune` commits that, then removes the revision folders with `artifacts.remove_revisions`, which was `api.remove_revision_folders` and is now used by both Finding deletions too. The scheduler runs it at start and then hourly, gated on `time.monotonic`.
  - Tests, each seen failing before the change:
    - `backend/tests/test_transcripts.py::test_retention_prunes_triage_transcripts_after_30_days_or_past_256_mb_oldest_first`: a 31-day-old row and a 40-day-old row about the Finding's source commit go; of three young 100 MiB rows, one about a commit linked to the Finding, only that oldest one goes.
    - `test_transcripts.py::test_retention_deletes_unlinked_analysis_transcripts_after_30_days_and_keeps_a_findings`: of four Analysis Transcripts (old and unlinked, old and the Finding's source commit, old and linked through a `linked` Action, young and unlinked) only the first loses its row, its Artifact rows and its revision folder. The other three still read through `GET /transcripts/{id}` with their integrity checked.
    - `test_no_stored_transcript_contains_a_configured_secret` also checks both rows' `size`.
    - `backend/tests/test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_transcript_size`: from `f6a7b8c9d0e1`, a Triage row gets its byte size (a non-ASCII reply counts in bytes), `alembic check` is clean at head, and the downgrade drops the column and the index and keeps the row.
  - The round trip (`make test-journey`, all on the isolated `asset-monitor-test` project). After `journey.py`, `scripts/test-journey.sh` runs `tests/integration/transcripts.py`:
    - `seed`, in the app container, gives the newest `Fixture Own change` Finding a Triage Transcript row and an Analysis Transcript published through `artifacts.publish`, both about its source commit, and prints their ids with the sha256 of the text `GET /transcripts/{id}` returns.
    - `up -d --wait --force-recreate` recreates every container (the container rebuild), and `check` logs in and finds both ids in the Finding's `transcripts` with the same sha256.
    - With `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test` and `COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml` exported, and a guard that `compose config` names `asset-monitor-test`, `scripts/backup.sh` writes `.numen/test-backups/<UTC stamp>`. Then `down -v` removes the test project's three volumes, `up -d --wait db` starts an empty database, `cmp -s .env` against the backup's `deployment.env` confirms that restore.sh's rewrite of the shared `.env` changes nothing, and `scripts/restore.sh` restores it. After `up -d --wait` of the services, `check` passes again, and the backup directory is deleted. `backup.sh` and `restore.sh` are unchanged: they already archive the database and all of `/data/artifacts`.
    - Result: `JOURNEY PASSED`, then "PASS a container rebuild keeps the Transcripts" and "PASS a backup, an empty project and a restore keep the Transcripts". The Operator's `asset-monitor` containers kept running throughout, and its volumes were not touched.
  - Runs on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to an outside host:

    - `make check` passed. `make test-journey` passed, as above.

- **T5, display hardening (A15's `127.0.0.1` and Content Security Policy bullets, AC-A15 bullet 1).**
  - `security.SecurityHeaders`, a pure ASGI middleware next to `RequestLimits` and outermost in `api.create_app`, sets on every HTTP response `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. It replaces a route's own copy, so the artifact and analysis download routes no longer set `nosniff` themselves. `img-src data:` is the select arrow's SVG in `styles.css`. `style-src` needs no `'unsafe-inline'`: React's `style` props go through the CSSOM, which the policy allows, and the browser check below saw no style violation.
  - `FastAPI(docs_url=None, redoc_url=None, openapi_url=None)`: Swagger UI was the one page that needed inline script, and nothing used it. `/docs` and `/openapi.json` now fall through to the dashboard.
  - The built `index.html` already loads only `<script type="module" crossorigin src="/assets/index-….js">` and a stylesheet link; Vite emits no inline script.
  - New `frontend/src/csp.ts`, imported first by `main.tsx`, sets `z.config({ jitless: true })`. When Zod 4.3.6 builds an object schema, it probes for eval with `try { Function("") } catch {}` unless `jitless` is set. Under `script-src 'self'` the probe fails safely, but it fired a `securitypolicyviolation` (`script-src eval`, from Zod's `allowsEval`) on every page load in the browser check. A `z.config` call in `main.tsx` itself ran too late, because the schemas in `types.ts` and the pages are built while `App` is imported. Jitless parsing gives the same results, so the frontend tests, which do not load `main.tsx`, are unaffected.
  - `frontend/eslint.config.js` adds `no-restricted-syntax` errors, citing A15, for `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` as a JSX attribute, a props object key (`createElement` props and spread objects, quoted or not) or a member access (`:matches(JSXIdentifier, Identifier)[name='dangerouslySetInnerHTML'], Literal[value='dangerouslySetInnerHTML']`, added after review), `AssignmentExpression[left.property.name=/^(innerHTML|outerHTML)$/]` and `CallExpression[callee.property.name='insertAdjacentHTML']`. `make check` runs it (`npm run lint`). Tests only read `innerHTML`, so they pass.
  - The `test` stage of `Dockerfile` copies `compose.yaml` and `compose.test.yaml` to `/app/` for the port test.
  - Rendering audit:
    - `grep` over `frontend/src` finds no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `srcDoc`, `iframe`, `eval` or `new Function`, and no `innerHTML` except four reads in `NativeCacheImport.integration.test.tsx`.
    - Markdown reports go through `ui.Markdown`: `react-markdown` 10.1.0 with `remark-gfm` only, no `rehype-raw` (not a dependency), images shown as their alt text and links through `ExternalLink`.
    - Diffs (`ui.tsx` `DiffView`, one `<span>` per line), agent output (`AgentSession` `AnsiText` and `SpanLines` in `<pre>`), Transcripts (`Findings.tsx` `TranscriptText`, `<pre className="json-view">`), reports and Proof rules (`json-view`), source files (`ArtifactPreview`), commit messages (`Commits.tsx` titles and "Upstream commit:"), and served-content values (fingerprints, Baselines) are all React text children.
    - Artifacts are served as `text/plain` or `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and, now from the middleware, `nosniff`.
  - Tests:
    - `backend/tests/test_api.py::test_every_response_is_served_under_a_content_security_policy_that_forbids_inline_script`: an app with a temporary `frontend_root` (`index.html` and `assets/app.js`). `GET /`, `GET /assets/app.js` and `GET /api/auth/session` each carry the policy with `script-src 'self'` and no `unsafe` source, `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'` and `form-action 'self'`, exactly one `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. `/docs`, `/openapi.json` and `/redoc` are not Swagger.
    - `test_api.py::test_the_built_dashboard_has_no_inline_script`: parses `$NUMEN_FRONTEND_ROOT/index.html` (`/app/frontend/dist` in the test image) with `html.parser`: every `<script>` has a same-origin `src` and an empty body, and no element has an `on*` attribute. It passed before the change too, because Vite emits no inline script; it guards the build.
    - `backend/tests/test_deployment.py::test_every_published_port_is_bound_to_loopback`: yaml-loads `compose.yaml` and `compose.test.yaml`. Every `ports` entry starts with `127.0.0.1:` (or is a long-form mapping with `host_ip: 127.0.0.1`), and `app` publishes `127.0.0.1:${NUMEN_PORT:-8788}:8080`.
    - `frontend/src/pages/CommitReview.integration.test.tsx` "renders a commit message containing <script> as text": the Reviews list and CommitDetail show the message `<script>window.__pwned = true</script> Fix login` as text (the list subtitle and "Upstream commit: …"), the rendered container has no `script` element and `window.__pwned` is undefined.
    - Before the change, the CSP test and the port test failed (no `content-security-policy` header; no `/app/compose.yaml`). The built-index test and the frontend test passed at once. To see the frontend test and the lint rule bite, "Upstream commit:" was rendered once through `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, with an `innerHTML` write and an `insertAdjacentHTML` call added: `eslint` reported 3 `no-restricted-syntax` errors and the test failed ("Unable to find an element with the text: Upstream commit: <script>…"). Both were then reverted.
  - Browser check, on the isolated test stack at http://localhost:18788 after `make test-journey`:
    - The browser was the host's Google Chrome 157, headless, with a throwaway profile in the scratchpad and DevTools on `127.0.0.1` only. A CDP client in the `asset-monitor-app` image (its `websockets`) drove it and added a `securitypolicyviolation` listener to every document. It logged in with the journey's password and loaded `/`, `/applications`, `/reviews`, `/findings`, `/cves`, `/jobs`, `/settings`, and one Application, Finding, Job and commit page. Each page rendered its heading.
    - `curl -I http://localhost:18788/` and the browser's own response both carry the policy, `nosniff` and `no-referrer`. `/openapi.json` returns the dashboard.
    - First run: no style and no inline-script violation, but 12 `script-src eval` events, one per page load, from Zod's `allowsEval` probe. After `csp.ts` and a rebuild: zero violations of any kind during the tour, and Chrome logged no `Content Security Policy` console error.
    - Control: an inline `<script>` injected after the tour was refused (Chrome logged "Executing inline script violates … 'script-src 'self''" and the listener saw `script-src-elem inline`), and its `window.__pwned` stayed undefined. So the zero count is real, and `style-src 'self'` needs no `'unsafe-inline'`.

The full `npm run format:check` still flags seven files T5 did not touch: `ArtifactPreview.tsx`, `ApplicationServedContent.tsx` and its test, `Findings.tsx` and its test, `Jobs.integration.test.tsx` and `types.ts`. It is not part of `make check`.
    - `make test-unit` passed: 250 passed; runner 145 passed, 69 skipped.

    - `make test-journey`, which runs `make build` first, passed on the final code: `JOURNEY PASSED`, "PASS a container rebuild keeps the Transcripts" and "PASS a backup, an empty project and a restore keep the Transcripts". `.numen/test-backups` is empty afterwards, and the Operator's `asset-monitor` containers and volumes were not touched.

- **Phase 10 acceptance, each mapped to its tests.**
  - AC-A13 bullet 2, the plugin screen has no install action (T1): `backend/tests/test_accounts.py::test_the_plugin_list_is_read_only` (GET lists only `runner/plugins.lock.json`; POST `/plugins` and PATCH, PUT and DELETE `/plugins/{id}` are 405 `plugins_read_only` naming the lockfile), `test_an_unavailable_commandcode_plugin_never_runs_and_a_ready_one_is_sent`, `test_commandcode_login_requires_a_ready_plugin`, `test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_drops_the_plugins_table`, and `frontend/src/pages/Settings.integration.test.tsx` "lists the pinned Pi plugins with no install, enable, upload or remove action".
  - AC-A13 bullet 3, a job records the plugin and image versions it used (T2): `runner/tests/test_docker_runtime.py::test_a_pi_job_records_its_pinned_image_agent_and_plugin_versions` and `backend/tests/test_accounts.py::test_a_job_records_the_image_agent_and_plugin_versions_it_used`.
  - Decision 13, the plugin pin (T2): `test_docker_runtime.py::test_actual_native_cli_version[pi-1.3.0]`, `test_pi_image_carries_the_pinned_plugin` and `test_pi_carries_the_fixed_brace_expansion`, with the registry-verified integrity recorded under T2.
  - Decision 14 and A10's amended Baseline bullet (T1): `backend/tests/test_served_playbooks.py::test_a_change_triage_declined_can_become_the_baseline`, `test_a_malicious_change_or_untriaged_change_cannot_become_the_baseline` (both cases) and `test_each_served_content_verdict_runs_only_its_cell[Insufficient context]` (dismissed from the Review queue).
  - AC-A14 bullet 1, no stored Transcript contains a configured secret (T3): `backend/tests/test_transcripts.py::test_no_stored_transcript_contains_a_configured_secret`; with `test_a_findings_transcripts_are_listed_with_it_and_the_analysis_one_names_its_verdict` and `test_an_analysis_without_a_session_still_records_its_verdict` for the link to the Verdict.
  - AC-A14 bullet 2, a Finding's Transcripts survive a container rebuild and a backup and restore (T4): `make test-journey`'s `tests/integration/transcripts.py` seed and check around `up --force-recreate` and around `scripts/backup.sh`, `down -v` and `scripts/restore.sh` on `asset-monitor-test`; retention by `test_transcripts.py::test_retention_prunes_triage_transcripts_after_30_days_or_past_256_mb_oldest_first` and `test_retention_deletes_unlinked_analysis_transcripts_after_30_days_and_keeps_a_findings`; the migration by `test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_transcript_size`.
  - AC-A15 bullet 1, a commit message containing `<script>` renders as text (T5): `CommitReview.integration.test.tsx` "renders a commit message containing <script> as text", and for Transcripts `Findings.integration.test.tsx` "shows a Finding's Transcripts as text".
  - A15 `127.0.0.1` only (T5): `backend/tests/test_deployment.py::test_every_published_port_is_bound_to_loopback`.
  - A15 escaped text under a CSP that forbids inline script (T5): `test_api.py::test_every_response_is_served_under_a_content_security_policy_that_forbids_inline_script`, `test_the_built_dashboard_has_no_inline_script`, the `eslint` rule in `make check`, and the browser check above.
  - Known failures: none.

- **Review fixes.**
  - `domain.exportable` maps a `transcript` Artifact to its Transcript's Analysis, so `GET /artifacts/{id}/content` refuses an Upstream commit Analysis Transcript's `transcript.txt` (403 `upstream_proof_not_exported`) as it refuses that Analysis's `poc.py`. Tested in `test_transcripts.py::test_a_findings_transcripts_are_listed_with_it_and_the_analysis_one_names_its_verdict`.
  - A CVE Analysis runs per Application, so its Transcript's `subject_id` is `<application id>:<CVE id>`, and `finding_subjects` names a `cve` Finding that way. One Application's cve Finding no longer lists, or keeps, another Application's Transcripts of the same CVE: `test_transcripts.py::test_each_applications_cve_finding_lists_only_its_own_analysis_transcript`.
  - Retention follows A14's first bullet as written: Triage Transcripts go after 30 days, or oldest first past 256 MB, even when a Finding is about their subject. Only Analysis Transcripts are kept as long as their Finding. The Triage retention test now has a Finding's 40-day-old Triage Transcript and its young 100 MiB one both pruned.
  - Analysis Transcripts are the broker archive's rendering of each native JSONL record, by role and content, then the Herdr screen. They are not the raw JSONL files, which also carry ids, timestamps and usage. The docstring and docs now say so. This is a deviation from A14's second bullet, reported to the orchestrator.
  - The `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` lint rule also catches a props object key and a member access. A temporary probe file had four forms: the JSX attribute, `createElement` props, a JSX spread object and a quoted key. `eslint` reported four errors, and the probe was then deleted.
  - Runs on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to an outside host: `make build` passed. `make check` passed. `make test-unit` passed: 250 passed; runner 145 passed, 69 skipped. `make test-integration`, with `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test` and `COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml` so the Operator's database was not used, passed: 612 passed. `make test-runtime` passed: 213 passed, 1 skipped. `make test-ui` passed: 161 passed in 22 files. `make test-journey` passed: `JOURNEY PASSED`, "PASS a container rebuild keeps the Transcripts" and "PASS a backup, an empty project and a restore keep the Transcripts". Its stack was then stopped.

## Phase 11: Demo (2026-11-15)

Phase 11 covers A16 and AC-A16 (the demo Node Application's five steps and the demo PHP Application), ADR-0005's "run every demo on a disposable VM" and A10's demo fixture private range, allowed only on the demo VM. The run sheet is `docs/demo.md`. Everything the demo watches is a local stand-in, and no real exploit code is used. No database change was needed, so there is no Alembic migration.

- **T1, the git-backed GitHub stand-in, the staged advisory feeds and the prepared demo content.**
  - **Demo mode.** `tests/integration/fixture_server.py` gains a demo mode, switched on by `NUMEN_FIXTURE_DEMO=1`. It is still one server with one Handler. Before it serves anything, it builds bare git repositories under `/tmp/asset-monitor-demo-git`, with `uploadpack.allowFilter` and `allowAnySHA1InWant` set:
    - `asset-monitor-demo/react` holds three commits: "Release 19.0.0" (annotated tag `v19.0.0`), a fix commit whose message names GHSA-fv66-9v8q-g76r (CVE-2025-55182), and "Release 19.0.1" (`v19.0.1`).
    - `asset-monitor-demo/shop` is built from `tests/integration/demo_repositories/shop/base/`. The code adds the vendored `vendor/react-server-dom-webpack-19.0.0.tgz` and a lockfileVersion 3 `package-lock.json` that locks 19.0.0 as `file:vendor/...`.

    Commits use a fixed author and dates one hour apart, so a rebuild gives the same shas.
  - **The GitHub routes.** Everything under `/repos/asset-monitor-demo/<repo>` is answered from git:
    - the reads Asset Monitor calls, including commits with an ETag and 304, compare, contents, tarball, tags, branches, `git/trees?recursive=1` and pulls;
