    - the writes `git/blobs`, `git/trees`, `git/commits`, `git/refs` (create only) and `pulls`, each recorded in `STATE.writes`.

    `/git/asset-monitor-demo/<repo>.git` runs `git http-backend` for read-only clones, and `git-receive-pack` answers 403. `/admin/demo/push`, `/admin/demo/merge` and `/admin/demo/publish` work in demo mode only.
  - **The advisory feeds.** In demo mode each stand-in advisory is published one step at a time: React2Shell at step 4, node2shell at step 5. Outside demo mode node2shell is listed from the start, so the journey is unchanged.
  - **The React stand-in is rewritten.** `decodeAction` resolves an action's `$$id` (`module#export`) through a server manifest. 19.0.0 has no own-property check: an inherited member such as `app/actions#constructor` is answered with the marker `asset-monitor-simulated-flaw`, and nothing is loaded or called. 19.0.1 adds the `hasOwnProperty` check and refuses. The two versions differ only in `resolveServerReference`, which is what the fix commit changes.
    - This changes the journey's React2Shell PoC body, which now sends `{"$$id": "app/actions#constructor"}`, and the stand-in tarballs' integrity.
    - The Phase 7 T6 line above that describes 19.0.0's `$$id` behaviour is now historical. `docs/implementation-contract.md` describes the new behaviour.
  - **The prepared Own-repository content:**
    - the storefront, whose `POST /action` goes through `decodeAction`, with a Dockerfile `FROM node:20.11.1-alpine` that runs `npm ci`;
    - step 1, a `GET /invoices/<name>` path traversal;
    - step 3, a `postinstall` hook and an `_0x` string-array and base64 loader. Its decoded payload only writes `/tmp/.demo-shop-telemetry`, and it names `NPM_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `collect.shop-metrics.test` as strings only.
  - **Tests.**
    - `python fixture_server.py check`, which `make test-demo` runs in a driver container, asserts the repositories and routes above. That includes a blobless clone whose `git log -p` fetches blobs lazily (`rev-list --missing=print`), `for-each-ref --contains` giving `v19.0.1`, a refused push, and an inert decoded payload.
    - `make test-journey` was re-run on the new stand-in and passes.
  - **Ad hoc checks, not committed:**
    - the real backend clients (Sources, exposure, remediation, dependencies and the advisory clients) ran against a live demo fixture without errors;
    - the Pre-filter flags step 3;
    - an offline `npm ci --ignore-scripts` of the step 1 shop in the Lab image succeeded and gave the marker and `root:x:0:0`;
    - `node --check` parses the three prepared JavaScript files.

- **T2, the demo overlay, `scripts/demo.sh`, the seed through the public API, and the seed's smoke test.**
  - **`make demo-up`** runs `make build`, then `./scripts/demo.sh up`. That starts `compose.yaml` plus `compose.demo.yaml` under the `asset-monitor-demo` project, with its own volumes, on 127.0.0.1:8790. It then bootstraps the Operator and seeds the demo through the HTTP API, idempotently:
    - the two stand-in GitHub accounts;
    - a Provider account for each model key present in the environment, followed by a capability refresh;
    - the default profiles (decision 11), `dashboard_url` and the Alert webhook;
    - the Demo Node shop, with its Own repository `asset-monitor-demo/shop` and the derived React Upstream repository at Locked version 19.0.0, both with Baselines;
    - the Demo PHP storefront, with no repository, two Watched pages and accepted Baselines.

    Keys reach the driver container only by name (`-e NAME`), and no value is printed. With no model keys, the seed prints `NEEDS MODEL KEYS`.
  - **`demo.sh` guards:**
    - it accepts only a project named `asset-monitor-demo` or `asset-monitor-demo-*`;
    - `config | head -n 1` must name that project;
    - `up` refuses unless `ASSET_MONITOR_DEMO_VM=1`.
  - **The overlay:**
    - runner-broker gets `NUMEN_SERVED_CONTENT_FIXTURE_NETWORK=${COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME}_served` and `NUMEN_NPM_REGISTRY_URL=https://registry.invalid`;
    - the `served` network is internal and has no fixed name;
    - `fixtures` publishes no port;
    - app and worker get `NUMEN_TEST_MODE=1` and the stand-in base URLs.
  - **Tests:**
    - `make test-demo` (`scripts/test-demo.sh`) runs both guards safely: an unrelated project name with no subcommand, and `up` with `ASSET_MONITOR_DEMO_VM` empty. It then runs the fixture self-check, `down`, `up` (which seeds) and a second seed, and `demo_check.py smoke seed`. That stage checks no duplicates, the Own and Upstream links and Baselines, the PHP Asset's 4 matching rows with every fetched resource on `toko-batik.test`, and no Finding, Alert, stand-in write or agent job.
    - `backend/tests/test_deployment.py`: `test_every_published_port_is_bound_to_loopback` now reads all four compose files. `test_the_served_content_fixture_network_is_opened_only_by_an_overlay` is new.
    - The Dockerfile's test stage copies `compose.demo.yaml` and `compose.demo-test.yaml` for those tests.

- **T3, the scripted demo steps.**
  - **`make demo-step STEP=<name>`** runs `./scripts/demo.sh step <name>`. The names are `1`, `2`, `3`, `merge-revert`, `4`, `5`, `merge-bump`, `php-inject` and `php-clean`:
    - steps 1 and 3 push a prepared commit and run Check now;
    - `2`, `merge-revert` and `merge-bump` squash-merge each open patch, revert or bump Remediation PR in the stand-in and run Check now. With none open they exit 1 and say what to approve first;
    - step 4 publishes React2Shell, runs the Advisory check and a Backfill of 2 on the React link;
    - step 5 publishes node2shell and runs the Advisory check;
    - `php-inject` and `php-clean` switch the served variants and run one Served-content check.

    Each step then waits until no job that runs no agent is still queued or running, and prints what to click.
  - **What the driver never does:** `demo.py` requests no `/lab-runs` or `/approvals` route. `status` lists the Findings, their Remediation PRs with pull URLs, pending Approvals and the stand-in's pull requests. `login()` waits out the login throttle (429) for up to about 90 s.
  - **Tests:** `make test-demo` runs every step through `./scripts/demo.sh step`, in run-sheet order, each followed by its `demo_check.py smoke <stage>`:
    - step 1's import with Triage off;
    - step 3's Pre-filter flags and its 0-match Advisory check;
    - step 4's Confirmed, exposed React2Shell advisory Finding with its ready bump, and the Backfill reaching the fix commit;
    - step 5's single node2shell Finding with its runtime bump, and no duplicate on a second check (AC-A09);
    - `approve`, which first checks that nothing was approved;
    - merge detection after `merge-bump`;
    - `php-inject`'s Googlebot-only link hosts and the ClickFix script host, with `/served/executed` never requested;
    - `php-clean`'s 4 matching rows.

    Steps 2 and `merge-revert` must fail with the "approve … first" message, and `status` must list the merged bump and the pending React bump.

- **T4, the dry run with canned Verdicts, through a test-only broker overlay.**
  - **The canned broker.** `tests/integration/canned_broker.py` is a stdlib server that speaks the broker contract.
    - It answers `agent` and `capabilities` runs at once with canned replies, chosen by operation and by the prepared commit or served change, and never forwards them. Every report says "Canned dry-run reply: no agent ran.", and the catalogue's only model is "Canned dry run, no agent".
    - An unmatched decision fails with `no_canned_reply`.
    - It forwards `lab`, `probe` and `detection_rule` runs, and `/health`, to the real runner-broker, so Labs, yara and served-content fetches run for real.
  - **The test-only overlay.** `compose.demo-test.yaml` adds `canned-broker` and points app, worker and scheduler's `NUMEN_BROKER_URL` at it. Only `scripts/test-demo.sh` adds it, through `NUMEN_DEMO_OVERLAY`, and `compose.demo.yaml` never does.
  - **Tests:**
    - `make test-demo`'s second phase runs `canned_broker.py check`, brings up a fresh project with placeholder keys, and checks that the seed output never contains the key and has no `NEEDS MODEL KEYS` line. It then runs `demo_check.py dry-run`, which took about 8 minutes on Docker Desktop.
    - `test_deployment.py::test_the_canned_dry_run_broker_is_only_in_the_test_overlay`.
    - `journey.py`'s React2Shell PoC moved to a module-level `R2S_POC`, which the canned broker reuses. The journey was re-run and passes.

- **T5, the run sheet, the README pointer and this record.**
  - **New `docs/demo.md`:**
    - the stand-ins and "no real exploit code" stated up front;
    - prerequisites (a disposable VM with demo-scoped, spend-capped keys; Docker Engine with Compose 2.24 or later, make and git; 4 vCPU, 8 GB RAM with 16 GB recommended, 40 GB disk; the SSH tunnel to 127.0.0.1:8790);
    - the keys and where they are set (exported in the shell, never in `.env`), the stand-in GitHub tokens the seed creates, and what works without model keys;
    - every step in order, with its command, what to click, what the audience sees and how long it takes;
    - troubleshooting, teardown, and the `make demo-up` deviation.
  - **README.md** gains a Demo section, `make test-demo` in the Checks list and the run sheet in the documents table. `docs/implementation-contract.md` gains a Demo overlay bullet and the rewritten stand-in's behaviour. `runner/README.md` gains a sentence on the overlay's fixture network and `registry.invalid`.
  - **The run-sheet check.** `scripts/test-demo.sh` checks that `docs/demo.md` names `make demo-step STEP=<name>` for every name in `demo.py`'s `STEPS`. The check failed before `docs/demo.md` existed ("docs/demo.md does not name make demo-step STEP=1.") and passes now.
  - **A Linux host.** `.numen/bootstrap-password` is mode 0600 and owned by the host user. The image's uid 10001 could not read the bind-mounted file on a Linux VM, so the scenario would need a manual step there. `demo.sh` therefore runs `bootstrap-operator` and the driver with `--user "$(id -u):$(id -g)"`. Neither writes outside the database and `/tmp`. `make test-demo` passes with this change on Docker Desktop. Docker Desktop maps file ownership, so the Linux case itself is still unobserved and is left to the Operator's VM run. `docs/demo.md` no longer carries the `chgrp 10001` workaround.
  - **The dry-run overlay stays out of the real demo.** `demo.sh` refuses a set `NUMEN_DEMO_OVERLAY` on any project but `asset-monitor-demo-test`, so a stale export cannot put canned Verdicts on the real `asset-monitor-demo` dashboard. `make test-demo` checks this guard first, together with the project and `ASSET_MONITOR_DEMO_VM` guards.
  - **The overlay's `NUMEN_TEST_MODE` comment** in `compose.demo.yaml` now lists its three side effects, as the contract does: the relaxed webhook check, http git clones and no NVD pacing.

- **Phase 11 acceptance, each mapped to its tests.**
  - **AC-A16** (on a fresh VM, the whole scenario runs to the last Remediation PR with no manual database edits) is proven under canned agents by `make test-demo`'s dry run, `demo_check.py dry-run`.
    - It runs on a fresh `asset-monitor-demo-test` project from `demo.sh up`, the same seed `make demo-up` runs, then every step in run-sheet order through `demo.py`'s own `step()`, and ends at the node2shell runtime bump's draft pull request, the last Remediation PR.
    - Only agent decisions are canned: the broker's `/state` shows only `agent` and `capabilities` canned, and `lab`, `probe` and `detection_rule` forwarded.
    - `demo.py` and `demo_check.py` use only the HTTP API and the stand-in's routes, never the database.
    - The real-key run on a fresh VM was not performed (see below).
  - **Step 1** (Vulnerability introduced, Lab Proof after Approval, patch Remediation PR): `demo_check.py` `dry_step_1` (Finding Open by the Verdict, bundle complete, patch ready, no Lab before Approval; Lab verified and Confirmed by proof; draft pull request on `asset-monitor/<sha7>-patch`). The smoke stage `1` covers the intake with no agent.
  - **Step 2** (merging the patch Remediates the Finding): `dry_step_2`. Merge detection with no agent is covered by the smoke stage `merge-bump`.
  - **Step 3** (Malicious change and its Cure): `dry_step_3`. It checks Confirmed by a validated Detection rule, the indicator files, a cleanup report naming the commit and `NPM_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN`, a ready revert, a Lab refused with 409 `lab_not_allowed`, and the revert approved and merged (Remediated). The smoke stage `3` covers the Pre-filter flags.
  - **Step 4** (Backfill reaches the fix commit, Vulnerability fix and exposed, shared with the advisory match, n-day Proof after Approval, bump Remediation PR): `dry_step_4`. It checks `fix_commit_id` on the advisory Finding, the Exposure "v19.0.0 does not contain the fix" with target `v19.0.1`, a single open Finding, the Lab verified at Locked version 19.0.0 on the Own head, and one bump opened on the shop stand-in only. The smoke stage `4` covers everything that needs no agent.
  - **Step 5** (the Advisory check opens node2shell): smoke stage `5` and `dry_step_5`, which approves its runtime bump.
  - **The demo PHP Application** (judol links only to Googlebot, plus a ClickFix loader tag): smoke stages `php-inject` and `php-clean` cover the fingerprints and Baselines. `dry_php` covers the 3 served-content Findings, each Confirmed by a validated Detection rule, with no Remediation PR and no Lab, Remediated by `baseline_match` after `php-clean`.
  - **Alerts:** one fixed-template Alert per opened Finding, 7 in all (`dry_run`).
  - **A10's demo fixture private range, demo VM only:** `test_deployment.py::test_the_served_content_fixture_network_is_opened_only_by_an_overlay`, and the smoke seed's check that every fetched resource is on `toko-batik.test`.
  - **ADR-0005, demos on a disposable VM:** the `demo.sh` guards, which `make test-demo` checks first (`ASSET_MONITOR_DEMO_VM=1` for `up`, and the project name), plus `docs/demo.md`'s prerequisites. The canned broker stays out of the demo: `test_the_canned_dry_run_broker_is_only_in_the_test_overlay`.
  - **The scripts never approve:** `make test-demo`'s grep of `demo.py`, and the `approve` stage's check that no Finding has an approved event.
  - **A15's loopback-only publishing:** `test_every_published_port_is_bound_to_loopback`, over all four compose files.
  - **Known failures:** none.

- **Deviations, for the orchestrator to record.** No PRD, ADR or glossary file was edited.
  - AC-A16 says "from `make up`". On the demo VM the scenario runs from `make demo-up`: `make build`, then the normal stack plus the stand-ins under `asset-monitor-demo`, then the seed. `make up` stays the Operator's production entry point.
  - The demo project sets `NUMEN_TEST_MODE=1` to point the base URLs at the stand-ins. That also relaxes the Discord-only webhook check, NVD pacing and git's https-only clone protocol inside the demo project.
  - The stand-in advisories are published per step. Real feeds would list both from the start.
  - Pull request links point at github.com, where they do not exist. The stand-in holds the pull requests, and `./scripts/demo.sh status` lists them.
  - The node2shell and php2shell CVE ids are still unconfirmed (a PRD risk). The demo uses only stand-in feeds.

- **Runs** on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), with no request to an outside host and the Operator's `asset-monitor` project on 127.0.0.1:8788 left untouched, on the final code:
  - `make build` passed. `make check` passed, and passed again after the last change.
  - `make test-unit` passed: 250 passed; runner 145 passed, 69 skipped.
  - `make test-integration` passed: 614 passed. It ran with `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test` and `COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml`, so the Operator's database was not used. The 2 tests added since Phase 10 are the new `test_deployment.py` tests.
  - `make test-runtime` passed: 213 passed, 1 skipped.
  - `make test-ui` passed: 161 passed in 22 files.
  - `make test-journey` passed: `JOURNEY PASSED`, "PASS a container rebuild keeps the Transcripts" and "PASS a backup, an empty project and a restore keep the Transcripts". Its `asset-monitor-test` stack was then stopped without `-v`.
  - `make test-demo` passed from scratch on `asset-monitor-demo-test` (127.0.0.1:18790), after the journey and never at the same time: `DEMO SMOKE PASSED` and `DEMO DRY RUN PASSED`. It printed every `PASS` line of the guards, "docs/demo.md names every demo step", the fixture check, the canned broker check (whose deliberate unmatched commit logs `no canned reply`), the seed, each step's smoke stage and each dry-run step, 7 Alerts and the broker split. Its containers, networks and volumes were removed afterwards.

- **Operator-dependent check.** The real-key run on a fresh VM was not performed here, because it means paid inference.
  - **What it needs from the Operator:** a disposable VM, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` (with `DEMO_ANALYSIS_MODEL` and `DEMO_TRIAGE_MODEL`), then `make demo-up` and `docs/demo.md` step by step.
  - **Not exercised here:**
    - the seed's real-key path: account creation, the capability refresh, model and profile selection, and `triage_mode` `pi`;
    - real Triage and Analysis Verdicts;
    - a Linux host's file permissions.
  - **Why it can differ from the dry run:** real agents are not deterministic. The prepared commits are designed so the intended Verdict follows from the diff alone (the fix commit's message names GHSA-fv66-9v8q-g76r), but a real Analysis may still differ. Each squash-merged Remediation PR commit also gets a paid Triage, and maybe an Analysis.

## Phase 12: Ingress (2026-11-15)

Phase 12 is the first phase of release 2. It covers B01 and AC-B01 of `docs/prd/asset-monitor-r2-prd.md`, with defaulted decisions 9, 12, 13, 22 and the Ingress parts of 15, and ADR-0006. One Alembic migration, `b8c9d0e1f2a3`, creates two roles and changes no table. `docs/implementation-contract.md` has a new "Ingress (B01, ADR-0006)" section, and every shape below is described there.

- **T1, `numen.ingress`: the push listener and the agent listener.**
  - **The module.** `backend/numen/ingress.py` builds two ASGI apps through `listener()`:
    - `listener()` serves `http` scopes only. It removes every `Cookie` header, whatever its case, before the app sees the request, and wraps the app in `security.SecurityHeaders`.
    - `push_app()` takes from its global `push` bucket first (`PUSH_LISTENER_PER_MINUTE` = 120) and answers `429` `rate_limited` with `retryable: true` when it is empty. Every other request, `POST /push/<anything>` included, gets `404` `not_found`, and its body is never read.
    - `agent_app()` reads every body through `read_body(BODY_CAP)` (64 KiB) and answers `413` `request_too_large` when it is refused. Only `GET /health` gets `200 {"status": "ok"}`. Every other method and path gets `404`, never `405`.
    - `read_body` refuses an invalid or over-cap `Content-Length` before the first `receive()`. Otherwise it adds up every `http.request` chunk and refuses once the total passes the cap, or on a disconnect.
    - `Buckets` keeps a sliding window per key, never per client address. A refused take is not counted, and an emptied key is dropped.
    - `tls_files` decodes the base64 DER PKCS#8 key, refuses anything but P-256, and writes `key.pem` and a self-signed `cert.pem`, each `O_EXCL` and 0600.
    - `main()` runs uvicorn on 8083 (push) or 8084 (agent, with the TLS files in a fresh `mkdtemp()`), with `access_log=False`, `lifespan="off"` and `ws="none"`.
    - The module imports only the standard library, starlette's `JSONResponse`, `cryptography`, `numen.config.secret_env` and `numen.security`.
  - **Tests.** `backend/tests/test_ingress.py` has 10 tests:
    - `test_neither_listener_serves_the_dashboard`;
    - `test_importing_the_ingress_never_imports_the_dashboard`, in a fresh interpreter, for `numen.api`, `numen.domain`, `numen.workflows` and `numen.worker`;
    - `test_the_agent_listener_caps_bodies_on_the_bytes_read`;
    - `test_an_unknown_push_id_gets_404_and_no_query`, whose `Engine` listener first proves it records a statement, so it cannot pass vacuously;
    - `test_a_session_cookie_changes_nothing`;
    - `test_every_ingress_response_carries_the_dashboard_headers`;
    - `test_buckets_count_each_key_apart`;
    - `test_the_push_listener_has_one_global_bucket`, from two client addresses;
    - `test_the_agent_listener_certificate_carries_its_key`;
    - `test_main_serves_each_listener_without_an_access_log`.

    Starlette's `TestClient` may send an iterator body as one message. So the cap test also drives the ASGI app directly: with a `receive()` that fails the test if it is awaited (an over-cap or invalid `Content-Length`), with four 30 KiB `more_body` chunks (`413`, and the fourth chunk is never read), with chunked bodies within the cap, with `Content-Length` plus chunks, and with a disconnect. Its `TestClient` case sends an iterator body with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` and no `Content-Length`.
  - **Fault checks, not committed.** The module was broken 10 ways in a throwaway container, one at a time: cookie stripping, the `Content-Length` check, byte counting, counting refused takes, the security headers, the access log, keeping empty buckets, a world-readable key, the disconnect path, and importing `numen.domain`. Each break failed at least one test.
  - **Live check, not committed.** Both listeners ran under real uvicorn in a container with no network:
    - the push listener gave `404` for a request with a cookie;
    - the agent listener served TLS, and its certificate's public key matched `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_KEY`;
    - the agent listener gave `413` for a 70 KB body and for a 90 KB chunked body, and `200` for a 60 KB chunked `GET /health`;
    - every response carried the CSP and none set a cookie, and neither process wrote an access-log line.

- **T2, the Ingress database roles.**
  - **Migration** `backend/migrations/versions/b8c9d0e1f2a3_ingress_roles.py`, chained from `a7b8c9d0e1f2`. Its upgrade runs one `DO` block that creates `asset_monitor_push` and `asset_monitor_agent` `NOLOGIN` when missing, and grants nothing. Its downgrade does nothing, citing ADR-0006 decision 8. There is no model change, and `alembic check` is clean.
  - **`python -m numen.cli ingress-roles`** (`ingress_roles(db, passwords)` in `backend/numen/cli.py`):
    - It runs the same create-if-missing block, then `ALTER ROLE ... WITH LOGIN PASSWORD ... CONNECTION LIMIT 5` and `ALTER ROLE ... SET statement_timeout = '5s'`, composed with `psycopg.sql.Identifier` and `Literal`. It grants nothing and can run any number of times.
    - `main()` checks for `ingress-roles` before the admin-password prompt. Without both passwords it exits before it connects, with "NUMEN_PUSH_DATABASE_PASSWORD and NUMEN_AGENT_DATABASE_PASSWORD must be set: run make init". Otherwise it prints "Ingress database roles configured", never the password.
  - **Tests** in `backend/tests/test_postgres.py`. The schema create and drop moved from the `schema_url` fixture into an `isolated_schema(base)` context manager, which the fixture now uses.
    - `test_postgres_ingress_roles_hold_no_grant_on_release_1_tables`: both roles are in `pg_roles`. The schema's table list must include `accounts`, `settings`, `operators`, `sessions`, `findings`, `jobs` and `notification_outbox`, so the check cannot pass on an empty list. `has_table_privilege` is false for every table, both roles and all seven privileges.
    - `test_postgres_ingress_roles_survive_a_downgrade_beside_another_schema`: schemas A and B are migrated to head. B grants `SELECT ON applications TO asset_monitor_push`, and B's rows in `information_schema.table_privileges` for both roles and its `alembic_version` are recorded. A is then downgraded to `a7b8c9d0e1f2`. Both roles remain, and B's grants and `alembic_version` are unchanged. A upgrades to head again with no `alembic check` drift.
    - `test_postgres_ingress_roles_command_gives_login_password_and_limits`: `ingress_roles` runs twice over two throwaway roles (`asset_monitor_test_<hex>`), with passwords that hold `:`, `%`, `@` and `/`.
      - `pg_roles` shows `(True, 5, ['statement_timeout=5s'])`.
      - Each password logs in as its role, and `SHOW statement_timeout` gives `5s`.
      - `SELECT 1 FROM accounts` is refused with "permission denied for table accounts".
      - `python -m numen.cli ingress-roles`, with both password variables and their `_FILE` forms removed, exits non-zero naming both and `make init`.
      - The throwaway roles are dropped afterwards. The cluster's real roles, and the passwords a running journey stack depends on, are never touched.
  - **Fault check, not committed.** With `GRANT INSERT ON jobs TO asset_monitor_agent` added to the migration, the grant test failed with `[('asset_monitor_agent', 'jobs')]`. The file was then restored.
  - T2's own `make test-integration` run was still going (past 91% with no failure) when T2 reported. T3's full run passed, and so does T5's below.

- **T3, the `.env` keys, the Compose services and the deployment tests.**
  - **`scripts/init.sh`:**
    - When `.env` holds all five keys (each matched with `grep -q "^KEY="`), it prints the "preserved" message and exits without Docker.
    - Otherwise it runs the pinned bootstrap container. A new `.env` is created as before (`O_EXCL`, mode 0600, plus `.numen/bootstrap-password`), now with the five keys. On an existing `.env` it appends only the missing keys with `open('a')`, after a newline if the file lacks a trailing one, and prints their names, never their values. It never touches `.numen/bootstrap-password`.
    - `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY` is a Fernet key, made by the same `fernet_key()` helper as `NUMEN_SECRET_KEY`. Each role password is `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`.
    - `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_KEY` is one line of standard base64 of `openssl genpkey -algorithm EC -pkeyopt ec_paramgen_curve:P-256 -outform DER`. `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN` is the lowercase hex SHA-256 of `openssl pkey -inform DER -pubout -outform DER` over that key.
    - The key and the pin stay a pair. A missing pin is derived from the existing key. A pin with no key, or a key that does not decode, stops init with an error and nothing changed.
    - In a scratch copy of the script, these cases all behaved as described: a fresh `.env`; a complete one, without Docker; a release-1 `.env` with no trailing newline; only the pin missing (the derived pin equals the original); the key missing with a pin present; and a bad key. In the backend-test image, `numen.ingress.tls_files` accepted the generated key, the SHA-256 of the certificate's SPKI equalled `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN`, and `Fernet()` accepted `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY`.
  - **The Operator's `.env`, checked by hand.**
    - `.env` was first copied to `.numen/env-before-phase12`. `make init` then printed "Added to .env: NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY, NUMEN_PUSH_DATABASE_PASSWORD, NUMEN_AGENT_DATABASE_PASSWORD, NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_KEY, NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN".
    - `grep -Fxv -f .env .numen/env-before-phase12` printed nothing. The original bytes are an exact prefix of the new `.env` (`cmp`), and each of the five keys appears once.
    - `.env` is still mode 600, and the SHA-256 of `.numen/bootstrap-password` is unchanged.
    - A second `make init` took the fast path, with no container, and left `.env` byte-identical. The copy was then deleted.
  - **`compose.yaml`:**
    - `migrate` runs `sh -c "alembic upgrade head && python -m numen.cli ingress-roles"`, with the anchor's environment plus both role passwords.
    - `app` and `worker` get `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY`. It is not on the anchor, so the scheduler, `migrate`, `bootstrap-operator` and `backend-tests` do not get it.
    - `db` is on `application`, `push-db` and `agent-db`.
    - `ingress-push` and `ingress-agent` run from `asset-monitor-app:0.1.0` with no build, `restart: unless-stopped`, `init: true`, `user: "10001:10001"`, `read_only`, `cap_drop: [ALL]` and `no-new-privileges:true`, and `depends_on: migrate: service_completed_successfully`. Their commands are `python -m numen.ingress push` and `agent`.
    - `ingress-push` is on `push-inbound` and `push-db`, published on `127.0.0.1:${NUMEN_PUSH_PORT:-8791}:8083`. `ingress-agent` is on `agent-inbound` and `agent-db`, published on `127.0.0.1:${NUMEN_AGENT_PORT:-8792}:8084`, with a `/tmp` tmpfs (`size=1m`, uid and gid 10001, mode 0700). Each has exactly the two environment keys of the plan.
    - The push healthcheck is a TCP connect, because an HTTP check would spend the global bucket. The agent healthcheck is `GET /health` over unverified TLS.
    - `push-inbound` and `agent-inbound` are plain bridges, and `push-db` and `agent-db` are internal. None has a fixed name.
  - **Other files.** The Dockerfile's test stage also copies `Dockerfile` and `Dockerfile.runner`. `make up` and `make logs` include both services. `.env.example` has placeholders for the five keys, plus `NUMEN_PUSH_PORT=8791` and `NUMEN_AGENT_PORT=8792`.
  - **Tests** in `backend/tests/test_deployment.py`:
    - `test_every_published_port_is_bound_to_loopback` now also covers both Ingress ports;
    - `test_each_ingress_service_is_on_its_own_two_networks`;
    - `test_each_ingress_service_holds_only_its_own_keys`;
    - `test_the_ingress_services_are_hardened`;
    - `test_every_external_base_image_is_pinned_by_digest`.

    They failed before the change (5 failed, 2 passed) and pass after it (7 passed).
  - **Fault checks, not committed.** Eight broken compose files and Dockerfiles were mounted over the image's copies, one at a time. Each failed the test meant to catch it: `ingress-push` on `application`; `NUMEN_SECRET_KEY` on `ingress-push`; `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY` on the anchor; `agent-db` not internal; the push port off loopback; an overlay giving `ingress-push` a network; `ingress-agent` not read-only; an unpinned `FROM`.
  - **Smoke check on `asset-monitor-test`** (ports 18791 and 18792). Only the `asset-monitor-app:0.1.0` tag was rebuilt, and the Operator's containers kept their image.
    - `up -d --wait ingress-push ingress-agent`: `db` became healthy, `migrate` exited after "Ingress database roles configured", and both services became healthy.
    - `POST http://127.0.0.1:18791/push/abc` and `GET /api/health` on the push port gave `404`.
    - `https://127.0.0.1:18792/health` gave `{"status":"ok"}`, `/api/health` gave `404`, and plain http got no answer.
    - Every response carried the CSP and no `Set-Cookie`. The certificate's SPKI SHA-256 matched `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN`.
    - From inside each Ingress container, psycopg logged in as its role with `statement_timeout` `5s`, and the hosts `app`, `runner-broker` and `fixtures` did not resolve.

- **T4, operations: backup and restore, the journey and the demo scripts.**
  - **`backup.sh`** stops and restarts `app|worker|scheduler|ingress-push|ingress-agent`.
  - **`restore.sh`:**
    - It refuses a backup whose `deployment.env` lacks any of `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY`, `NUMEN_PUSH_DATABASE_PASSWORD`, `NUMEN_AGENT_DATABASE_PASSWORD` or `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_KEY`, with "Backup deployment.env lacks <keys>. For a backup made before release 2, copy them from the current .env into its deployment.env." This runs before any service stops or any data changes.
    - Both Ingress services are in its stop and resume list.
    - Before `DROP SCHEMA public`, it pipes the migration's create-if-missing `DO` block to `exec -T db psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1`.
  - **`tests/integration/ingress_check.py`** runs inside each Ingress service's own container and prints `PASS` lines:
    - `push`: `current_user` is `asset_monitor_push`, and the role cannot read `public.accounts`. `POST /push/<random hex>` and `GET /api/health` each get `404`, with the CSP and no `Set-Cookie`.
    - `agent <pin>`: the same role check for `asset_monitor_agent`. The SHA-256 of the served certificate's SPKI must equal the pin. `GET /health` gets `200 {"status":"ok"}`, and `/api/health` gets `404`.
    - It fails, as it should, with a wrong pin, when the agent check runs from the push container (the name does not resolve), and when the push role is given `SELECT` on `accounts`.
  - **`scripts/test-journey.sh`:**
    - It exports `NUMEN_PUSH_PORT=18791` and `NUMEN_AGENT_PORT=18792`, and both services are in its service list.
    - `numen_ingress_check` runs `ingress_check.py` after the first `up` and again after the restore.
    - `numen_ingress_grants` records each role's `LOGIN`, `CONNECTION LIMIT`, `rolconfig` and every table privilege. Before `backup.sh`, both roles must have `LOGIN`.
    - After `down -v`, it restores a copy whose `deployment.env` lacks the `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY` line. That restore must be refused with the message, and `.env` must still equal the backup's `deployment.env`. Had the restore gone on, the script would first copy the good `deployment.env` back over `.env`, then fail.
    - After the real restore, before any `migrate` runs, both roles must be in `pg_roles`. After `up --wait`, `numen_ingress_grants` must equal the recorded value.
    - New lines: "PASS restore.sh refuses a backup without the ingress keys and changes nothing", "PASS the ingress roles exist right after restore.sh on a fresh database volume" and "PASS the restore brings back the ingress roles, their limits and their grants".
  - **`demo.sh`** exports `NUMEN_PUSH_PORT` and `NUMEN_AGENT_PORT` (8793 and 8794 by default, from `NUMEN_DEMO_PUSH_PORT` and `NUMEN_DEMO_AGENT_PORT`), and `up` starts both services. **`test-demo.sh`** sets 18793 and 18794. `compose config` confirmed `127.0.0.1:18793` and `18794` for the demo test, and 8793 and 8794 by default.
  - **The grant round trip, checked by hand on `asset-monitor-test`.** The push role was granted `SELECT` on `applications` and the agent role `INSERT` on `jobs`, then `backup.sh` ran, and `pg_dump` wrote those GRANTs.
    - On a fresh volume, loading `database.sql` directly fails with `role "asset_monitor_push" does not exist`, while `restore.sh` succeeds.
    - Straight after the restore the grants are back, with `NOLOGIN`. After `up`, `LOGIN`, the limit of 5 and `statement_timeout=5s` match too, and the output equals the value before the backup.
    - The grants were revoked afterwards.

- **T5, the documents and this record.**
  - **README.md:**
    - Start: `make init` appends missing keys to an existing `.env` and never changes a line; after updating to release 2 run it, or `make up`; until then every Compose command, `backup.sh` and `restore.sh` stops with `Run setup first`.
    - Access: the dashboard stays on 127.0.0.1:8788. The push listener on 127.0.0.1:8791 (`POST /push/<push id>` only) is the only port a Public tunnel may point at. The agent listener on 127.0.0.1:8792 serves TLS pinned by `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN`, reached by one Agent tunnel per server, with the `restrict,port-forwarding,permitopen="127.0.0.1:8792",command="/bin/false"` example. No tunnel from a server may point at 8788, and neither listener has cookies, sessions or CSRF.
    - Checks: the journey's 18791 and 18792. Demo: 8793 and 8794, and 18793 and 18794 for `make test-demo`.
    - Backup and restore: both services are paused and resumed; restore creates the roles before it loads and `migrate` gives them `LOGIN`; the refusal and the release-1 workaround (copy the four keys from the current `.env` into the backup's `deployment.env`, which is safe because nothing in such a backup is encrypted with them). `make logs` includes both services.
  - **`docs/implementation-contract.md`** gains the "Ingress (B01, ADR-0006)" section: services, ports and commands; the Phase 12 routes, status codes and error codes; the caps; the buckets wired now and later; cookies, headers and logs; the key and pin formats and the certificate; the role attributes and the `migrate` command; the environment split; the networks; `make init`; and restore's checks.
  - **`docs/demo.md`**: `demo.sh up` also publishes the two Ingress listeners on 127.0.0.1:8793 and 8794.

- **Phase 12 acceptance, each mapped to its tests.**
  - **AC-B01:**
    - *Every published port, both Ingress listeners included, is on `127.0.0.1`:* `test_every_published_port_is_bound_to_loopback`, over all four compose files.
    - *Neither listener serves `/api`, `/api/auth/login`, the SPA or `/assets`, and the push listener answers nothing but `POST /push/<push id>`:* `test_neither_listener_serves_the_dashboard`, plus the journey's `ingress_check.py`, live on both listeners.
    - *Importing `numen.ingress` does not import `numen.api`:* `test_importing_the_ingress_never_imports_the_dashboard`.
    - *Neither role can read Accounts, Settings, Operators, sessions or Findings, or insert a job:* `test_postgres_ingress_roles_hold_no_grant_on_release_1_tables`, plus `ingress_check.py`'s live refusal on `public.accounts` for each role. Phase 12 adds no push or agent table, so "no grant on the other listener's tables" and "cannot change an agent's key or status" hold trivially now. Phases 13 and 15b test them on their tables.
    - *A body over its route's cap gets `413` before it is parsed, chunked too:* `test_the_agent_listener_caps_bodies_on_the_bytes_read`.
    - *An unknown push id gets `404` and makes no database query:* `test_an_unknown_push_id_gets_404_and_no_query`.
    - *A `numen_session` cookie changes nothing:* `test_a_session_cookie_changes_nothing`.
    - *No request to the Ingress sends an Alert by itself:* the import test (neither `numen.domain`, `numen.workflows` nor `numen.worker`, where Alerts are made, is loaded), plus the grant test's `jobs` and `notification_outbox` checks.
    - *Two schemas in one cluster, one downgraded, keep the roles and the other schema's grants:* `test_postgres_ingress_roles_survive_a_downgrade_beside_another_schema`.
    - *A backup restored into a fresh database volume brings back the grants:* the journey's `numen_ingress_grants` round trip ("PASS the ingress roles exist right after restore.sh on a fresh database volume" and "PASS the restore brings back the ingress roles, their limits and their grants"), plus T4's hand check with real grants.
  - **The plan's deployment bullets:** the four new `test_deployment.py` tests above. The role command's attributes: `test_postgres_ingress_roles_command_gives_login_password_and_limits`. The listener's bucket: `test_the_push_listener_has_one_global_bucket` and `test_buckets_count_each_key_apart`. The headers: `test_every_ingress_response_carries_the_dashboard_headers`.
  - **Done when "AC-B01 has tests":** the mapping above.
  - **Done when "`make up` starts both Ingress services next to an unchanged dashboard":** proven on the isolated `asset-monitor-test` project, never the Operator's (see Runs). The dashboard's own suites still pass: `make test-ui`, `make test-integration` and the journey.
  - **Done when "the journey's backup round trip passes":** `make test-journey` printed `JOURNEY PASSED`, the Transcript round-trip lines and T4's three Ingress lines (see Runs).
  - **Known failures:** none.

- **Deviations and choices, for the orchestrator to record.** No PRD, ADR or glossary file was edited.
  - **Rate limits.** Phase 12 has no route that names an identity. Only the keyed limiter (`Buckets`, never per client address) and the push listener's global bucket of 120 a minute are wired. The per-push-id bucket (60 a minute) comes with Phase 13. The per-agent (600 a minute), per-Enrollment-code (10 an hour) and unknown-agent-or-code (60 a minute in all) buckets come with Phase 15b.
  - **Body caps.** The push route answers `404` before it reads any body, as B01 orders: the push id is checked first, and none exists yet. The 1 MiB Push delivery cap therefore first gets a route test in Phase 13. In Phase 12, `read_body`'s `Content-Length`, chunked and byte-counting paths are proven on the agent listener, which caps every request at 64 KiB.
  - **The push id check.** The push id format check, and the in-memory set of known push ids refreshed every 15 s, are left to Phase 13. With no `push_webhooks` table they would have no observable effect. Neither Ingress service opens a database connection in Phase 12, and the journey's `ingress_check.py` proves their role logins.
  - **Choices B01 leaves open** (recorded in the contract): `CONNECTION LIMIT` 5 and `statement_timeout` 5s per role; the TLS key as a P-256 key in base64 DER PKCS#8; the pin as the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the DER SubjectPublicKeyInfo, which the Go agent of Phases 15a and 15b must use too; a self-signed certificate valid for 1 year, made again at every start; and an agent healthcheck with an unverified TLS context, because the container holds the key, not the pin. Later phases should size their SQLAlchemy pools under the connection limit, for example `pool_size` 2 and `max_overflow` 0.
  - **Release-1 backups.** `restore.sh` now refuses every release-1 backup, because each lacks the four Ingress keys. That is the plan's rule. README.md gives the workaround: copy the keys from the current `.env` into the backup's `deployment.env`.
  - **For Phase 13: `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA`.** Backend tests migrate into non-public schemas (`numen_test_<hex>`), where `PUBLIC` has no `USAGE`. A database restored by a release-1 `restore.sh` also has a `public` schema without `PUBLIC`'s `USAGE`, because it re-created it with `CREATE SCHEMA public AUTHORIZATION numen`. Phase 13's grant migration must therefore `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA` to the roles in every case. `restore.sh` now re-creates `public` as initdb makes it (`AUTHORIZATION pg_database_owner` and `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO PUBLIC`), the ACL `pg_dump` assumes, and the journey's `ingress_check.py` requires each role's refusal to be on the `accounts` table, not on the schema, after the restore too. `pg_dump` writes that grant, so it survives a restore once it exists. Phase 12 itself grants nothing.
  - **The shared test cluster.** The migration creates the real roles `NOLOGIN` in the `asset-monitor-test` cluster, and the journey's `migrate` gives them `LOGIN` with `.env`'s passwords. T2's command test therefore uses throwaway role names and drops them, so `make test-integration` never changes passwords a running journey stack depends on.
  - **The Operator's main project was not restarted.** Its `.env` now holds the five keys (checked above), and its running stack still runs the old image on 127.0.0.1:8788. When the Operator runs `make up`, Compose recreates `db` (new networks), `migrate`, `app` and `worker` (new environment), keeps the volumes, and starts both Ingress services on 127.0.0.1:8791 and 8792.

- **Runs** on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), on the final code, with no request to an outside host. The Operator's `asset-monitor` project was left untouched: `docker ps --filter name=asset-monitor-app-1` gave `dcce11745c74 2026-11-15 10:25:07 +0700 WIB Up 3 hours (healthy)` before the runs and the same line after them, and 127.0.0.1:8788 still answered `200`.
  - `make build` passed. `make check` passed: ruff "All checks passed!", and the frontend lint, typecheck and build.
  - With `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test` and `COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml` exported, so the Operator's database was not used:
    - `make test-unit` passed: 250 passed; runner 145 passed, 69 skipped.
    - `make test-integration` passed: 631 passed. That is the 614 of Phase 11 plus the 17 new tests: 10 in `test_ingress.py`, 3 in `test_postgres.py` and 4 in `test_deployment.py`.
    - `make test-runtime` passed: 213 passed, 1 skipped.
    - `make test-ui` passed: 161 passed in 22 files.
  - `make test-journey` passed. It printed `JOURNEY PASSED`, "PASS a container rebuild keeps the Transcripts" and "PASS a backup, an empty project and a restore keep the Transcripts". It also printed `ingress_check.py`'s five `PASS` lines after the first `up` and again after the restore, and the three new Ingress lines of T4.
  - **The isolated `make up`.** The journey's stack was stopped without `-v`. Then `make up` ran with `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml NUMEN_PORT=18788 NUMEN_DASHBOARD_URL=http://localhost:18788 NUMEN_PUSH_PORT=18791 NUMEN_AGENT_PORT=18792`.
    - `migrate` exited, every service reported Healthy, `ingress-push` and `ingress-agent` included, and `bootstrap-operator` printed "Operator already exists".
    - `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:18788/api/health` gave `{"status":"ok"}`. `./scripts/compose.sh ps` showed `ingress-push` on `127.0.0.1:18791->8083/tcp` and `ingress-agent` on `127.0.0.1:18792->8084/tcp`, both `(healthy)`, next to a healthy app.
    - `POST http://127.0.0.1:18791/push/abc` gave `404` with `{"code":"not_found","message":"Not found","retryable":false}`, the CSP, `nosniff`, `no-referrer` and no `Set-Cookie`. `GET /api/health` on the push port gave `404`.
    - `https://127.0.0.1:18792/health` gave `200 {"status":"ok"}` with the same headers, `/api/health` gave `404`, and plain http got no answer (curl exit 52).
    - The SHA-256 of the served certificate's SPKI (`openssl s_client`, `x509 -pubkey`, `pkey -pubin -outform DER`) equalled `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN` from `.env`.
    - The stack was then stopped without `-v`.
  - `make test-demo` passed on `asset-monitor-demo-test` (127.0.0.1:18790, 18793 and 18794), after the journey and never at the same time. It printed `DEMO SMOKE PASSED` and `DEMO DRY RUN PASSED`, with "PASS docs/demo.md names every demo step", and both Ingress services became Healthy in both phases. Its containers were removed afterwards.

### Phase 12 review fixes (2026-11-15)

- **A restore no longer drops `PUBLIC`'s `USAGE` on `public`.** `restore.sh` re-created `public` with `CREATE SCHEMA public AUTHORIZATION numen`, which gives `{numen=UC/numen}`. `pg_dump` writes the owner and ACL of `public` only where they differ from initdb's `{owner=UC/owner,=U/owner}`, so nothing in `database.sql` put the `USAGE` back. A restored cluster could therefore hold every table `GRANT` while neither ingress role could reach any table, and the journey's grant comparison, which reads only `information_schema.table_privileges`, would still pass. `restore.sh` now runs `CREATE SCHEMA public AUTHORIZATION pg_database_owner; GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO PUBLIC;`, the ACL a fresh cluster has. `ingress_check.py`'s `check_role` now requires `permission denied for table accounts`, so a refusal on the schema fails the journey, before and after the restore.
- **The test cluster was repaired once.** The first journey run after the fix failed at its first `ingress_check.py`, before any restore, with `permission denied for schema public`. The `asset-monitor-test` database volume had last been restored by the release-1 `restore.sh`: its `public` was `numen|{numen=UC/numen}`, while the same cluster's `postgres` database showed `pg_database_owner|{pg_database_owner=UC/pg_database_owner,=U/pg_database_owner}`. `ALTER SCHEMA public OWNER TO pg_database_owner; GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO PUBLIC;` on `asset-monitor-test` only reset it to initdb's state. After the next journey's restore, `public` again read `pg_database_owner|{pg_database_owner=UC/pg_database_owner,=U/pg_database_owner}`. The Operator's main cluster was not touched. If it was ever restored by the release-1 `restore.sh`, it lacks the same `USAGE`, and Phase 13's `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA` to the roles covers that.
- **Runs**, on the final code, with the Operator's `asset-monitor` project untouched (its six containers kept their uptimes):
  - `make build` passed. `make check` passed: ruff "All checks passed!", and the frontend lint, typecheck and build.
  - With `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test` and `COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml` exported: `make test-unit` passed (250 passed; runner 145 passed, 69 skipped). `make test-integration` passed (631 passed). `make test-runtime` passed (213 passed, 1 skipped). `make test-ui` passed (22 files, 161 tests).
  - `make test-journey` passed after the repair above, with every PASS line, both `ingress_check.py` runs, the refusal of a backup without `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY` and the grant round trip into a fresh cluster among them. Its stack was then stopped with `./scripts/compose.sh -p asset-monitor-test -f compose.yaml -f compose.test.yaml down`.

## Phase 13: Push webhooks (2026-11-15)

Phase 13 covers B02 and AC-B02 of `docs/prd/asset-monitor-r2-prd.md`, the A02 change in its release-2 change table, the release-2 defaulted decisions 10, 14, 15, 16 and 17, and ADR-0006. One Alembic migration, `c9d0e1f2a3b4`, chained from `b8c9d0e1f2a3`, adds two tables and keeps existing data. `docs/implementation-contract.md` describes every shape below.

- **T1, the push tables, migration `c9d0e1f2a3b4` and the `asset_monitor_push` grants.**
  - `PushWebhook` (`push_webhooks`: repository, push id, encrypted secret, `last_accepted`, `last_refusal`, unique on repository and on push id) and `PushDelivery` (`push_deliveries`: Push webhook, delivery id, body SHA-256, received at, processed at, unique on delivery id and on body SHA-256). The migration creates both with the models' constraints, so `alembic check` finds no drift.
  - `asset_monitor_push` gets exactly `USAGE` on the current schema (through `format('%I', current_schema())` in a `DO` block), `SELECT` on `push_webhooks`, `UPDATE (last_accepted, last_refusal)` on `push_webhooks` and `INSERT` on `push_deliveries`. `asset_monitor_agent` gets nothing. The downgrade drops both tables, and their grants with them, revokes that `USAGE` and drops no role.
- **T2, Set up, Regenerate and Turn off, redaction, and the Push webhook panel.**
  - `POST .../repositories/{repository_id}/push-webhook` (Set up, `201 {path, secret}`), `POST .../push-webhook/secret` (Regenerate, `200 {path, secret}`, the push id kept) and `DELETE .../push-webhook` (Turn off, which deletes the deliveries and then the row). All three are on the session router, so they need the session and `X-CSRF-Token`, and no password. They refuse `push_webhook_own_only` (422) unless the link is the active Own link on GitHub, `push_webhook_exists` (409) on a second Set up, and `not_found` without a Push webhook. Each records an Action without the push id or the secret.
  - The secret is `token_hex(32)`, stored as `Vault(NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY).encrypt(secret)` and shown once. `domain.link_json` adds `push_webhook` (`null` or `{created_at, last_accepted, last_refusal}`) to Own links only.
  - `workflows.all_secrets` adds each Push webhook's decrypted secret and `NUMEN_INGRESS_KEY` itself.
  - The Repositories tab shows a Push webhook panel after the Own repository: Set up, the one-time setup dialog (Payload URL, content type, secret, "Just the push event"), Regenerate and Turn off with confirmations, the last accepted delivery and the last refusal, all as React text.
- **T3, the push route in `numen.ingress`.** `POST /push/<push id>`: the global bucket; then format and the in-memory known push id set, refreshed every 15 s, before the body is read (an unknown id gets `404` with no query); the per-push-id bucket of 60 a minute; the `push` or `ping` event; the delivery id; the 1 MiB cap on the bytes read; then, in one transaction, the row read again, `X-Hub-Signature-256` over the raw bytes with `hmac.compare_digest`, and a savepointed insert with no `RETURNING`, so a repeated delivery id or body answers `200 {"status": "duplicate"}` and changes nothing. A refusal reason is recorded at most once a minute per push id. The body is never parsed.
- **T4, the nudge, the Repository row lock and the links tie-break.**
  - `domain.nudge_push_deliveries`, run by `scheduler.main` in its own transaction after `schedule` in each 15 s loop, prunes deliveries older than 7 days, then applies the B02 rule: the enabled Own repository of an Application that is not archived, whether or not monitoring is on, under A02's read token rule; a queued source check is reused, a running one makes the deliveries wait, and `next_check_at` and `last_check_at` are never touched.
  - `domain.active_source_check` takes the Repository row lock (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`) before it looks, and `domain.schedule`, `api.check_application` and the nudge all queue through it. `schedule()` now locks its due Applications `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, and `check_application` and the nudge lock their Application before the Repository.
  - `domain.links` orders by role, `created_at`, then link id, and `linked_applications` by `created_at`, then id. That fixes the ordering flake of `test_check_now_queues_one_source_check_per_linked_repository`, which now pins equal `created_at` and expects the order by link id.
- **T5, the GitHub push stand-in, the `stand-ins` network, the journey and the documents.**
  - **`tests/integration/fixture_server.py`.** `numen/push-app-<hex>` has its own commits in `FixtureState.push_repos`, newest first. Its first read creates commit 0, and only `POST /admin/push` adds one, so `/admin/advance` never moves it. Commit `n` has the sha `sha1("<repository>:<n>")` and changes `VERSION` to `1.0.<n>`. It serves the commit list (with an `ETag` over the body and `304` on `If-None-Match`), each commit with its files, and compare (`ahead`, `total_commits`, chronological commits). Contents and every other route answer `404`, so derivation finds no manifest. `POST /admin/hooks {repository, url, secret}` records a hook and sends it a signed `ping`. `POST /admin/push {repository}` adds one commit to that repository only, then sends each of its hooks a push delivery (`X-GitHub-Event: push`, a uuid4 `X-GitHub-Delivery`, `X-Hub-Signature-256` over the body, `application/json`) whose GitHub-shaped payload lists only `'f' * 40`, which no route serves. `POST /admin/hooks/redeliver`, `/admin/hooks/replay` and `/admin/hooks/forge` resend a delivery as it was, resend its body and signature under a new delivery id, and send a push signed with the wrong secret. Each answers the listener's status. `/admin/state` lists hooks without secrets and deliveries without bodies or signatures, and `/admin/reset` clears them.
  - **`compose.test.yaml`.** `fixtures` joins `stand-ins`, `ingress-push` gets `networks: [stand-ins]`, and the top-level `stand-ins` is `internal: true` with no fixed name. `./scripts/compose.sh -p asset-monitor-test -f compose.yaml -f compose.test.yaml config` merges `ingress-push` to `push-db`, `push-inbound` and `stand-ins`, leaves `ingress-agent` on `agent-db` and `agent-inbound`, and names the network `asset-monitor-test_stand-ins` (`internal: true`). `compose.yaml` is unchanged.
  - **`backend/tests/test_deployment.py`.** New `test_the_stand_ins_network_is_only_in_overlays_and_internal`: no `stand-ins` network or member in `compose.yaml`; where an overlay defines it, it is exactly `{internal: true}`; across every overlay only `fixtures` and `ingress-push` of `compose.test.yaml` join it. `test_each_ingress_service_is_on_its_own_two_networks` now allows exactly `networks: [stand-ins]` on `ingress-push` in `compose.test.yaml`, and nothing else in any overlay. Both failed first (2 failed, 6 passed) and pass after the overlay change.
  - **`tests/integration/journey.py`**, a Push webhook case before the archive step. `Push fixture <run>` from `common` (monitoring off, Triage disabled) links the Own repository `https://github.com/numen/push-app-<run>`, and one Check now sets its Baseline (`imported` 0). Set up answers `201` with exactly `path` and `secret`. `POST /admin/hooks` registers `http://ingress-push:8083<path>`, and the ping is redelivered once a second until it gets `200`, within 30 s; the link's `push_webhook.last_accepted.event` is then `ping`. One `POST /admin/push` gets `200`, and with no Check now exactly one new `source_check` appears within 60 s and succeeds with `imported` 1; the commit list holds the pushed sha and not `'f' * 40`. A redelivery and a replay get `200` and a forgery `401`. After 20 s, more than one scheduler loop, there is no other `source_check`, `last_accepted` is still the push's event and delivery id, and `last_refusal.reason` is `wrong_signature`. After Turn off, `push_webhook` is `null` and the next `POST /admin/push` gets `404`. The Application is archived with the others. New lines: "PASS a Push webhook set up on the Own repository accepts its signed ping within the 15 s refresh, and the repository shows it", "PASS a Push delivery imports the pushed commit through the GitHub API with no Check now, and never a commit listed only in its payload", "PASS a redelivery and a replayed body queue no second check, and a forged delivery is refused and the repository shows why" and "PASS after Turn off the old push id is refused at once".
  - **`tests/integration/ingress_check.py`.** `check_role` takes the tables the role must read and those it must be refused. `push` now reads `public.push_webhooks` and is refused `SELECT` on `public.push_deliveries` and `public.accounts`, each with `InsufficientPrivilege` and `permission denied for table <name>`. It runs before the journey and again after the restore.
  - **`scripts/test-journey.sh`.** `numen_ingress_grants` also aggregates each role's column `UPDATE`s from `information_schema.column_privileges`, which `table_privileges` does not list. Before the backup the push row must end in `push_deliveries:INSERT,push_webhooks:SELECT|push_webhooks.last_accepted:UPDATE,push_webhooks.last_refusal:UPDATE`, and after the restore into a fresh volume the whole output must be the same.
  - **README.md**: the "For now the push listener answers 404" sentence is replaced; a new "Push webhooks" section: Set up on the Repositories tab, registering the webhook in GitHub by hand (Settings, Webhooks, Add webhook: the Public tunnel's URL plus `/push/<push id>`, `application/json`, the secret, Just the push event), a Public tunnel pointed at 127.0.0.1:8791 only and never at 8788 or 8792, redelivering the first ping once the push id is accepted, what is checked and deduplicated, the refusal reasons shown, Regenerate and Turn off, and scheduled polling continuing. The journey paragraph covers the push case and the push role's grants.
  - **`docs/implementation-contract.md`**: the `stand-ins` network under Ingress > Networks, the grant round trip under Ingress > Backup and restore, and a "Push webhooks (B02; Phase 13)" bullet in Verified implementation adjustments with the push stand-in, its admin routes and the journey case.

- **Phase 13 acceptance, each mapped to its tests.**
  - **AC-B02:**
    - *A valid Push delivery imports the new commit through the GitHub API with no Check now, and creates the same jobs as a source check:* `test_push_webhooks.py::test_a_push_delivery_imports_the_new_commit_through_the_github_api_without_check_now`, and the journey's push case (one nudged `source_check`, `imported` 1).
    - *A wrong signature or another event changes nothing and the repository shows why; an unknown push id gets `404` and is recorded nowhere:* `test_ingress.py::test_a_wrong_signature_or_event_changes_nothing_and_shows_why` and `test_an_unknown_push_id_gets_404_and_no_query`; the journey's forged delivery (`401`, `last_refusal.reason` `wrong_signature`); `ApplicationRepositories.integration.test.tsx` "shows the last accepted delivery and the last refusal as text".
    - *The same delivery id twice, or the same body under a new delivery id, queues one check:* `test_a_valid_push_is_recorded_once_per_delivery_id_and_once_per_body`, and the journey's redelivery and replay (no second `source_check` after 20 s).
    - *Commits listed in a payload are never imported from it:* `test_the_body_is_never_parsed`, and the journey's commit list without `'f' * 40`.
    - *Ten deliveries while a check is queued add none; one while it runs is followed by exactly one more:* `test_push_webhooks.py::test_ten_deliveries_while_a_check_is_queued_add_none_and_one_while_running_adds_exactly_one`, with `test_postgres.py::test_postgres_source_checks_are_queued_once_under_the_repository_lock` for the lock across the scheduler, Check now and the nudge.
    - *With a delivery every minute, the scheduled check, Advisory check and version check stay on time:* `test_a_nudge_never_moves_next_check_at_and_scheduled_checks_stay_on_time`.
    - *After Turn off or Regenerate the old push id or secret is refused at once:* `test_ingress.py::test_turn_off_and_regenerate_are_refused_at_once`, and the journey's `404` straight after Turn off.
    - *No Push webhook on an Upstream repository:* `test_a_push_webhook_cannot_be_set_up_on_an_upstream_repository`.
  - **The plan's bullets:**
    - *Tables and grants:* `test_postgres_migration_adds_push_webhook_tables`, `test_postgres_ingress_roles_hold_no_grant_on_release_1_tables` (now the exact table and column privilege sets of both roles), `test_postgres_push_role_cannot_change_a_secret_or_read_deliveries`, `test_postgres_push_listener_works_on_the_push_role_grants` and `test_postgres_ingress_roles_survive_a_downgrade_beside_another_schema`. Pruning: `test_push_deliveries_older_than_seven_days_are_pruned`.
    - *Setup API:* `test_a_push_webhook_is_set_up_on_an_own_repository_and_shown_once` and `test_push_webhook_routes_need_the_session_and_csrf_but_no_password`.
    - *The push route:* the 1 MiB cap `test_a_push_body_is_capped_at_1_mib_on_the_bytes_read`, the refresh `test_a_new_push_id_is_accepted_after_the_next_refresh` and `test_a_failed_refresh_keeps_the_listener_and_logs_only_the_error_class`, the per-push-id bucket `test_each_push_id_has_its_own_bucket`, the refusal rate `test_refusals_are_recorded_at_most_once_a_minute_per_push_id`, plus Phase 12's cookie, header, import and `main` tests on the new signature.
    - *The nudge rule:* `test_the_nudge_applies_only_to_an_enabled_own_repository_of_an_application_in_use`.
    - *Redaction:* `test_all_secrets_holds_each_push_webhook_secret`.
    - *UI:* `ApplicationRepositories.integration.test.tsx` "sets up the Push webhook and shows its secret once", "regenerates and turns off the Push webhook" and "shows the last accepted delivery and the last refusal as text".
    - *The GitHub stand-in and the `stand-ins` network:* the journey's push case, and `test_the_stand_ins_network_is_only_in_overlays_and_internal` with `test_each_ingress_service_is_on_its_own_two_networks`.
    - *The grants across the backup round trip (Phase 12 note, AC-B01):* `numen_ingress_grants`, now with column `UPDATE`s, required before the backup and equal after the restore, and `ingress_check.py push` before and after `restore.sh`.
  - **Done when "AC-B02 has tests, including the journey's push imported with no Check now":** the mapping above, and the journey's push lines under Runs.
  - **Known failures:** the ordering flake of `test_check_now_queues_one_source_check_per_linked_repository` is fixed by T4's tie-break. None remain.

- **Deviations and choices, for the orchestrator to record.** No PRD, ADR or glossary file was edited.
  - **Refusal reasons.** Besides B02's three examples (`wrong_signature`, `event_not_accepted`, `body_too_large`), a refusal also records `delivery_id_missing` and `rate_limited` (the per-push-id bucket). The global bucket's `429` comes before the push id is looked up, so it records nothing.
  - **Shown once.** After Set up, neither the push id nor the secret is shown again. Regenerate keeps the push id and shows the path again with the new secret.
  - **A removed Own link.** Removing an Own link leaves its Push webhook row. The nudge ignores its deliveries (marks them processed) until the repository is again the enabled Own repository of an Application in use.
  - **`push_deliveries` grant.** The push role has the plan's table-level `INSERT` on `push_deliveries`, so a compromised `ingress-push` could set `received_at` or `processed_at`. That costs it only its own nudges or its own dedupe window.
  - **Locks beyond the plan.** With the Repository lock in `active_source_check`, the old `schedule()` `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` deadlocked against a worker's `source_check` (psycopg `DeadlockDetected`, reproduced in T4). `schedule()` now takes `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, and `check_application` and the nudge lock their Application before the Repository.
  - **Grants on restore.** The brief said the grants must be re-applied by migrations on restore. In this repository `pg_dump` writes them into `database.sql` and `restore.sh` creates the roles before loading it, so no migration re-runs at head; the journey's comparison, now with column privileges, and `ingress_check.py push` prove they come back.
  - **The stand-in in demo mode.** The push stand-in's routes are not gated on test mode, so the demo fixture serves them too. They are unused there: the demo overlays add no `stand-ins` network, so the demo's `ingress-push` is unreachable from the fixture.
  - **The stand-in's commit list** ignores `sha`, `per_page` and `page` and always lists every commit: the journey never backfills the push stand-in.
  - **Timing.** The journey proves "no new check" by waiting 20 s after the redelivery, replay and forgery, which, with the ping's wait for the 15 s refresh and the nudge's scheduler loop, adds about 40 to 60 s to the journey.

- **Runs** on Docker Desktop (Engine 20.10, 8 GB), on the final code, with no request to an outside host. The Operator's `asset-monitor` project was left untouched: `docker ps --filter name=asset-monitor-app-1` gave `dcce11745c74 2026-11-15 10:25:07 +0700 WIB` before the runs and after them, its six containers kept their creation times, and 127.0.0.1:8788 still answered `200`.
  - `./scripts/compose.sh -p asset-monitor-test -f compose.yaml -f compose.test.yaml config` merged `ingress-push`'s networks to `push-db`, `push-inbound` and `stand-ins`, `fixtures`' to `application`, `served` and `stand-ins`, and left `ingress-agent` on `agent-db` and `agent-inbound`.
  - A throwaway script in a `fixtures` container (not kept) drove the stand-in against a local HMAC-checking receiver: the base commit, the `ETag` and `304`, contents `404`, the signed ping, one push adding one commit to that repository only, compare and commit detail, `'f' * 40` answering `404`, redelivery with the same id and body, replay under a new id with the same body, a forgery the receiver refused, no delivery for a repository without hooks, `/admin/advance` leaving the commits alone, `/admin/state` without secrets, bodies or signatures, and `/admin/reset`.
  - `make test-journey` passed, its `make build` included. It printed `JOURNEY PASSED` with the four new Push webhook lines, "PASS asset_monitor_push logs in through its own network, reads push_webhooks and cannot read push_deliveries or accounts" before the journey and again after the restore, "PASS the restore brings back the ingress roles, their limits and their grants" (with the column `UPDATE`s required before the backup), and the Transcript round-trip lines: 38 `PASS` lines in all. After it, `information_schema.column_privileges` on the restored database still listed `push_webhooks.last_accepted:UPDATE,push_webhooks.last_refusal:UPDATE` for `asset_monitor_push` and nothing for `asset_monitor_agent`. Its stack was then stopped with `./scripts/compose.sh -p asset-monitor-test -f compose.yaml -f compose.test.yaml down`.
  - With `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=asset-monitor-test` and `COMPOSE_FILE=compose.yaml:compose.test.yaml` exported, so the Operator's database was not used:
    - `make check` passed: ruff "All checks passed!", and the frontend lint, typecheck and build.
    - `make test-unit` passed: 250 passed; runner 145 passed, 69 skipped.
    - `make test-integration` passed: 656 passed. That is Phase 12's 631 plus Phase 13's 25: 9 in `test_push_webhooks.py`, 9 in `test_ingress.py`, 6 in `test_postgres.py` (three tests, and the Repository lock test run for `check_application`, `schedule` and the nudge) and 1 in `test_deployment.py`.
    - `make test-runtime` passed: 213 passed, 1 skipped.
    - `make test-ui`: right after the runtime suite it had 3 timeouts in 164 (the Push webhook "regenerates and turns off", a Served-content and a native login test, each a 5 s timeout or a missing region under load). `npm test -- --maxWorkers=2` then passed 164 of 164 in 22 files, and `make test-ui` run again alone passed 164 of 164.
  - `make test-demo` was not run: Phase 13 adds nothing to the demo overlays, and the push stand-in's additions to `fixture_server.py` leave its demo routes and `check()` unchanged.

## Phase 15b-19a MVP (deadline cut) (2026-11-15)

The deadline cut of phases 15b to 19a of `docs/plans/20261115-feature-asset-monitor-r2.md`, against B01, B04 to B08 and B10 of `docs/prd/asset-monitor-r2-prd.md`, ADR-0006 and ADR-0007, matching `agent/PROTOCOL.md` byte for byte. One Alembic migration so far, `d1e2f3a4b5c6`, chained from `c9d0e1f2a3b4`.

- **T1, Enrollment, polling and signed messages.**
  - **`backend/numen/agent_wire.py`**, pure (standard library and `cryptography`): the request signing string, the target, `verify`, `fingerprint`, `envelope` and `valid_path`, shared by the agent listener and application code.
  - **Migration `d1e2f3a4b5c6`**: `agents`, `enrollment_codes`, `agent_requests`, `agent_messages` and `dashboard_keys`, and `asset_monitor_agent`'s column grants: `SELECT (id, public_key, status, wanted)` on `agents`, `SELECT (id, agent_id, expires_at, used_at)` on `enrollment_codes`, `SELECT (agent_id, type, sequence, key_id, payload, signature, status, expires_at)` on `agent_messages`, `INSERT (id, agent_id, route, method, target, timestamp, nonce, content_sha256, signature, body)` on `agent_requests`, and schema `USAGE`. Nothing on `dashboard_keys`.
  - **The agent listener** (`ingress.agent_app(factory)`): `GET /health` and the five `/agent/v1` routes, the header, agent, body cap, hash, signature, clock and route checks, the per-agent, per-code and unknown buckets, stored with no `RETURNING`, and `{"error": code}` refusals. `main()` gives it its own pool.
  - **`backend/numen/servers.py`**: the Dashboard key (encrypted under `NUMEN_SIGNING_KEY`), `sign_message`, `sign_configuration`, `create_agent`, `agent_json`, and `process_agent_requests`, which `scheduler.main` runs every loop.
  - **API** (`api.py`): `GET/POST /applications/{id}/agents`, `POST /agents/{id}/confirmations`, `/revocations` and `/rescans`, each recording an Action, none an Approval.
  - **UI**: the Application's **Server files** tab (`ApplicationServerFiles.tsx`): Add agent with the URL prefilled, the enroll command and Enrollment code in a dialog shown once, and the agents table (status, fingerprint, Allowed paths, version and architecture, last seen, last refusal, signed messages) with Confirm fingerprint, Check now and Revoke. Everything an agent sends is text.
  - **Ops**: `NUMEN_SIGNING_KEY`, `NUMEN_AGENT_TLS_PIN` and `NUMEN_AGENT_URL` on `app` only; `make init` appends `NUMEN_SIGNING_KEY` (Fernet) to an existing `.env` and changes no line; `restore.sh` requires it.
  - **Stand-in server**: the Dockerfile's `agent-build` and `server-standin` stages, the `server-standin` service in `compose.test.yaml`, `tests/integration/server_standin.py`, the fixture's `POST /admin/server/enroll` and `/admin/state` `server.enroll_results`, `ingress-agent` on `stand-ins`, and `scripts/test-journey.sh` building and starting it. `ingress_check.py agent` now reads `agent_messages` and is refused `agent_requests` and `accounts`.

- **T1 acceptance, each mapped to its tests.**
  - *Every PROTOCOL.md test vector through `numen.agent_wire`:* `test_agent_wire.py` (keys, fingerprint, both request signatures, the configuration envelope and the command signature, and `valid_path`).
  - *Migration chain, no drift, no role dropped, exact grants:* `test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_on_server_agent_tables` and `test_postgres_ingress_roles_hold_no_grant_on_release_1_tables` (the agent role's exact column set), and `test_postgres_ingress_roles_survive_a_downgrade_beside_another_schema`.
  - *The listener answers only `/health` and the five routes, verifies hash, signature, timestamp and nonce, stores with no `RETURNING`, and imports no dashboard module:* `test_ingress.py::test_neither_listener_serves_the_dashboard`, `test_importing_the_ingress_never_imports_the_dashboard`, `test_server_agents.py::test_the_listener_refuses_a_bad_signature_a_skewed_clock_and_a_reused_nonce`, and `test_postgres_agent_listener_works_on_the_agent_role_grants` (as `asset_monitor_agent`, with every write and read beyond its grants refused).

  - *Confirm fingerprint signs a configuration (Watched roots = Allowed paths, 2 s poll only under test mode) with the Dashboard key held only by the app; `make init` appends the key:* `test_confirmation_signs_a_configuration_and_commands_the_agent_can_verify`, `test_the_enroll_command_and_code_are_shown_once` (the private half decrypts only with `NUMEN_SIGNING_KEY`), `test_deployment.py::test_each_ingress_service_holds_only_its_own_keys` and `test_the_enroll_command_names_the_agent_listener_as_the_server_sees_it`; `make init` on the journey's `.env` printed "Added to .env: NUMEN_SIGNING_KEY. Existing lines were preserved." and the first 12 lines were byte-identical.

- **T1 deviations and choices, for the orchestrator to record.** No PRD, ADR or glossary file was edited.
  - **One table for Enrollments and agent requests.** The plan names "Enrollment requests" and "agent requests"; both are rows of `agent_requests` (`route = 'enroll'`), so the agent role inserts into one table.
  - **The wanted list is `agents.wanted`**, not a table of its own; the agent role reads only that column.
  - **The listener refuses unknown, used and expired code ids itself**, from `enrollment_codes (id, agent_id, expires_at, used_at)`, so it can bucket per code and keep junk out of `agent_requests`. It never sees the secret's hash: application code alone checks the secret, spends the code and binds the key (B01: "the ingress never checks an Enrollment code" holds for the secret).
  - **Re-verification runs in the scheduler**, in `process_agent_requests`, not as a worker job, so within 15 s, with no job row. The scheduler holds no `NUMEN_SIGNING_KEY`; only the app signs.
  - **Nonces** are unique per agent by `UNIQUE (agent_id, nonce)` for as long as a request is kept (an hour after it is processed, past the 300 s window), so application code does not query for them again.
  - **Agent listener error bodies** are `{"error": code}` on every answer, `/health`'s 404 and 413 included, as PROTOCOL.md says; the push listener keeps `{code, message, retryable}`.
  - **The enroll command** is one line, with `--allow /var/www/html` as the example the Operator edits.
  - **A refusal the agent sends for a message it could not verify** has no matching message, so it is recorded on the agent row as `agent_refused: <reason>`.
  - **The stand-in removes `state.json` after a successful Enrollment**, because a re-enrolled server keeps its sequences and would silently ignore a new agent id's first configuration (`not_newer` at the same sequence). The agent should reset them itself; that is noted, not changed (no Go changes in T1).
  - **Report handling in T1** only checks that the report sequence increases; manifests, Baselines and Server changes are T2.

- **Deferred past the deadline (from T1's part of phases 15b to 19a):**
  - Rotate Dashboard key, `repin` from the dashboard, and the "pinned to old key" status (`sign_message` refuses an agent pinned to another key id with `agent_pinned_to_old_key`, which cannot happen until rotation exists).
  - The silent-agent status (no verified poll for 5 minutes) and its display.
  - The agent binaries' SHA-256 checksums in the dashboard.
  - The Watched roots editor through `approval()`, and re-signing the configuration when the Application's interval or monitoring changes.
  - Showing a second Enrollment with a used code on the agent's row: the listener refuses it with `401 enrollment_refused` (the agent prints it), but the agent role cannot write the row, and the request is never stored.
  - The stand-in's check for no `LISTEN` entries in `/proc/net/*` and that the dashboard is unreachable from it.

No ingress role has a grant on them. The downgrade raises `RuntimeError` naming the Findings while any has the `server_files` source.

- **T2 acceptance, each mapped to its tests.**
  - *Migration chained after `d1e2f3a4b5c6` with no drift; downgrade refused while a `server_files` Finding exists; no ingress grant on the new tables:* `test_postgres.py::test_postgres_migration_adds_file_manifests_and_server_changes`, and `test_postgres_ingress_roles_hold_no_grant_on_release_1_tables`, which now names `file_manifests` and `server_changes` among the tables it checks.

  - *The journey:* the Baseline is accepted after a stale SHA-256 is refused, and the injected inert PHP file is exactly one Server change with its bytes held after Check now; a second rescan adds none and nothing requested the file.

- **T2 deviations and choices, for the orchestrator to record.** No PRD, ADR or glossary file was edited.
  - **The scheduler, not the worker, hashes uploads again and holds them** (B05 says "the worker"), inside `process_agent_requests`, so there is no `server_check` job: re-verification, holding bytes and diffing all run there, within the scheduler's loop.
  - **Server change identity ignores mtime** except for a file over the hash limit, where size and mtime stand in for the SHA-256; a link's target is part of it.
  - **An incomplete change's Unrelated Verdict is stored as Insufficient context** (so it lands in the Review queue), as an incomplete Triage's `unrelated` becomes `insufficient_context`.
  - **The Baseline request names the manifest**, `{manifest_id, manifest_sha256}`, not a Server change: the change it stands for is found by the manifest's diff against the current Baseline, so a later report of the same change can be accepted too.
  - **An older manifest still awaiting uploads is completed when a newer report arrives**, since the agent uploads only for its latest report; its missing bytes make its change incomplete.
  - **The report refusal for a non-increasing sequence keeps T1's name**, `report_sequence_not_increasing`, not `not_newer`; a report now also needs an integer `config_sequence` (`bad_request`), so T1's test reports carry one.
  - **A server_files Finding's Playbook is `("quarantine",)` for now**: the static Proof, Detection rule and cleanup report steps of B07 are deferred, and the agent-drafted indicators are shown as drafted (`server_indicators`).
  - **Analysis stages no `source/previous/`**: only the changed bytes and the Baseline version.

- **Deferred past the deadline (from T2's part of phases 15b to 19a):**
  - Held-bytes budgets (128 MiB per Baseline, 64 MiB per change beyond the one 64 MiB wanted-list cap), reference tracking and releasing bytes nothing references.
  - The Pre-filter flags for Server changes (web-executable and secret-list files, suspicious secret names) and the rule that a flagged change cannot become the Baseline after a Triage decline alone.
  - The Detection rule validation (`detection_rule`'s `server_files` branch), the static Proof and the checked cleanup report for server files: the agent-drafted indicators are shown only.
  - The Upstream sweep of server files.
  - Linking later manifest entries to a Finding by SHA-256, remediation and regression (T3), and Findings opened by hand with `source_server_change_id`.
  - A Triage of more than 300 entries of a kind lists the first 300 only and is incomplete; Analysis lists every entry.
